\|[\beta_{k},\mathcal{D}_{\ell}]\Psi\|_{\mathscr{F}}\lesssim R^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{S}(2)}^{\frac{1}{2}}\Psi\|_{\mathscr{F}}\ .

The same argument can be repeated for the next commutator with
\mathcal{D}_{q}
,
provided one enlarges the Fermi surface
from
\mathcal{S}(2)
to
\mathcal{S}(3)
.
In other words, it holds that

(4.42)

\|[[\beta_{k},\mathcal{D}_{\ell}],\mathcal{D}_{q}]\Psi\|_{\mathscr{F}}\lesssim R^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{S}(3)}^{\frac{1}{2}}\Psi\|_{\mathscr{F}}\ .

The above motivation contains the main ideas for the proof of the lemma.
One merely has to include additional bounded coefficients
in the definition of
\beta_{k}
and
\mathcal{D}_{\ell}

to account for the dependence on
t\in\mathbb{R}
and
k\in\Lambda^{*}
,
that comes from
b_{k}(t)
and
D_{\ell}(s)
.
We leave the details to the reader.
∎

Sketch of Proof of Lemma
4.7
.

Let us fix
m>0
,
k,\ell,q\in\textnormal{supp}\hat{V},\,

t,s,r\in\mathbb{R}
and
\varphi\in\ell^{1}_{m}(\Lambda^{*})
.
Starting from Eq. (
4.13
) we easily estimate that

(4.43)

\displaystyle\|b_{k}(t,\varphi)\|_{B(\mathscr{F})}\leqslant 2|\Lambda|\int_{\Lambda^{*}}\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{S}}(p)|\varphi(p)|\mathrm{d}p\ .

It suffices then to note that

\int_{\mathcal{S}}|\varphi(p)|\mathrm{d}p\lesssim p_{F}^{-m}\|\varphi\|_{\ell_{m}^{1}}
.
For the next estimate, the same analysis can be carried out,
starting from the commutator identity found in Eq. (
4.14
).
For the last estimate, one has to calculate the upcoming
commutators and bound each term in the same way.
∎

Sketch of Proof of Lemma
4.8
.

Let us fix
k\in\Lambda^{*}
and
\varphi\in\ell^{1}
.
Starting from Eq. (
4.2
)
we use
0\leqslant\chi,\chi^{\perp}\leqslant 1

and

\|a_{p}(t)\|_{B(\mathscr{F})}=\|a_{p}^{*}(t)\|_{B(\mathscr{F})}\leqslant|\Lambda|^{\frac{1}{2}}

to find

(4.44)

\displaystyle\|D_{k}(t,\varphi)\|_{B(\mathscr{F})}\leqslant 4|\Lambda|\int_{\Lambda^{*}}|\varphi(p)|\mathrm{d}p\ .

A similar inequality can be found upon calculation
of the commutator
[D_{k}(t),D_{\ell}(s,\varphi)].
This finishes the proof.
∎

5.
Tool Box II: Excitation Estimates

In Section
3

we introduced the two following observables:

(5.1)

\mathcal{N}=\int_{\Lambda^{*}}a_{p}^{*}a_{p}\mathrm{d}p\qquad\textnormal{ and }\qquad\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{S}}=\int_{\mathcal{S}}a_{p}^{*}a_{p}\mathrm{d}p

corresponding to the the Number Operator and Surface-Localized Number Operator, respectively.
The main purpose of this section is to prove
estimates
that control
the growth-in-time of the expectation of
\mathcal{N}
and
\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{S}}

with respect to the interaction dynamics
(\nu_{t})_{t\in\mathbb{R}}
, defined in (
3.14
).
These estimates are precisely stated in the following two propositions, which we prove in the reminder of this section.

Proposition 5.1
.

Let
(\nu_{t})_{t\in\mathbb{R}}

solve the interaction dynamics defined in (
3.14
), with initial data
\nu_{0}\equiv\nu

satisfying Condition
2
.
Assume that
n=\nu(\mathcal{N})\geqslant 1
.
Then,
for all
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
there exists a constant
C>0
such that

(5.2)

\nu_{t}(\mathcal{N}^{\ell})\leqslant Cn^{\ell}\exp(C\lambda Rt)\ ,\qquad\forall t\geqslant 0\ .

Proposition 5.2
.

Let
(\nu_{t})_{t\in\mathbb{R}}

solve the interaction dynamics defined in (
3.14
), with initial data
\nu_{0}\equiv\nu

satisfying Condition
2
.
Further, assume that
n=\nu(\mathcal{N})\lesssim R^{1/2}
.
Then, there exists a constant
C>0
such that

(5.3)

\nu_{t}(\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{S}})\leqslant C(\lambda R\left\langle t\right\rangle)^{2}\exp(C\lambda Rt)\ ,\qquad\forall t\geqslant 0\ ,

where
\left\langle t\right\rangle=(1+t^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}.

The idea behind the proof of our estimates
relies on a
standard Grönwall argument, in which we
bound expectations of
commutators
[\mathcal{N},\mathfrak{h}_{I}(t)]
and
[\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{S}},\mathfrak{h}_{I}(t)]

in terms of combinations of expectations of

\mathcal{N}
and
\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{S}}
.
This proof relies heavily in the fact that
the interaction Hamiltonian decomposes into three parts, corresponding to fermion-fermion, fermion-boson and boson-boson interactions.
Namely, there holds

(5.4)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{h}_{I}(t)\ =\ \lambda\,\big(V_{F}(t)+V_{F,B}(t)+V_{B}(t)\big)\ ,\qquad\forall t\geqslant 0\ .

Here, time-dependence corresponds to the Heisenberg evolution associated to the solvable Hamiltonian
\mathfrak{h}_{0}
–see Eq. (
3.19
).
In particular, using the formulae (
3.8
), (
3.9
) and (
3.10
) for
V_{F}
,
V_{F,B}
and
V_{B}
, respectively, we may write that
for all
t\in\mathbb{R}

(5.5)

\displaystyle V_{F}(t)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Lambda^{*}}\hat{V}(k)D_{k}^{*}(t)D_{k}(t)\ \mathrm{d}k

(5.6)

\displaystyle V_{FB}(t)

\displaystyle=\int_{\Lambda^{*}}\hat{V}(k)D_{k}^{*}(t)\big[b_{k}(t)+b_{-k}^{*}(t)\big]\mathrm{d}k

(5.7)

\displaystyle V_{B}(t)

\displaystyle=\int_{\Lambda^{*}}\hat{V}(k)\big[b_{k}^{*}(t)b_{k}(t)+\frac{1}{2}\,b^{*}_{k}(t)b_{-k}(t)+\frac{1}{2}\,b_{-k}(t)b_{k}(t)\big]\mathrm{d}k

where
b_{k}(t)
and
D_{k}(t)
correspond to the Heisenberg evolution of the
b
- and
D
-operators, respectively, as given in Definition
7
.

5.1.
Number Operator Estimates

The main purpose of this section is to prove the Proposition
5.1
.
The first step in this direction
is to prove appropiate commutator estimates between
\mathcal{N}
and the generator of the interaction dynamics,
\mathfrak{h}_{I}(t)
.
The commutator estimates that we prove are contained in the upcoming Lemma.
We recall that
R=|\Lambda|p_{F}^{d-1}
.

Lemma 5.1

(Commutator Estimates for
\mathcal{N}
)
.

For all
\ell\geqslant 1
there exists a constant

C=C(\ell)>0

such that:

(1)

For all
\Psi\in\mathscr{F}
and
t\geqslant 0
there holds

\displaystyle\left\langle\Psi,[\mathcal{N}^{\ell},V_{F}(t)]\Psi\right\rangle_{\mathscr{F}}=0

(2)

For all
\Psi\in\mathscr{F}
and
t\geqslant 0
there holds

\displaystyle|\left\langle\Psi,[\mathcal{N}^{\ell},V_{F,B}(t)]\Psi\right\rangle_{\mathscr{F}}|\leqslant CR\left\langle\Psi,(\mathcal{N}^{\ell}+\mathds{1})\Psi\right\rangle_{\mathscr{F}}

(3)

For all
\Psi\in\mathscr{F}
and
t\geqslant 0
there holds

|\left\langle\Psi,[\mathcal{N}^{\ell},V_{B}(t)]\Psi\right\rangle_{\mathscr{F}}|\leqslant CR\left\langle\Psi,(\mathcal{N}^{\ell}+\mathds{1})\Psi\right\rangle_{\mathscr{F}}

Remark 5.1
.

Let us recall that we assume that
\nu:B(\mathscr{F})\rightarrow\mathbb{C}

is a mixed state.
Namely,
there exists
sequences

(\lambda_{n})_{n=0}^{\infty}\subset(0,\infty)

and
(\Psi_{n})_{n=0}^{\infty}\subset\mathscr{F}

satisfying the normalization condition
\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\lambda_{n}=1

and
\|\Psi_{n}\|_{\mathscr{F}}=1
, respectively, such that
the following decomposition holds true

(5.8)

\displaystyle\nu(\mathcal{O})=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\lambda_{n}\left\langle\Psi_{n},\mathcal{O}\Psi_{n}\right\rangle_{\mathscr{F}}\ ,\qquad\forall\mathcal{O}\in B(\mathscr{F})\ .

In particular, the estimates contained in Lemma
5.1

can be easily converted into estimates for mixed states.
For instance, if
\mathcal{O}_{1},\mathcal{O}_{2},\mathcal{O}_{3}
are operators such that

(5.9)

|\left\langle\Psi,\mathcal{O}_{1}\Psi\right\rangle_{\mathscr{F}}|\leqslant C\|\mathcal{O}_{2}\Psi\|_{\mathscr{F}}\|\mathcal{O}_{3}\Psi\|_{\mathscr{F}}\ ,\qquad\forall\Psi\in\mathscr{F}

for a constant
C>0
, then it follows from the above decomposition of
\nu
and the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality that

(5.10)

|\nu(\mathcal{O}_{1})|\leqslant C\nu(\mathcal{O}_{2}^{*}\mathcal{O}_{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}\,\nu(\mathcal{O}_{3}^{*}\mathcal{O}_{3})^{\frac{1}{2}}\ .

In most applications,
\mathcal{O}_{2}
and
\mathcal{O}_{3}
shall corespond to either
\mathcal{N}
or
\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{S}}
.

Let us briefly postpone the proof of the above Lemma to the next subsubsection.
First, we turn to the proof of the important Proposition
5.1
.

Proof of Proposition
5.1
.

The decomposition for
\mathfrak{h}_{I}(t)
from (
5.4
)
combined with the commutator estimates
from Lemma
5.1

imply that for all
\ell\geqslant 1
there exists
C=C(\ell)>0

such that

(5.11)

\partial_{t}\nu_{t}(\mathcal{N}^{\ell}+\mathds{1})=\nu_{t}\big(i[\mathfrak{h}_{I}(t),\mathcal{N}^{\ell}]\big)\leqslant C\lambda R\nu_{t}(\mathcal{N}^{\ell}+\mathds{1}),\qquad\forall t\geqslant 0\ .

Gronwall’s inequality now easily implies that
there exists a constant
C>0
such that

(5.12)

\displaystyle\nu_{t}(\mathcal{N}^{\ell})\,\leqslant\,C\lambda R\,\nu_{t}(\mathcal{N}^{\ell}+\mathds{1})e^{C\lambda Rt}\ ,\qquad\forall t\geqslant 0\ .

To finalize the proof, we use the fact that for quasi-free states it holds true that
\nu(\mathcal{N}^{\ell})\lesssim\nu(\mathcal{N})^{\ell}
,
together with the assumption

\nu(\mathcal{N})=n\geqslant 1
.
∎

5.1.1.
Commutator Estimates for
\mathcal{N}

Proof of Lemma
5.1
.

Throughout this proof,
\Psi\in\mathscr{F}
denotes an element in
\cap_{k=1}^{\infty}D(\mathcal{N}^{k})
,
which will justify all of the upcoming calculations.
Let us now fix
\ell\in\mathbb{N}.

Proof of (1).
This is an immediate consequence of the fact that
[D_{k}(t),\mathcal{N}]=0
for all
k\in\Lambda^{*}
and
t\in\mathbb{R}
. See Lemma
4.3
.

Proof of (2).

Using the fact that
D_{k}^{*}(t)=D_{-k}(t)
and
[D_{k}^{*}(t),b_{k}(t)]=0

we may re-write
the fermion-boson interaction term as

(5.13)

\displaystyle V_{FB}(t)=\int_{\Lambda^{*}}\hat{V}(k)D^{*}_{k}(t)b_{k}(t)\mathrm{d}k\ +\ \mathrm{h.c}\ .

Thus, we find that
for all
t\in\mathbb{R}

(5.14)

\left\langle\Psi,[\mathcal{N}^{\ell},V_{FB}(t)]\Psi\right\rangle=2\mathrm{Im}\int_{\Lambda^{*}}\hat{V}(k)\langle\Psi,[\mathcal{N}^{\ell},D^{*}_{k}(t)b_{k}(t)]\Psi\rangle\ .

In view of Lemma
4.3
,
we see that
[D_{k}^{*}(t),\mathcal{N}^{\ell}]=0
.
Further, using the pull-through formulae for
b
-operators
with
f(x)=x^{\ell}

we find the following useful identity

(5.15)

\displaystyle[\mathcal{N}^{\ell},b_{k}(t)]=\sum_{n=0}^{\ell-1}\binom{\ell}{n}(-2)^{\ell-n}\mathcal{N}^{n}b_{k}(t)\ ,\qquad\forall k\in\Lambda^{*},\ t\in\mathbb{R}\ .

Consequently,
we can estimate that

(5.16)

\displaystyle|\left\langle\Psi,[\mathcal{N}^{\ell},V_{FB}]\Psi\right\rangle|

\displaystyle\leqslant\sum_{n=0}^{\ell-1}\binom{\ell}{n}(-2)^{\ell-n}\int_{\Lambda^{*}}|\hat{V}(k)|\ |\left\langle\Psi,D_{k}^{*}(t)\mathcal{N}^{n}b_{k}(t)\Psi\right\rangle|\mathrm{d}k

\displaystyle\leqslant\sum_{n=0}^{\ell-1}\binom{\ell}{n}(-2)^{\ell-n}\int_{\Lambda^{*}}|\hat{V}(k)|\ \|\mathcal{N}^{\frac{n-1}{2}}D_{k}(t)\Psi\|\,\|\mathcal{N}^{\frac{n+1}{2}}b_{k}(t)\Psi\|\mathrm{d}k\ .

We can now combine Lemma
4.3
,
the Type-I estimate (
4.21
)
and the norm bound (
4.20
)
to find that there exists a constant
C>0

such that

(5.17)

\|\mathcal{N}^{\frac{n-1}{2}}D_{k}(t)\Psi\|\,\|\mathcal{N}^{\frac{n+1}{2}}b_{k}(t)\Psi\|\leqslant CR\|\mathcal{N}^{\frac{n+1}{2}}\Psi\|^{2}\ ,\qquad\forall n\geqslant 0\ .

Finally, we put
the two above estimates together and use the elementary fact

\mathcal{N}^{\frac{n+1}{2}}\lesssim\mathcal{N}^{\ell}+1
(valid for
n\leqslant\ell-1
)
to find that for some
C>0
there holds

(5.18)

|\left\langle\Psi,[\mathcal{N}^{\ell},V_{FB}]\Psi\right\rangle|\leqslant CR\|\hat{V}\|_{\ell^{1}}\|(\mathcal{N}^{\ell}+1)\Psi\|^{2}\ ,\qquad\forall t\geqslant 0\

which gives the desired estimate.

Proof of (3)
.
First, we note that
[\mathcal{N},b_{k}^{*}(t)b_{k}(t)]=0
for all
t\in\mathbb{R}
and
k\in\Lambda^{*}
.
Hence, we can readily check that

(5.19)

\left\langle\Psi,[\mathcal{N}^{\ell},V_{B}(t)]\Psi\right\rangle=\mathrm{Im}\int\hat{V}(k)\left\langle\Psi,[\mathcal{N}^{\ell},b_{k}(t)b_{-k}(t)]\Psi\right\rangle\mathrm{d}k\qquad\forall t\in\mathbb{R}\ .

In view of the commutation relation

\mathcal{N}b_{k}(t)b_{-k}(t)=b_{k}(t)b_{-k}(t)(\mathcal{N}-4)
we can calculate
using the pull-through formula for
f(x)=x^{\ell}
that

(5.20)

\displaystyle[\mathcal{N}^{\ell},b_{k}(t)b_{-k}(t)]=\sum_{n=0}^{\ell-1}\binom{\ell}{n}4^{\ell-n}(\mathcal{N}+4)^{\frac{n-1}{2}}b_{k}(t)b_{-k}(t)\mathcal{N}^{\frac{n+1}{2}}\ .

Consequently, putting the last two displayed equations together one finds that
for all
t\in\mathbb{R}

(5.21)

\displaystyle|\left\langle\Psi,[\mathcal{N}^{\ell},V_{B}(t)]\Psi\right\rangle|

\displaystyle\leqslant\sum_{n=0}^{\ell-1}\binom{\ell}{n}4^{\ell-n}\int_{\Lambda^{*}}|\hat{V}(k)|\|(\mathcal{N}+4)^{\frac{n+1}{2}}\Psi\|\ \|b_{k}(t)b_{-k}(t)\mathcal{N}^{\frac{n-1}{2}}\Psi\|\mathrm{d}k\ .

We estimate the right hand side as follows.
First,
we note that

\|(\mathcal{N}+4)^{\frac{n+1}{2}}\Psi\|\leqslant C(\ell)\|(\mathcal{N}+1)^{\ell/2}\Psi\|

for all
0\leqslant n\leqslant\ell-1
.
Secondly, we use the Type-II estimate (
4.24
)
and the commutation relation (
4.16
) for
f\equiv 1

to find that

\displaystyle\|b_{k}(t)b_{-k}(t)\mathcal{N}^{\frac{n-1}{2}}\Psi\|

\displaystyle\ \lesssim\ R^{\frac{1}{2}}\,\|(\mathcal{N}+2)^{\frac{1}{2}}b_{-k}(t)\mathcal{N}^{\frac{n-1}{2}}\Psi\|

\displaystyle\ =\ R^{\frac{1}{2}}\,\|b_{-k}(t)\mathcal{N}^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathcal{N}^{\frac{n-1}{2}}\Psi\|

\displaystyle\ \lesssim\ R\,\|\mathcal{N}^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathcal{N}^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathcal{N}^{\frac{n-1}{2}}\Psi\|

(5.22)

\displaystyle\ \lesssim\ R\,\|(\mathcal{N}+1)^{\frac{\ell}{2}}\Psi\|

where again we used the fact that
n\leqslant\ell-1
.
The proof of the Lemma is easily
finished after we put together the last two displayed estimates.
∎

5.2.
Surface-localized Number Operator Estimates

The main purpose of this section is proving Proposition
5.2
.
In order to control the time evolution of
\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{S}}
with respect to
\mathfrak{h}_{I}(t)
, we establish the following commutator estimates.
Recall that
R=|\Lambda|p_{F}^{d-1}
.

Lemma 5.2
.

There exists a constant
C>0
such that the following estimates hold true

(1)

For all
\Psi\in\mathscr{F}

(5.23)

|\left\langle\Psi,[\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{S}},V_{F}(t)]\Psi\right\rangle_{\mathscr{F}}|\leqslant C\|\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{S}}^{1/2}\Psi\|_{\mathscr{F}}\|\mathcal{N}^{3/2}\Psi\|_{\mathscr{F}}\ .

(2)

For all
\Psi\in\mathscr{F}

\displaystyle|\left\langle\Psi,[\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{S}},V_{FB}(t)]\Psi\right\rangle_{\mathscr{F}}|

\displaystyle\leqslant CR^{1/2}\|\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{S}}^{1/2}\Psi\|_{\mathscr{F}}\|\mathcal{N}\Psi\|_{\mathscr{F}}\ .

(3)

For all
\Psi\in\mathscr{F}

|\left\langle\Psi,[\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{S}},V_{B}(t)]\Psi\right\rangle_{\mathscr{F}}|\leqslant CR\|\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{S}}^{1/2}\Psi\|^{2}_{\mathscr{F}}+CR\|\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{S}}^{1/2}\Psi\|_{\mathscr{F}}\|\Psi\|_{\mathscr{F}}\ .

We shall defer the proof of Lemma
5.2
to next subsubsection. Now
we turn our attention to the proof of Proposition
5.2
.

Proof of Proposition
5.2
.

Throughout the proof,
C>0
is a constant whose value may change from line to line.
First, in view of the decomposition of
\mathfrak{h}_{I}(t)
given in (
5.4
),
Lemma
5.2

and Remark
5.1
,
there holds
for all
t\in\mathbb{R}

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\nu_{t}(\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{S}})=\nu_{t}(i[\mathfrak{h}_{I}(t),\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{S}}])

\displaystyle\leqslant C\lambda[\nu_{t}(\mathcal{N}_{S})]^{\frac{1}{2}}[\nu_{t}(\mathcal{N}^{3})]^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\ +C\lambda R^{\frac{1}{2}}[\nu_{t}(\mathcal{N}_{S})]^{\frac{1}{2}}[\nu_{t}(\mathcal{N}^{2})]^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\ +C\lambda R[\nu_{t}(\mathcal{N}_{S})]

(5.24)

\displaystyle\ +C\lambda R[\nu_{t}(\mathcal{N}_{S})]^{\frac{1}{2}}[\nu_{t}(\mathds{1})]^{\frac{1}{2}}\ .

Thus, we divide
1
1

1

Technically, one should introduce a regularization
u_{\delta}(t)\equiv(\delta+\nu_{t}(\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{S}}))^{1/2}
in order to avoid possible singularities whenever
\nu_{t}(\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{S}})=0
. One should then close the estimates after taking the limit
\delta\downarrow 0
. We leave the details to the reader

by
\nu_{t}(\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{S}})^{1/2}

to find that thanks to Proposition
5.1

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\nu_{t}(\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{S}})^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leqslant C\lambda R\nu_{t}(\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{S}})^{\frac{1}{2}}+C\lambda R\Big(\nu_{t}(\mathcal{N}^{3})^{\frac{1}{2}}/R+\nu_{t}(\mathcal{N}^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}/R^{\frac{1}{2}}+1\Big)

(5.25)

\displaystyle\leqslant C\lambda R\nu_{t}(\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{S}})^{\frac{1}{2}}+C\lambda R\exp(\lambda Rt)\Big(n^{\frac{3}{2}}/R+n/R^{\frac{1}{2}}+1\Big)\ .

The Grönwall inequality
now implies that for all
t\geqslant 0

(5.26)

\displaystyle\nu_{t}(\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{S}})^{\frac{1}{2}}\leqslant C\exp(C\lambda Rt)\Big(\nu_{0}(\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{S}})^{\frac{1}{2}}+\lambda Rt\,(n^{\frac{3}{2}}/R+n/R^{\frac{1}{2}}+1)\Big)\ .

Finally, we notice that in view of Condition
2
we have
\nu_{0}(\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{S}})\lesssim(\lambda R)^{2}
.
The proof is then finished once we simplify the right hand side using the bound

n\lesssim R^{1/2}
,
and take squares on both sides of the inequality.
∎

5.2.1.
Commutator Estimates for
\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{S}}

In order to prove Lemma
5.2
, we shall first establish the following useful lemma.
Here and in the sequel,
\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{S}}
denotes the characteristic function of the Fermi surface
\mathcal{S}
.

Lemma 5.3
.

For all
k\in\Lambda^{*}
and
g\in\ell^{\infty}
the operator

(5.27)

\mathcal{O}(k):=\int_{\Lambda^{*}}\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{S}}(p)g(p)a_{p+k}^{*}a_{p}\mathrm{d}p

satisfies the following estimate

(5.28)

\displaystyle\big|\left\langle\Phi,\mathcal{O}(k)\Psi\right\rangle_{\mathscr{F}}\big|\leqslant\|g\|_{\ell^{\infty}}\|\mathcal{N}^{1/2}\Phi\|\|\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{S}}^{1/2}\Psi\|\ ,\qquad\forall\Phi,\,\Psi\in\mathscr{F}.

Proof.

Let
\Phi,\Psi\in\mathscr{F}
,
k\in\Lambda^{*}
and
g\in\ell^{\infty}
.
Then, we calculate

\displaystyle|\left\langle\Phi,\mathcal{O}(k)\Psi\right\rangle_{\mathscr{F}}|

\displaystyle=\bigg|\int_{\Lambda^{*}}\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{S}}(p)g(p)\left\langle a_{p+k}\Phi,a_{p}\Psi\right\rangle_{\mathscr{F}}\mathrm{d}p\bigg|

\displaystyle\leqslant\int_{\Lambda^{*}}\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{S}}(p)|g(p)|\|a_{p+k}\Phi\|_{\mathscr{F}}\|a_{p}\Psi\|_{\mathscr{F}}\mathrm{d}p

\displaystyle\leqslant\|g\|_{\ell^{\infty}}\biggl(\int_{\Lambda^{*}}\|a_{p+k}\Phi\|_{\mathscr{F}}^{2}\mathrm{d}p\biggr)^{\frac{1}{2}}\biggl(\int_{\Lambda^{*}}\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{S}}(p)\|a_{p}\Psi\|_{\mathscr{F}}^{2}\mathrm{d}p\biggr)^{\frac{1}{2}}

(5.29)

\displaystyle=\|g\|_{\ell^{\infty}}\|\mathcal{N}^{\frac{1}{2}}\Phi\|_{\mathscr{F}}\|\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{S}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\Psi\|_{\mathscr{F}}\ .

In the last line we used the fact that
\|a_{p}\Phi\|^{2}_{\mathscr{F}}=\left\langle\Phi,a_{p}^{*}a_{p}\Phi\right\rangle_{\mathscr{F}}
for all
p\in\Lambda^{*}
, plus a change of variables
p\mapsto p-k
.
A similar argument holds for the term containing
\Psi
. This finishes the proof.
∎

Proof of Lemma
5.2
.

Throughout this proof,
\Psi\in\mathscr{F}
is fixed.
In addition, in order to ease the notation, we shall drop the explicit time dependence in our estimates – since the estimates are uniform in
t\in\mathbb{R}
, there is no risk of confusion.
Let us now fix
\ell\in\mathbb{N}.

Proof of (1).

Starting from (
3.8
) we can first calculate that

(5.30)

\displaystyle\left\langle\Psi,[\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{S}},V_{F}]\Psi\right\rangle

\displaystyle=2\mathrm{i}\ \int_{\Lambda^{*}}\hat{V}(k)\mathrm{Im}\left\langle\Psi,[\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{S}},D^{*}(k)]D(k)\Psi\right\rangle\mathrm{d}k\ .

We now put the above commutator in an appropiate form.
Using the explicit
expression of
D^{*}(k)
in terms of creation- and annihilation- operators (see Def.
7
) together with the CAR,
we find that
for all
k\in\Lambda^{*}
there holds

\displaystyle[\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{S}},D^{*}(k)]

\displaystyle=\int_{\Lambda^{*}}\Big(\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{S}}(p)-\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{S}}(p-k)\Big)\chi^{\perp}(p)\chi^{\perp}(p-k)a_{p}^{*}a_{p-k}\,\mathrm{d}p

\displaystyle-\int_{\Lambda^{*}}\Big(\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{S}}(h)-\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{S}}(h+k)\Big)\chi(h)\chi(h+k)a_{h}^{*}a_{h+k}\,\mathrm{d}h

(5.31)

\displaystyle\equiv\mathcal{O}_{1}(k)+\mathcal{O}_{2}(k)

where we introduce the two following auxiliary operators (notice the change of variables
p\mapsto p+k
and
h\mapsto h-k
in the second operator)

(5.32)

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}_{1}(k)

\displaystyle:=\int_{\Lambda^{*}}\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{S}}(p)\chi^{\perp}(p,p-k)a_{p}^{*}a_{p-k}\,\mathrm{d}p-\int_{\Lambda^{*}}\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{S}}(h)\chi(h,h+k)a_{h}^{*}a_{h+k}\,\mathrm{d}h

(5.33)

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}_{2}(k)

\displaystyle:=-\int_{\Lambda^{*}}\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{S}}(p)\chi^{\perp}(p,p+k)a_{p+k}^{*}a_{p}\,\mathrm{d}p+\int_{\Lambda^{*}}\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{S}}(h)\chi(h,h-k)a_{h-k}^{*}a_{h}\,\mathrm{d}h

where for simplicity we denote
\chi^{\perp}(p,p-k)\equiv\chi^{\perp}(p)\chi^{\perp}(p-k)
and similarly for
\chi(h,h+k)
.
We are now able to write

(5.34)

\displaystyle\left\langle\Psi,[\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{S}},V_{F}]\Psi\right\rangle

\displaystyle=2\mathrm{i}\ \int_{\Lambda^{*}}\hat{V}(k)\mathrm{Im}\left\langle\Psi,\mathcal{O}_{1}(k)D(k)\Psi\right\rangle\mathrm{d}k

(5.35)

\displaystyle+2\mathrm{i}\ \int_{\Lambda^{*}}\hat{V}(k)\mathrm{Im}\left\langle\Psi,D(k)\mathcal{O}_{2}(k)\Psi\right\rangle\mathrm{d}k

(5.36)

\displaystyle+2\mathrm{i}\ \int_{\Lambda^{*}}\hat{V}(k)\mathrm{Im}\left\langle\Psi,[\mathcal{O}_{2}(k),D(k)]\Psi\right\rangle\mathrm{d}k\ .

The first term in the above equation can be estimated using Lemma
5.3
for
\mathcal{O}(k)=\mathcal{O}^{*}_{1}(k)
. Namely,

(5.37)

\displaystyle\big|2\mathrm{i}\ \int_{\Lambda^{*}}\hat{V}(k)\mathrm{Im}\left\langle\Psi,\mathcal{O}_{1}(k)D(k)\Psi\right\rangle\mathrm{d}k\big|\leqslant 2\|\hat{V}\|_{\ell^{1}}\|\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{S}}^{1/2}\Psi\|\|\mathcal{N}^{3/2}\Psi\|

The second term in the above equation is estimates using Lemma
5.3
for
\mathcal{O}(k)=\mathcal{O}_{2}(k)
. We get

(5.38)

\displaystyle\big|2\mathrm{i}\ \int_{\Lambda^{*}}\hat{V}(k)\mathrm{Im}\left\langle\Psi,D(k)\mathcal{O}_{2}\Psi\right\rangle\mathrm{d}k\big|\leqslant 2\|\hat{V}\|_{\ell^{1}}\|\mathcal{N}^{3/2}\Psi\|\|\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{S}}^{1/2}\Psi\|

The third term in the above equation is actually zero. This comes from the fact that the commutator between
\mathcal{O}_{2}(k)
and
D(k)
is self-adjoint. More precisely, we can calculate
using the CAR

(5.39)

\displaystyle[\mathcal{O}_{2}(k),D(k)]

\displaystyle=\int_{\Lambda^{*}}\Big(\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{S}}(p+k)-\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{S}}(p)\Big)\chi^{\perp}(p,p+k)a_{p}^{*}a_{p}\mathrm{d}p

(5.40)

\displaystyle-\int_{\Lambda^{*}}\Big(\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{S}}(h-k)-\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{S}}(h)\Big)\chi(h,h-k)a_{h}^{*}a_{h}\mathrm{d}h\ .

We put our results together to find that

(5.41)

\displaystyle\big|\left\langle\Psi,[\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{S}},V_{F}]\Psi\right\rangle\big|

\displaystyle\leqslant 4\|\hat{V}\|_{\ell^{1}}\|\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{S}}^{1/2}\Psi\|\|\mathcal{N}^{3/2}\Psi\|\ .

Proof of (2).

Starting form (
3.9
) we can calculate that

\displaystyle|\left\langle\Psi,[\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{S}},V_{FB}]\Psi\right\rangle|

\displaystyle\leqslant 2\int_{\Lambda^{*}}|\hat{V}(k)|\,|\left\langle\Psi,[\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{S}},D^{*}(k)b(k)]\Psi\right\rangle|\mathrm{d}k\ ,

\displaystyle\leqslant 2\int_{\Lambda^{*}}|\hat{V}(k)|\,\|[\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{S}},D(k)]\Psi\|\,\|b(k)\Psi\|\mathrm{d}k

(5.42)

\displaystyle\quad+2\int_{\Lambda^{*}}|\hat{V}(k)|\,\|[\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{S}},b(k)]\Psi\|\,\|D(k)\Psi\|\mathrm{d}k\ .

Let us estimate the first term contained in the right hand side of (
5.42
).
In view of
D^{*}(k)=D(-k)
and (
5.31
) we have
that
[\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{S}},D(k)]=\mathcal{O}_{1}(-k)+\mathcal{O}_{2}(-k)
.
Each
\mathcal{O}_{i}(k)
can be estimated using (
4.18
) –we conclude that

\|[\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{S}},D(k)]\Psi\|\lesssim\|\mathcal{N}\Psi\|\ .

On the other hand, we use the Type-II estimate (
4.24
) on
b(k)
. We conclude that

(5.43)

\displaystyle\int_{\Lambda^{*}}|\hat{V}(k)|\,\|[\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{S}},D(k)]\Psi\|\,\|b(k)\Psi\|\mathrm{d}k\ \lesssim\ R^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\mathcal{N}\Psi\|\,\|\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{S}}^{1/2}\Psi\|\ .

Let us now look at the second term contained in (
5.42
).
First, we
recall that for
k\in\textnormal{supp}\hat{V}

there holds
[\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{S}},b(k)]=-2b(k)
, see Lemma
4.4
.
Consequently, using
the Type-II estimate (
4.24
) we
see that
\|[\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{S}},b(k)]\Psi\|\lesssim R^{1/2}\|\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{S}}^{1/2}\Psi\|
.
On the other hand, we can use the Type-I estimate (
4.21
) to find
\|D(k)\Psi\|\lesssim\|\mathcal{N}\Psi\|
.
These upper bounds can be put together to find that

(5.44)

\displaystyle\int_{\Lambda^{*}}|\hat{V}(k)|\,\|[\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{S}},b(k)]\Psi\|\,\|D(k)\Psi\|\mathrm{d}k

\displaystyle\ \lesssim\ R^{1/2}\|\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{S}}^{1/2}\Psi\|\|\mathcal{N}\Psi\|\ .

A direct combination of the last three displayed estimates finish the proof of (2).

Proof of (3)
.
Starting from (
3.10
) we decompose the boson-boson interaction into a diagonal, and off-diagonal part.
Namely, we write
V_{B}=V_{1}+V_{2}
, where we
set

(5.45)

\displaystyle V_{1}\equiv\int_{\Lambda^{*}}\hat{V}(k)b^{*}(k)b(k)\mathrm{d}k\quad\textnormal{and}\quad V_{2}\equiv\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Lambda^{*}}\hat{V}(k)\Big(b(k)b(-k)+\mathrm{h.c}\Big)\mathrm{d}k\ .

For
V_{1}
we can quickly verify that its commutator with
\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{S}}
vanishes.
Indeed, thanks to Lemma
4.4

we find that

[\mathcal{N}_{S},b^{*}(k)b(k)]=+2b^{*}(k)b(k)-2b^{*}(k)b(k)=0

for all
k\in\textnormal{supp}\hat{V}
.
Hence,
[\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{S}},V_{1}]=0
upon summing over
k\in\Lambda^{*}
.

For
V_{2}
, we have the preliminary upper bound as our starting point

(5.46)

\displaystyle|\left\langle\Psi,[\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{S}},V_{2}]\Psi\right\rangle|

\displaystyle\leqslant 2\int_{\Lambda^{*}}|\hat{V}(k)||\left\langle\Psi,b(k)b(-k)\Psi\right\rangle|\mathrm{d}k\ .

We estimate the integrand of the right hand side as follows –let us fix
k\in\textnormal{supp}\hat{V}
.
First, recalling that
[\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{S}},b_{k}]=0
(see Lemma
4.4
)
we find that for any measurable function
\varphi:\mathbb{R}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}

the following
pull-through formula
holds true

(5.47)

\displaystyle\varphi(\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{S}})b(k)=b(k)\varphi(\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{S}}-2)

Thus, using
\varphi(x)=(x+5)^{1/2}
we find

\displaystyle|\left\langle\Psi,b(k)b(-k)\Psi\right\rangle|

\displaystyle=|\left\langle(\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{S}}+5)^{1/2}\Psi,b(k)b(-k)(\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{S}}+1)^{-1/2}\Psi\right\rangle|

(5.48)

\displaystyle\leqslant\|(\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{S}}+5)^{1/2}\Psi\|\,\|b(k)b(-k)(\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{S}}+1)^{-1/2}\Psi\|\ .

We use again the commutation relation
[\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{S}},b_{k}]=0

and the Type-II estimate (
4.24
) for
b
-operators to find that

\displaystyle\|b(k)b(-k)(\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{S}}+1)^{-1/2}\Psi\|

\displaystyle\,\lesssim\,R^{1/2}\|\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{S}}^{1/2}b(-k)(\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{S}}+1)^{-1/2}\Psi\|

\displaystyle\,\leqslant\,R^{1/2}\|(\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{S}}+2)^{1/2}b(-k)(\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{S}}+1)^{-1/2}\Psi\|

\displaystyle\,=\,R^{1/2}\|b(-k)\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{S}}^{1/2}(\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{S}}+1)^{-1/2}\Psi\|

\displaystyle\,\lesssim\,R\|\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{S}}^{1/2}\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{S}}^{1/2}(\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{S}}+1)^{-1/2}\Psi\|

(5.49)

\displaystyle\,\leqslant\,R\|\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{S}}^{1/2}\Psi\|\ .

On the other hand, the other term multiplying in (
5.48
)
can be bounded as follows

\|(\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{S}}+5)^{1/2}\Psi\|\lesssim\|\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{S}}^{1/2}\Psi\|+\|\Psi\|\ .

A straightforward combination of the estimates contained in (
5.46
), (
5.48
) and (
5.49
) now finish the proof.
∎

6.
Leading Order Terms I: Emergence of
Q

In Section
3
we considered a double commutator expansion (
3.20
) for the momentum distribution of particles and holes,

f_{t}(p)
.
This expansion is expressed in terms of the nine quantities
\{T_{\alpha,\beta}(t)\}

that
arise from the three different interacting potentials
V_{F}
,
V_{FB}
and
V_{B}
, respectively.
The main goal of this section is analyzing the single term
T_{F,F}
.
In particular, we prove that one may extract the mollified collision operator
Q_{t}
–originally introduced in Def.
3
–
up to reminder terms that we have control of.
A precise statement is given in the following proposition.
We remind the reader that
R=|\Lambda|p_{F}^{d-1}

Proposition 6.1

(Analysis of
T_{F,F}
)
.

Let
T_{F,F}(t,p)
be the quantity defined in Eq. (
3.21
) for
\alpha=\beta=F
,
and let
m>0
.
Then, there exists a constant
C>0

such that for all
\varphi\in\ell_{m}^{1}
and
t\geqslant 0

the following inequality holds true

(6.1)

\displaystyle\big|T_{F,F}(t,\varphi)+\,|\Lambda|\,t\left\langle\varphi,Q_{t}[f_{0}]\right\rangle\big|\leqslant C|\Lambda|\lambda t^{3}\|\hat{V}\|_{\ell^{1}}^{3}\|\varphi\|_{\ell^{1}}\sup_{\tau\leqslant t}\Big(R^{2}\nu_{\tau}(\mathcal{N}^{4})^{\frac{1}{2}}+\nu_{\tau}(\mathcal{N}^{4})\Big)

where
T_{F,F}(t,\varphi)\equiv\left\langle\varphi,T_{F,F}(t)\right\rangle
and
Q_{t}
is given in Def.
3
.

In order to prove Proposition
6.1
we shall perform an additional expansion of
\nu_{t}
with respect to the interaction Hamiltonian
\mathfrak{h}_{I}(t)
.
Namely, we consider

\displaystyle T_{F,F}(t,\varphi)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{t_{1}}\nu\big([[N(\varphi),V_{F}(t_{1})],V_{F}(t_{2})]\big)\mathrm{d}t_{1}\mathrm{d}t_{2}

(6.2)

\displaystyle\quad-i\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{t_{1}}\int_{0}^{t_{2}}\nu_{t_{2}}\big([[[N(\varphi),V_{F}(t_{1})],V_{F}(t_{2})],\mathfrak{h}_{I}(t_{3})]\big)\mathrm{d}t_{1}\mathrm{d}t_{2}\mathrm{d}t_{3}\ ,

where we recall
N(\varphi)\equiv\int_{\Lambda^{*}}\overline{\varphi(p)}a_{p}^{*}a_{p}\,\mathrm{d}p
.
We then analyze the two terms of the right hand side of (
6.2
) separately.
Thus, we split the proof into two parts, which are contained in the following two lemmas.

Lemma 6.1
.

Let
\nu:B(\mathscr{F})\rightarrow\mathbb{C}
be an initial state
satisfying Condition
2
,
and let

f_{0}(p)=|\Lambda|^{-1}\nu(a_{p}^{*}a_{p})
for all
p\in\Lambda^{*}
.
Let
V_{F}(t)
be the Heisenberg evolution of the fermion-fermion interaction,
defined in (
3.19
) for
\alpha=F
.
Then, for all
\varphi\in\ell^{1}
and
t\geqslant 0

(6.3)

\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{t_{1}}\nu\big([[N(\varphi),V_{F}(t_{1})],V_{F}(t_{2})]\big)\mathrm{d}t_{1}\mathrm{d}t_{2}=-t|\Lambda|\left\langle\varphi,Q_{t}[f_{0}]\right\rangle\ .

The proof of the identity contained in Lemma
6.1

will be heavily inspired by the work of Erdős, Salmhofer and Yau
[
32
]
,
on a heuristic derivation of the quantum Boltzmann equation.
In fact, we shall make use of some of their algebraic relations.

Lemma 6.2
.

Let
(\nu_{t})_{t\in\mathbb{R}}
be the interaction dynamics as given in Def.
5
, with initial data
\nu=\nu_{0}
satisfying Condition
2
.
Let
V_{F}(t)
be the Heisenberg evolution of the fermion-fermion interaction,
defined in (
3.19
) for
\alpha=F
.
Then, there exists a constant
C>0
such that
for all
\varphi\in\ell^{1}
and
t\geqslant 0

\displaystyle\bigg|\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{t_{1}}\int_{0}^{t_{2}}\nu_{t_{2}}\big([[[N(\varphi),

\displaystyle V_{F}(t_{1})],V_{F}(t_{2})],\mathfrak{h}_{I}(t_{3})]\big)\mathrm{d}t_{1}\mathrm{d}t_{2}\mathrm{d}t_{3}\bigg|

(6.4)

\displaystyle\leqslant C\lambda t^{3}\|\hat{V}\|_{\ell^{1}}^{3}|\Lambda|\|\varphi\|_{\ell^{1}}\sup_{\tau\leqslant t}\Big(\nu_{\tau}(\mathcal{N}^{4})+R^{2}\nu_{\tau}(\mathcal{N}^{4})^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big)\ .

We remind the reader that the interaction Hamiltonian
\mathfrak{h}_{I}(t)

admits the decomposition given in (
5.4
)
in terms of the Heisenberg evolution of
b
and
D
-operators–see (
5.5
), (
5.6
) and (
5.7
).

Proof of Proposition
6.1
.

It suffices to put together Eq. (
6.2
) and Lemmas
6.1
and
6.2
.
∎

We dedicate the rest of this section to the proof of Lemmas
6.1
and
6.2
.

6.1.
Proof of Lemma
6.1

Before we jump into the proof of Lemma
6.1
,
we shall re-write the fermion-fermion interaction term
V_{F}(t)

in a form that will be suitable for our analysis.
This representation is recorded in Lemma
6.3
,
which we study in the next subsubsection.

6.1.1.
Normal ordering of
V_{F}(t)

Let us fix the time label
t\in\mathbb{R}
.
First, we see from (
5.5
) that
V_{F}(t)=\int_{\Lambda^{*}}\hat{V}(k)D_{k}^{*}(t)D_{k}(t)\mathrm{d}k
can be written in terms of
the Heisenberg evolution of the
D
-operators, as given in Def.
7
.
These can be written explicitly in terms of creation- and annihilation-
in the following way

(6.5)

\displaystyle D_{k}(t)=\int_{(\Lambda^{*})^{2}}d_{t}(k,p,q)a_{p}^{*}a_{q}\mathrm{d}p\mathrm{d}q

where the coefficients in the above expression are given as follows

(6.6)

d_{k}(t,p,q)\,\equiv\,e^{it(E_{p}-E_{q})}\big[\chi^{\perp}(p)\chi^{\perp}(q)\delta(p-q+k)-\chi(p)\chi(q)\delta(p-q-k)\big]

for all
k,p,q\in\Lambda^{*}
.
Since
D_{k}^{*}(t)=D_{-k}(t)
it readily follows that we can write the fermion-fermion interaction in the following form

(6.7)

V_{F}(t)=\int_{\Lambda^{*4}}\bigg[\int_{\Lambda^{*}}\hat{V}(k)d_{t}(-k,p_{1},q_{1})\,d_{t}(k,p_{2},q_{2})\mathrm{d}k\bigg]a_{p_{1}}^{*}a_{q_{1}}a_{p_{2}}^{*}a_{q_{2}}\mathrm{d}p_{1}\mathrm{d}p_{2}\mathrm{d}q_{1}\mathrm{d}q_{2}\ .

Clearly, the expression in (
6.7
) is
not
normally ordered.
Our next goal is then to put
V_{F}(t)
in normal order, with explicit coefficients.
To this end, we introduce the following coefficient function

(6.8)

\phi_{t}(\vec{p})\equiv\int_{\Lambda^{*}}\hat{V}(k)\,d_{t}(-k,p_{1},p_{4})\,d_{t}(k,p_{2},p_{3})\mathrm{d}k

where
\vec{p}=(p_{1},p_{2},p_{3},p_{4})\in(\Lambda^{*})^{4}
.
A straightforward calculation using the CAR in Eq. (
6.7
)
now yields

\displaystyle V_{F}(t)

\displaystyle=\int_{\Lambda^{*4}}\phi_{t}(p_{1},p_{2},q_{2},q_{1})a_{p_{1}}^{*}a_{p_{2}}^{*}a_{q_{2}}a_{q_{1}}\mathrm{d}p_{1}\mathrm{d}p_{2}\mathrm{d}q_{1}\mathrm{d}q_{2}

(6.9)

\displaystyle+\int_{\Lambda^{*2}}\bigg[\ \int_{\Lambda^{*2}}\phi_{t}(p_{1},p_{2},q_{2},q_{1})\delta(q_{1}-p_{2})\mathrm{d}p_{2}\mathrm{d}q_{1}\bigg]a_{p_{1}}^{*}a_{q_{2}}\mathrm{d}p_{1}\mathrm{d}q_{2}\ .

We shall denote by
:V_{F}(t):
the normal ordering of
V_{F}(t)
, that is, the first term in Eq. (
6.9
).

Next, we shall put the above normal order form in a more explicit representation by calculating
explictly the coefficient function
\phi_{t}
, together with its contraction for
q_{1}=p_{2}
.
Before we do so, let us introduce some convenient notation:

\square
When
\vec{p}=(p_{1},p_{2},p_{3},p_{4})\in(\Lambda^{*})^{4}
is known from context, we let

\chi_{1234}\equiv\chi(p_{1})\chi(p_{2})\chi(p_{3})\chi(p_{4})\qquad\textnormal{and}\qquad\chi_{1234}^{\perp}\equiv 1-\chi_{1234}

and similarly for
\chi_{ij}
and
\chi_{ij}^{\perp}
for any combination of

i,j\in\{1,2,3,4\}
.

\square
For any
\vec{p}=(p_{1},p_{2},p_{3},p_{4})\in(\Lambda^{*})^{4}
we let

(6.10)

\displaystyle\Delta E(\vec{p})\equiv E_{p_{1}}+E_{p_{2}}-E_{p_{3}}-E_{p_{4}}

where
E_{p}
is the dispersion relation of the system–see (
2.20
).

Starting from (
6.8
) and using the definition of
d_{t}(k,p,q)

we may explicitly calculate that
for all
\vec{p}\in(\Lambda^{*})^{4}
there holds

\displaystyle\phi_{t}(\vec{p})

\displaystyle=e^{it\Delta E(\vec{p})}\delta(p_{1}+p_{2}-p_{3}-p_{4})\hat{V}(p_{1}-p_{4})\big(\chi_{1234}+\chi_{1234}^{\perp}\big)

(6.11)

\displaystyle-e^{it\Delta E(\vec{p})}\delta(p_{1}-p_{2}+p_{3}-p_{4})\hat{V}(p_{1}-p_{4})\big(\chi_{13}\chi^{\perp}_{24}+\chi_{13}^{\perp}\chi_{24}\big)\ .

In particular, a straightforward calculation using (
6.1.1
)
shows that the integrand of the quadratic term in (
6.9
) can be written as

(6.12)

\int_{\Lambda^{*2}}\phi_{t}(p_{1},p_{2},q_{2},q_{1})\delta(q_{1}-p_{2})\mathrm{d}p_{2}\mathrm{d}q_{1}=\delta(p_{1}-p_{3})g(p_{1})

where

g(p)\equiv\chi(p)(\hat{V}*\chi)(p)+\chi^{\perp}(p)(\hat{V}*\chi^{\perp})(p)
–the explicit form of
g(p)
is not important,
but the
\delta(p_{1}-p_{3})
dependence in the last equation implies that the second term in (
6.9
)

commutes
with
a_{p}^{*}a_{p}
.
This fact we shall use in the proof of Lemma
6.1
.

Finally, thanks to the CAR, the coefficients
\phi_{t}(p_{1},p_{2},p_{3},p_{4})

inside of
:V_{F}(t):
can be antisymmetrized with respect to the permutation of the variables
(p_{1},p_{2})\mapsto(p_{2},p_{1})

and
(p_{3},p_{4})\mapsto(p_{4},p_{3})
, respectively.
Namely, the coefficients
\phi_{t}
in
:V_{F}(t):
may be replaced by

(6.13)

\Phi_{t}(\vec{p})\equiv\frac{1}{4}\Big(\phi_{t}(p_{1},p_{2},p_{3},p_{4})-\phi_{t}(p_{2},p_{1},p_{3},p_{4})+\phi_{t}(p_{2},p_{1},p_{4},p_{3})-\phi_{t}(p_{1},p_{2},p_{3},p_{4})\Big)\ ,

which can be put in an explicit form, using (
6.1.1
).
We record all these results in the following lemma.

Lemma 6.3

(Normal ordering)
.

Let
t\in\mathbb{R}
and

V_{F}(t)
the Heisenberg evolution of the fermion-fermion interaction.
Then, the following identity holds

(6.14)

V_{F}(t)\ =\ :\!V_{F}(t)\!:\ +\ N(g)\ .

Here,
:\!V_{F}(t)\!:\ =\ \int_{\Lambda^{*4}}\Phi_{t}(p_{1}\cdots p_{4})a_{p_{1}}^{*}a_{p_{2}}^{*}a_{p_{3}}a_{p_{4}}\mathrm{d}p_{1}\cdots\mathrm{d}p_{4}

is the normal ordering of
V_{F}(t)
,
and

N(g)=\int_{\Lambda^{*}}g(p)a_{p}^{*}a_{p}\mathrm{d}p
,
where

g(p)\equiv\chi(p)(\hat{V}*\chi)(p)+\chi^{\perp}(p)(\hat{V}*\chi^{\perp})(p)
.

The coefficient function
\Phi_{t}:(\Lambda^{*})^{4}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}

is partially antisymmetric

(6.15)

\Phi_{t}(p_{1},p_{2},p_{3},p_{4})=-\Phi_{t}(p_{2},p_{1},p_{3},p_{4})=+\Phi_{t}(p_{2},p_{1},p_{4},p_{3})=-\Phi_{t}(p_{1},p_{2},p_{3},p_{4})

and admits the following decomposition

\Phi_{t}=\Phi_{t}^{(1)}+\Phi_{t}^{(2)}

where
\Phi_{t}^{(1)}
is given by

(6.16)

\Phi_{t}^{(1)}(\vec{p})=\,\frac{1}{2}\,e^{it\Delta E(\vec{p})}\delta(p_{1}+p_{2}-p_{3}-p_{4})\big(\hat{V}(p_{1}-p_{4})-\hat{V}(p_{1}-p_{3})\big)\big(\chi_{1234}+\chi^{\perp}_{1234}\big)

and
\Phi_{t}^{(2)}
is given by

\displaystyle\Phi_{t}^{(2)}(\vec{p})

\displaystyle=\,\frac{1}{2}\,e^{it\Delta E(\vec{p})}\delta(p_{1}+p_{3}-p_{2}-p_{4})\hat{V}(p_{1}-p_{4})\big(\chi^{\perp}_{14}\chi_{23}+\chi^{\perp}_{23}\chi_{14}\big)

(6.17)

\displaystyle\ \ \ -\frac{1}{2}e^{it\Delta E(\vec{p})}\delta(p_{1}+p_{4}-p_{2}-p_{3})\hat{V}(p_{1}-p_{3})\big(\chi^{\perp}_{13}\chi_{24}+\chi^{\perp}_{24}\chi_{13}\big)\ .

6.1.2.
Proof of Lemma
6.1

Proof.

We start with the normal ordering of
V_{F}(t)
found in Lemma
6.3
.

First, we observe that we may disregard the quadratic term
N(g)\equiv\int_{\Lambda^{*}}g(t,p)a_{p}^{*}a_{p}\mathrm{d}p
.
Indeed, since
[a_{p}^{*}a_{p},N(g)]=0
we find that for any
p\in\Lambda^{*}

(6.18)

\displaystyle\nu\big([[a_{p}^{*}a_{p},V_{F}(t)],V_{F}(s)]\big)=\nu\big([[a_{p}^{*}a_{p},:\!V_{F}(t)\!:],V_{F}(s)]\big)

Furthermore, since
\nu
is
quasi-free and translation invariant,
it verifies the identities (
3.16
).
Thus, since
[a_{p}^{*}a_{p},:V_{F}(t):]
is quartic in creation- and annihilation operators, we find that

\displaystyle\nu\big([[a_{p}^{*}a_{p},V_{F}(t)],V_{F}(s)]\big)

\displaystyle=\nu\big([[a_{p}^{*}a_{p},:\!V_{F}(t)\!:],V_{F}(s)]\big)

\displaystyle=\nu\big([[a_{p}^{*}a_{p},:\!V_{F}(t)\!:]\,,\,:\!\!V_{F}(s)\!\!:]\big)-\nu\big([N(g),[a_{p}^{*}a_{p},:\!V_{F}(t)\!:]]\big)

(6.19)

\displaystyle=\nu\big([[a_{p}^{*}a_{p},:\!V_{F}(t)\!:]\,,\,:\!\!V_{F}(s)\!\!:]\big)\ ,

for all
p\in\Lambda^{*}
.

Secondly, we note that a standard calculation using the CAR implies that

\displaystyle\nu\big([[a_{p}^{*}a_{p},V_{F}(t)],V_{F}(s)]\big)

\displaystyle=\int_{\Lambda^{*4}\times\Lambda^{*4}}\big(\delta(p-k_{1})+\delta(p-k_{2})-\delta(p-k_{3})-\delta(p-k_{4})\big)

\displaystyle\qquad\times\Phi_{t}(\vec{k})\Phi_{s}(\vec{\ell})\nu\big([\,a_{k_{1}}^{*}a_{k_{2}}^{*}a_{k_{3}}a_{k_{4}}\,,\,a^{*}_{\ell_{1}}a^{*}_{\ell_{2}}a_{\ell_{3}}a_{\ell_{4}}\,]\big)\mathrm{d}\vec{k}\mathrm{d}\vec{\ell}

(6.20)

\displaystyle=\int_{\Lambda^{*4}\times\Lambda^{*4}}M_{p}(\vec{k},\vec{\ell})\,\nu(a_{k_{1}}^{*}a_{k_{2}}^{*}a_{k_{3}}a_{k_{4}}a^{*}_{\ell_{1}}a^{*}_{\ell_{2}}a_{\ell_{3}}a_{\ell_{4}})\mathrm{d}\vec{k}\mathrm{d}\vec{\ell}

where (supressing the explicit
t,s\in\mathbb{R}
dependence)

\displaystyle M_{p}(\vec{k},\vec{\ell})

\displaystyle\equiv\Phi_{t}(\vec{k})\Phi_{s}(\vec{\ell})\big(\delta(p-k_{1})+\delta(p-k_{2})-\delta(p-k_{3})-\delta(p-k_{4})\big)

\displaystyle\ \ -\Phi_{t}(\vec{\ell})\Phi_{s}(\vec{k})\big(\delta(p-\ell_{1})+\delta(p-\ell_{2})-\delta(p-\ell_{3})-\delta(p-\ell_{4})\big)\ .

A change of variables
(k_{3},k_{4},\ell_{1},\ell_{2},\ell_{3},\ell_{4})\mapsto(\ell_{3},\ell_{4},k_{3},k_{4},\ell_{1},\ell_{2})
now yields

(6.21)

\displaystyle\nu\big([[a_{p}^{*}a_{p},V_{F}(t)]

\displaystyle,V_{F}(s)]\big)

\displaystyle=\int_{\Lambda^{*4}\times\Lambda^{*4}}M_{p}(k_{1}k_{2}\ell_{3}\ell_{4},k_{3}k_{4}\ell_{1}\ell_{2})\,\nu(a_{k_{1}}^{*}a_{k_{2}}^{*}a_{\ell_{4}}a_{\ell_{3}}a^{*}_{k_{3}}a^{*}_{k_{4}}a_{\ell_{2}}a_{\ell_{1}})\mathrm{d}\vec{k}\,\mathrm{d}\vec{\ell}\ .

It is important to note that the coefficient function
M_{p}(\vec{k},\vec{\ell})
is antisymmetric with respect to
k_{1}\mapsto k_{2}
,
k_{3}\mapsto k_{4}
,
\ell_{1}\mapsto\ell_{2}
and
\ell_{3}\mapsto\ell_{4}
respectively.
In addition,
M_{p}(\vec{k},\vec{\ell})=-M_{p}(\vec{\ell},\vec{k})
.
Indeed, these symmetries allow to simplify the right hand side of the last equation as follows.
First, quasi-freeness of the state
\nu
implies that

(6.22)

\displaystyle\nu(a_{k_{1}}^{*}a_{k_{2}}^{*}a_{\ell_{4}}a_{\ell_{3}}a^{*}_{k_{3}}a^{*}_{k_{4}}a_{\ell_{2}}a_{\ell_{1}})=\det\begin{pmatrix}\nu_{11}&\nu_{12}&\nu_{13}&\nu_{14}\\
\nu_{21}&\tilde{\nu}_{22}&\tilde{\nu}_{23}&\tilde{\nu}_{24}\\
\nu_{31}&\tilde{\nu}_{32}&\tilde{\nu}_{33}&\tilde{\nu}_{34}\\
\nu_{41}&\tilde{\nu}_{42}&\tilde{\nu}_{43}&\tilde{\nu}_{44}\\
\end{pmatrix}

where we denote
\nu_{ij}\equiv\nu(a_{k_{i}}^{*}a_{\ell_{j}})
and

\tilde{\nu}_{ij}\equiv\delta(k_{i}-\ell_{j})-\nu_{ij}
.
Secondly, based on the symmetries of
M_{p}(\vec{k},\vec{\ell})
,
we may follow the algebraic analysis carried out in
[
32
, pps 374–375]

to find that

(6.23)

\displaystyle\nu\big([[a_{p}^{*}a_{p},

\displaystyle V_{F}(t)],V_{F}(s)]\big)

\displaystyle=\int_{\Lambda^{*4}\times\Lambda^{*4}}M_{p}(k_{1}k_{2}\ell_{3}\ell_{4},k_{3}k_{4}\ell_{1}\ell_{2})\,4\big(\nu_{11}\nu_{22}\tilde{\nu}_{33}\tilde{\nu}_{44}+4\nu_{11}\nu_{23}\nu_{42}\tilde{\nu}_{34}\big)\mathrm{d}\vec{k}\mathrm{d}\vec{\ell}\ .

Thirdly, translation invariance
\nu(a_{p}^{*}a_{q})=\delta(p-q)f_{0}(p)

now yields two terms

\displaystyle\nu\big([[a_{p}^{*}a_{p},V_{F}(t)],

\displaystyle V_{F}(s)]\big)

\displaystyle=4\int_{\Lambda^{*}}M_{p}(k_{1}k_{2}k_{3}k_{4},k_{3}k_{4}k_{1}k_{2})f_{0}(k_{1})f_{0}(k_{2})\tilde{f}_{0}(k_{3})\tilde{f}_{0}(k_{4})\mathrm{d}\vec{k}

(6.24)

\displaystyle\quad+16\int_{\Lambda^{*}}M_{p}(k_{1}k_{2}k_{2}k_{3},k_{3}k_{4}k_{1}k_{4})f_{0}(k_{1})f_{0}(k_{2})f_{0}(k_{3})\tilde{f}_{0}(k_{4})\mathrm{d}\vec{k}.

Similarly as in
[
32
]
, we look at the two terms of the right hand side of (
6.1.2
) by evaluating the function
M_{p}
in the different cases.

The second term of (
6.1.2
)
. Let us show that the second term vanishes.
Indeed, we use the fact that

\Phi_{t}(k_{3}k_{4}k_{1}k_{2})=\Phi_{-t}(k_{1}k_{2}k_{3}k_{4})

together with antisymmetry with respect to
k_{1}\mapsto k_{2}
and
k_{3}\mapsto k_{4}
to find that

(6.25)

\displaystyle M_{p}(k_{1}k_{2}k_{2}

\displaystyle k_{3},k_{3}k_{4}k_{1}k_{4})

\displaystyle=2\cos\big[(t-s)(E_{1}-E_{3})\big]\big(\delta(p-k_{3})-\delta(p-k_{1})\big)\Phi(k_{1}k_{2}k_{3}k_{2})\Phi(k_{1}k_{4}k_{3}k_{4})

where we denote
\Phi(\vec{k})\equiv\Phi_{0}(\vec{k})
.
One may verify that

\Phi(k_{1}k_{2}k_{3}k_{2})

is proportional to
\delta(k_{1}-k_{3})

and,
consequently,
it holds that

\big(\delta(p-k_{3})-\delta(p-k_{1})\big)\Phi(k_{1}k_{2}k_{3}k_{2})=0
.

The first term of (
6.1.2
)
.
Using the fact that

\Phi_{t}(k_{3}k_{4}k_{1}k_{2})=\Phi_{-t}(k_{1}k_{2}k_{3}k_{4})

one finds

(6.26)

\displaystyle M_{p}(k_{1}k_{2}

\displaystyle k_{3}k_{4},k_{3}k_{4}k_{1}k_{2})

\displaystyle=2\cos\big[(t-s)\Delta E(\vec{k})\big]|\Phi(\vec{k})|^{2}\big(\delta(p-k_{1})+\delta(p-k_{2})-\delta(p-k_{3})-\delta(p-k_{4})\big)\ .

We plug this result back in (
6.1.2
) to find that
after a change of variables
(k_{1}k_{2})\mapsto(k_{3}k_{4})
,

(6.27)

\displaystyle\nu\big([[a_{p}^{*}a_{p},

\displaystyle V_{F}(t)],V_{F}(s)]\big)

\displaystyle=4\int_{\Lambda^{*}}\big(\delta(p-k_{1})+\delta(p-k_{2})-\delta(p-k_{3})-\delta(p-k_{4})\big)\ |\Phi(\vec{k})|^{2}

\displaystyle\times\cos\big[(t-s)\Delta E(\vec{k})\big]\Big(f_{0}(k_{1})f_{0}(k_{2})\tilde{f}_{0}(k_{3})\tilde{f}_{0}(k_{4})-f_{0}(k_{3})f_{0}(k_{4})\tilde{f}_{0}(k_{1})\tilde{f}_{0}(k_{2})\Big)\ .

Finally, we integrate against time and a test function
\varphi(p)

to find that

(6.28)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{t_{1}}\nu\big([[N(\varphi),V_{F}(t_{1})],V_{F}(t_{2})]\big)\mathrm{d}t_{1}\mathrm{d}t_{2}=-t|\Lambda|\int_{\Lambda^{*}}\varphi(p)Q_{t}[f_{0}](p)\mathrm{d}p\

where
Q_{t}[f_{0}]

is the expression given by

(6.29)

\displaystyle Q_{t}[f_{0}](p)

\displaystyle=4\pi\int_{\Lambda^{*4}}\,\frac{|\Phi(\vec{k})|^{2}}{|\Lambda|}\,\Big[\delta(p-k_{1})+\delta(p-k_{2})-\delta(p-k_{3})-\delta(p-k_{4})\Big]

\displaystyle\times\delta_{t}[\Delta E(\vec{k})]\Big(f(k_{3})f(k_{4})\tilde{f}(k_{1})\tilde{f}(k_{2})-f(k_{1})f(k_{2})\tilde{f}(k_{3})\tilde{f}(k_{4})\Big)\mathrm{d}\vec{k}\ .

where
we recall

\delta_{1}(x)=\frac{2}{\pi}\frac{\sin^{2}(x/2)}{x^{2}}

and

\delta_{t}(x)=t\delta_{1}(tx).

Upon expanding
\Phi=\Phi^{(1)}+\Phi^{(2)}
in the above expression
with respect to the decomposition found in Lemma
6.3
,
one may check that the formula is in agreement with the operator
Q_{t}
, as given by Def.
3
. This finishes the proof of the lemma.
∎

6.2.
Proof of Lemma
6.2

Proof.

Let
\varphi\in\ell^{1}
and
t,s\in\mathbb{R}
,
let us introduce the following notation for the fermion-fermion double commutator

\displaystyle C_{F}(\varphi,t,s)

\displaystyle:=[[N(\varphi),V_{F}(t)],V_{F}(s)]

(6.30)

\displaystyle=\int_{\Lambda^{*2}}\hat{V}(k)\hat{V}(\ell)\Big[\Big[N(\varphi),D_{k}^{*}(t)D_{k}(t)\Big],D_{\ell}^{*}(s)D_{\ell}(s)\Big]\ \mathrm{d}k\mathrm{d}\ell

where we have written
V_{F}(t)

in terms of
D
-operators, see (
5.5
).
For simplicity, we shall assume that
\varphi
is real-valued so that
C_{F}(\varphi,t,s)
is self-adjoint (in the general case, one may decompose
\varphi=\mathrm{Re}\varphi+\mathrm{i}\mathrm{Im}\varphi
and apply linearity of the commutator).
We claim that there exists a constant
C>0
such that

(6.31)

\|C_{F}(\varphi,t,s)\Psi\|\leqslant C\|\hat{V}\|_{\ell^{1}}^{2}|\Lambda|\|\varphi\|_{\ell^{1}}\|\mathcal{N}^{2}\Psi\|\ ,

for all
\Psi\in\mathscr{F}
.
To see this,
we shall expand the
double commutator of the right hand side of (
6.30
) into eight terms.
In order to ease the notation, we shall
drop the time labels
t,s\in\mathbb{R}
. Since our estimates are uniform in time, there is no risk in doing so.
In terms of the contraction operators

D_{k}^{*}(\varphi)\equiv[N(\varphi),D_{k}^{*}]

and

D_{k}(\varphi)\equiv[N(\varphi),D_{k}]

we find

\displaystyle\Big[\Big[N(\varphi),D_{k}^{*}D_{k}\Big],D_{\ell}^{*}D_{\ell}\Big]

\displaystyle\ =\ D_{k}^{*}(\varphi)\Big[D_{k},D_{\ell}^{*}\Big]D_{\ell}\ +\ D_{k}^{*}(\varphi)D_{\ell}^{*}\Big[D_{k},D_{\ell}\Big]

\displaystyle\ +\ \Big[D_{k}^{*}(\varphi),D_{\ell}^{*}\Big]D_{\ell}D_{k}\ +\ D_{\ell}^{*}\Big[D_{k}^{*}(\varphi),D_{\ell}\Big]D_{k}

\displaystyle\ +\ D_{k}^{*}D_{\ell}^{*}\Big[D_{k}(\varphi),D_{\ell}\Big]\ +\ D_{k}^{*}\Big[D_{k}(\varphi),D_{\ell}^{*}\Big]D_{\ell}

(6.32)

\displaystyle\ +\ D_{\ell}^{*}\Big[D_{k}^{*},D_{\ell}\Big]D_{k}(\varphi)\ +\ \Big[D_{k}^{*},D_{\ell}^{*}\Big]D_{\ell}D_{k}(\varphi)\ .

All these operators
can be controlled
using the Type-I and Type-IV estimates, found in Lemma
4.5

and Lemma
4.8
, respectively,
together with the commutator identities

[D_{k},\mathcal{N}]=[D_{k}(\varphi),\mathcal{N}]=0
, see Lemma
4.3
.
For instance, given
\Psi\in\mathscr{F}

the first term can be estimated as follows

\displaystyle\|D_{k}^{*}(\varphi)\big[D_{k},D_{\ell}^{*}\big]D_{\ell}\Psi\|

\displaystyle\,\leqslant\,\|D_{k}^{*}(\varphi)\|\|\big[D_{k},D_{\ell}^{*}\big]D_{\ell}\Psi\|

\displaystyle\,\leqslant\,C\|\varphi\|_{\ell^{1}}|\Lambda|\|\mathcal{N}D_{\ell}\Psi\|

\displaystyle\,=\,C\|\varphi\|_{\ell^{1}}|\Lambda|\|D_{\ell}\mathcal{N}\Psi\|

(6.33)

\displaystyle\,\leqslant\,C\|\varphi\|_{\ell^{1}}|\Lambda|\|\mathcal{N}^{2}\Psi\|

for a constant
C>0
.
Every other term in the expansion (
6.32
)
can be analyzed in the same fashion, and satisfy the same bound –we leave the details to the reader.
Thus, we plug the estimate (
6.33
)
back in the expansion (
6.32
)
and integrate over
k,\ell\in\Lambda^{*}
.
One then obtains
(
6.31
).

Let us now estimate the integral reminder term, we fix
0\leqslant t_{3}\leqslant t_{2}\leqslant t_{1}
.
As a first step, since
C_{F}
and
\mathfrak{h}_{I}
are self-adjoint,
we use the following rough upper bound

(6.34)

\displaystyle\nu_{t_{3}}\big(

\displaystyle[[[N(\varphi),V_{F}(t_{1})]V_{F}(t_{2})],\mathfrak{h}_{I}(t_{3})]\big)\leqslant 2\nu_{t_{3}}\Big(C_{F}(\varphi,t_{1},t_{2})^{2}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\nu_{t_{3}}\Big(\mathfrak{h}_{I}(t_{3})^{2}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}

In view of Remark
5.1
, we can turn the estimate (
6.31
) into the upper bound

(6.35)

\nu_{t_{3}}\Big(C_{F}(\varphi,t_{1},t_{2})^{2}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\leqslant C\|\hat{V}\|_{\ell^{1}}^{2}|\Lambda|\|\varphi\|_{\ell^{1}}\nu_{t_{3}}\big(\mathcal{N}^{4}\big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\ .

On the other hand, using the operator norm estimates (
4.20
),
a simple but rough estimate
for the interaction Hamiltonian is found to be

\displaystyle\|\mathfrak{h}_{I}(t)\Psi\|

\displaystyle\ \leqslant\ \lambda\|V_{F}(t)\Psi\|+\lambda\|V_{FB}(t)\Psi\|+\lambda\|V_{BB}(t)\Psi\|

\displaystyle\ \lesssim\ \lambda\|\hat{V}\|_{\ell^{1}}\|\mathcal{N}^{2}\Psi\|+\lambda\|\hat{V}\|_{\ell^{1}}R\|\mathcal{N}\Psi\|+\lambda\|\hat{V}\|_{\ell^{1}}R^{2}\|\Psi\|

\displaystyle\ \lesssim\ \lambda\|\hat{V}\|_{\ell^{1}}\big(\|\mathcal{N}^{2}\Psi\|+R^{2}\|\Psi\|\big)

where we recall that
R=|\Lambda|p_{F}^{d-1}
.
Consequently, in view of Remark
5.1
we find that

(6.36)

\nu_{t_{3}}\Big(\mathfrak{h}_{I}(t_{3})^{2}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\leqslant C\lambda\|\hat{V}\|_{\ell^{1}}^{2}\Big(\nu_{t_{3}}\big(\mathcal{N}^{4}\big)^{\frac{1}{2}}+R^{2}\Big)

where we used the fact that
\nu_{t}(\mathds{1})=1
for all
t\in\mathbb{R}.

The proof of the lemma is now finished once we combine
Eqs. (
6.34
), (
6.35
) and (
6.36
),
and integrate over the time variables
0\leqslant t_{3}\leqslant t_{2}\leqslant t_{1}\leqslant t
.
∎

7.
Leading Order Terms II: Emergence of
B

The main purpose of this section
is to analyze the term
T_{FB,FB}(t)

found in the double commutator expansion (
3.20
), introduced in Section
3
.
In particular, we show that this term gives rise to the operator
B_{t}
, as given in Def.
2
,
corresponding to the second leading order term describing the dynamics of
f_{t}(p).

It describes interactions between particles/holes
as mediated by

virtual bosons
around the Fermi surface.
This is manifest in the fact that, as we shall see,
it contains the
propagator
of free bosons

(7.1)

G_{k}(t-s)\equiv\left\langle\Omega,[b_{k}(t),b_{k}^{*}(s)]\Omega\right\rangle_{\mathscr{F}}

defined for
k\in\Lambda^{*}
, and
t,s\in\mathbb{R}
.

We state the main result of this section in the following proposition,
which we prove in the remainder of the section.

Proposition 7.1

(Analysis of
T_{FB,FB}
)
.

Let
T_{FB,FB}(t,p)
be the quantity defined in Eq. (
3.21
) for
\alpha=\beta=FB
,
and let
m>0
.
Then, there exists a constant
C>0

such that for all
\varphi\in\ell_{m}^{1}
and
t\geqslant 0

the following inequality holds true

\displaystyle\big|T_{FB,FB}

\displaystyle(t,\varphi)+|\Lambda|t\left\langle\varphi,B_{t}[f_{0}]\right\rangle\big|

\displaystyle\leqslant C|\Lambda|t^{2}\|\varphi\|_{\ell^{1}}\|\hat{V}\|_{\ell_{1}}^{2}\sup_{\tau\leqslant t}\Big(R^{\frac{1}{2}}\nu_{\tau}(\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{S}})^{\frac{1}{2}}\nu_{\tau}(\mathcal{N})^{\frac{1}{2}}+CR^{\frac{3}{2}}\nu_{\tau}(\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{S}})^{\frac{1}{2}}+Rp_{F}^{-m}\nu_{\tau}(\mathcal{N}_{1}^{2})\Big)

(7.2)

\displaystyle\,+\,|\Lambda|t^{3}\lambda R\|\varphi\|_{\ell^{1}}\|\hat{V}\|_{\ell_{1}}^{3}\sup_{\tau\leqslant t}\Big(R^{\frac{3}{2}}\nu_{\tau}(\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{S}})^{\frac{1}{2}}+R\nu_{\tau}(\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{S}})+Rp_{F}^{-m}\nu_{\tau}(\mathcal{N})^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big)

where
T_{FB,FB}(t,\varphi)\equiv\left\langle\varphi,T_{FB,FB}(t)\right\rangle
and
B_{t}
is given in Def.
2
.

Remark 7.1
.

In order to prove Proposition
7.1
, we expand

T_{FB,FB}

into several terms and analyze each one separately.
This expansion is based in the following two observations:

(i) For any self-adjoint operators
N,T,S
and state
\mu
,
there holds:

(7.3)

\mu\big([[N,T+T^{*}],S]\big)=2\mathrm{Re}\,\mu\big([[N,T],S]\big)\ .

(ii) Thanks to the symmetries

D_{k}=D^{*}_{-k}
,

\hat{V}(-k)=\hat{V}(k)

and the vanishing commutator
[D^{*}_{k},b_{k}]=0
,
starting from the representation (
5.6
)
we may re-write the fermion-boson interaction term
as

(7.4)

V_{FB}(t)=\int_{\Lambda^{*}}\hat{V}(k)B^{*}_{k}(t)D_{k}(t)\mathrm{d}k\qquad\textnormal{where}\qquad B^{*}_{k}(t)\equiv b^{*}_{k}(t)+b_{-k}(t)\ .

Starting from (
3.21
), based on these two observations we are able to re-write
the term
T_{FB,FB}

for all
t\in\mathbb{R}

and
\varphi\in\ell^{1}
in the following form

\displaystyle T_{FB,FB}

\displaystyle(t,\varphi)

\displaystyle=2\mathrm{Re}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{t_{1}}\int_{\Lambda^{*2}}\hat{V}(k)\hat{V}(\ell)\nu_{t_{2}}\Big([[N(\varphi),D_{k}^{*}(t_{1})b_{k}(t_{1})],B^{*}_{\ell}(t_{2})D_{\ell}(t_{2})]\Big)\mathrm{d}t_{1}\mathrm{d}t_{2}\mathrm{d}k\mathrm{d}\ell

(7.5)

\displaystyle\equiv M(t,\varphi)+R^{(1)}(t,\varphi)+R^{(2)}(t,\varphi)+R^{(3)}(t,\varphi)+R^{(4)}(t,\varphi)

where in the second line we have expanded the commutator into five terms.
The first one we shall refer to as the
main term
, and is defined as follows

(7.6)

\displaystyle M(t,\varphi)

\displaystyle=2\mathrm{Re}\,\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{t_{1}}\int_{\Lambda^{*2}}\hat{V}(k)\hat{V}(\ell)\nu_{t_{2}}\Big(D_{k}^{*}(t_{1},\varphi)[b_{k}(t_{1}),b^{*}_{\ell}(t_{2})]D_{\ell}(t_{2})\Big)\,\mathrm{d}t_{1}\mathrm{d}t_{2}\mathrm{d}k\mathrm{d}\ell\ .

The last four, which we shall refer to as the
remainder terms
, are defined as follows

\displaystyle R^{(1)}(t,\varphi)

\displaystyle=2\mathrm{Re}\,\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{t_{1}}\int_{\Lambda^{*2}}\hat{V}(k)\hat{V}(\ell)\nu_{t_{2}}\Big(D_{k}^{*}(t_{1},\varphi)B^{*}_{\ell}(t_{2})[b_{k}(t_{1}),D_{\ell}(t_{2})]\Big)\,\mathrm{d}t_{1}\mathrm{d}t_{2}\mathrm{d}k\mathrm{d}\ell

\displaystyle R^{(2)}(t,\varphi)

\displaystyle=2\mathrm{Re}\,\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{t_{1}}\int_{\Lambda^{*2}}\hat{V}(k)\hat{V}(\ell)\nu_{t_{2}}\Big([D_{k}^{*}(t_{1},\varphi),B^{*}_{\ell}(t_{2})]D_{\ell}(t_{2})b_{k}(t_{1})\Big)\,\mathrm{d}t_{1}\mathrm{d}t_{2}\mathrm{d}k\mathrm{d}\ell

\displaystyle R^{(3)}(t,\varphi)

\displaystyle=2\mathrm{Re}\,\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{t_{1}}\int_{\Lambda^{*2}}\hat{V}(k)\hat{V}(\ell)\nu_{t_{2}}\Big(B_{\ell}^{*}(t_{2})[D_{k}(t_{1},\varphi),D_{\ell}(t_{2})]b_{k}(t_{1})\Big)\,\mathrm{d}t_{1}\mathrm{d}t_{2}\mathrm{d}k\mathrm{d}\ell

(7.7)

\displaystyle R^{(4)}(t,\varphi)

\displaystyle=2\mathrm{Re}\,\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{t_{1}}\int_{\Lambda^{*2}}\hat{V}(k)\hat{V}(\ell)\nu_{t_{2}}\Big([D_{k}(t_{1})b_{k}(t_{1},\varphi),B_{\ell}^{*}(t_{2})D_{\ell}(t_{2})]\Big)\,\mathrm{d}t_{1}\mathrm{d}t_{2}\mathrm{d}k\mathrm{d}\ell\ .

Remark 7.2
.

We remind
the reader that we have previously introduced the notation

(7.8)

D_{k}^{*}(t,\varphi)=[N(\varphi),D_{k}^{*}(t)]\quad\textnormal{and}\quad b_{k}(t,\varphi)=[N(\varphi),b_{k}(t)]

for any
k\in\Lambda^{*}
and
t\in\mathbb{R}
. We have also used the fact that
[b_{k}(t),b_{\ell}(s)]=0
.

In the remainder of this section, we shall study these five terms separately.
The proof of Proposition
7.1
follows directly from the following two lemmas.
Here, we remind the reader that
R=|\Lambda|p_{F}^{d-1}
is our recurring parameter.

Lemma 7.1

(The main term)
.

Let
M
be the quantity defined in (
7.6
), and let
m>0
.
Then, there exists a constant

C>0

such that for all
\varphi\in\ell_{m}^{1}

and
t\geqslant 0
the following estimate holds true

(7.9)

\displaystyle|M(t,\varphi)+|\Lambda|t

\displaystyle\langle\varphi,B_{t}[f_{0}]\rangle|

\displaystyle\leqslant Ct^{2}\|\hat{V}\|_{\ell^{1}}^{2}|\Lambda|\|\varphi\|_{\ell^{1}_{m}}\sup_{\tau\leqslant t}\Big(R^{\frac{1}{2}}\nu_{\tau}(\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{S}})^{\frac{1}{2}}+p_{F}^{-m}\Big)\nu_{\tau}(\mathcal{N}^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle+C\lambda t^{3}R|\Lambda|\|\varphi\|_{\ell^{1}_{m}}\|\hat{V}\|_{\ell^{1}}^{3}\sup_{0\leqslant\tau\leqslant t}\Big(R^{\frac{3}{2}}\nu_{\tau}(\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{S}})^{\frac{1}{2}}+R\nu_{\tau}(\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{S}})+Rp_{F}^{-m}\nu_{\tau}(\mathcal{N})^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big)

where the operator
B_{t}
was introduced in Def.
2
.

Lemma 7.2

(The remainder terms)
.

Let
R^{(1)}
,
R^{(2)}
,
R^{(3)}
and
R^{(4)}

be the quantities defined in (
7.7
), and let
m>0.

Then, there exists a constant
C>0
such that for all
\varphi\in\ell_{m}^{1}
and
t\geqslant 0

(1) There holds

(7.10)

|R^{(1)}(t,\varphi)|\leqslant Ct^{2}\|\hat{V}\|_{\ell^{1}}^{2}\|\varphi\|_{\ell^{1}}|\Lambda|R^{\frac{3}{2}}\sup_{0\leqslant t\leqslant\tau}\nu_{\tau}(\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{S}})^{\frac{1}{2}}\ .

(2) There holds

(7.11)

\displaystyle|R^{(2)}(t,\varphi)|\leqslant Ct^{2}\|\hat{V}\|_{\ell^{1}}^{2}\|\varphi\|_{\ell^{1}_{m}}|\Lambda|\frac{R}{p_{F}^{m}}\sup_{0\leqslant t\leqslant\tau}\nu_{\tau}(\mathcal{N}^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}\ .

(3) There holds

(7.12)

|R^{(3)}(t,\varphi)|\leqslant Ct^{2}\|\hat{V}\|_{\ell^{1}}^{2}\|\varphi\|_{\ell^{1}}|\Lambda|R^{\frac{3}{2}}\sup_{0\leqslant t\leqslant\tau}\nu_{\tau}(\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{S}})^{\frac{1}{2}}\ .

(4) There holds

(7.13)

|R^{(4)}(t,\varphi)|\leqslant Ct^{2}\|\hat{V}\|_{\ell^{1}}^{2}\|\varphi\|_{\ell^{1}_{m}}|\Lambda|\frac{R}{p_{F}^{m}}\sup_{0\leqslant t\leqslant\tau}\nu_{\tau}(\mathcal{N}^{2})\ .

Proof of Proposition
7.1
.

Straightforward combination of the expansion given in Eq. (
7.5
),
and the estimates contained in Lemmas
7.1
and
7.2
.
∎

We dedicate the rest of the section to the proof of Lemma
7.1
and
7.2
, respectively.
This is done in the two following subsections.

7.1.
Analysis of the main term

The main goal of this subsection is to prove Lemma
7.1

by analyzing the main term
M
.
Our first step in this direction is to give an additional
decomposition of
M
.
Indeed, we start by noting that the commutator
of the bosonic operators may be written as (see (
4.1
) in Section
4
)

(7.14)

\displaystyle[b_{k}(t),b_{\ell}^{*}(s)]

\displaystyle=\ \delta(k-\ell)G_{k}(t-s)\mathds{1}-\mathcal{R}_{k,\ell}(t,s)\ ,

which corresponds to a decomposition into its “diagonal” and “off-diagonal” parts,
with respect to the variables
k,\ell\in\Lambda^{*}
.
Here,
G_{k}(t-s)
is a scalar that
corresponds to the
propagator

of the boson field–it can be explicitly calculcated to be

(7.15)

G_{k}(t-s)=\left\langle\Omega,[b_{k}(t),b_{k}^{*}(s)],\Omega\right\rangle_{\mathscr{F}}=\int_{\Lambda^{*}}\chi^{\perp}(p)\chi(p-k)e^{-i(t-s)(E_{p}+E_{p-k})}\mathrm{d}p\ .

for all
k\in\Lambda^{*}
and
t,s\in\mathbb{R}
.
On the other hand, the second term of (
7.14
) corresponds to an operator remainder term

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{k,\ell}(t,s)

\displaystyle\equiv\int_{\Lambda^{*}}\chi^{\perp}(p)\chi^{\perp}(p+\ell-k)\chi(p-k)e^{-i(t-s)E_{p-k}}a_{p}^{*}(t)a_{p+\ell-k}(s)\,\mathrm{d}p

(7.16)

\displaystyle+\int_{\Lambda^{*}}\chi(h)\chi(h+\ell-k)\chi^{\perp}(h+\ell)e^{-i(t-s)E_{h+k}}a_{h}^{*}(t)a_{h+\ell-k}(s)\,\mathrm{d}h\ .

The decomposition of the bosonic commutator given in (
7.14
)
now suggests that we split the main term into two parts.
The first one contains the
\delta(k-\ell)
function,
and the
second one contains the operator
\mathcal{R}_{k,\ell}
.
In other words, we shall consider

(7.17)

\displaystyle M(t,\varphi)=M^{\delta}(t,\varphi)+M^{\mathcal{R}}(t,\varphi)\ .

We shall analyze
M^{\delta}
and
M^{\mathcal{R}}
separately in the two next subsubsections.
The proof of Lemma
7.1
is given in the third subsubsection.

7.1.1.
Analysis of
M^{\delta}

Upon expanding the bosonic commutator (
7.14
) in (
7.6
), we evaluate the
\delta(k-\ell)
function to find that

(7.18)

\displaystyle M^{\delta}(t,\varphi)

\displaystyle=2\mathrm{Re}\,\int_{\Lambda^{*}}|\hat{V}(k)|^{2}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{t_{1}}G_{k}(t_{1}-t_{2})\nu_{t_{2}}\Big(D_{k}^{*}(t_{1},\varphi)D_{k}(t_{2})\Big)\,\mathrm{d}t_{1}\mathrm{d}t_{2}\mathrm{d}k\ .

In order to analyze the above expectation value,
we shall expand
\nu_{t_{2}}

with respect to the interaction dynamics (
3.14
).
Namely, we consider

(7.19)

\displaystyle M^{\delta}=M^{\delta}_{0}+M^{\delta}_{1}

where for all
t\in\mathbb{R}
and
\varphi\in\ell^{1}

we define

(7.20)

\displaystyle M^{\delta}_{0}(t,\varphi)

\displaystyle\equiv 2\mathrm{Re}\,\int_{\Lambda^{*2}}|\hat{V}(k)|^{2}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{t_{1}}G_{k}(t_{1}-t_{2})\nu\big(D_{k}^{*}(t_{1},\varphi)D_{k}(t_{2})\big)\,\mathrm{d}t_{1}\mathrm{d}t_{2}\mathrm{d}k

together with

(7.21)

\displaystyle M^{\delta}_{1}

\displaystyle(t,\varphi)

\displaystyle\equiv 2\mathrm{Im}\int_{\Lambda^{*}}|\hat{V}(k)|^{2}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{t_{1}}\int_{0}^{t_{2}}G_{k}(t_{1}-t_{2})\nu_{t_{3}}\big([D_{k}^{*}(t_{1},\varphi)D_{k}(t_{2}),\mathfrak{h}_{I}(t_{3})]\big)\mathrm{d}t_{1}\mathrm{d}t_{2}\mathrm{d}t_{3}\mathrm{d}k\ .

First, we identify that from the first term in the above expansion will the
B_{t}
operator emerge. Namely, we claim that

Claim 1
.

For all
t\in\mathbb{R}
and real-valued
\varphi\in\ell^{1}
, the following identity holds true

(7.22)

\displaystyle M_{0}^{\delta}(t,\varphi)=-t\left\langle\varphi,B_{t}[f_{0}]\right\rangle

where
B_{t}
is the operator given in Def.
2
.

Once this is established, it suffices to control the second term in the expansion of
M^{\delta}
, that is,
the extra integral reminder term in (
7.19
),
M_{1}^{\delta}
.

Claim 2
.

For all
m>0
there exists a constant
C>0

such that for all
t\geqslant 0
and
\varphi\in\ell^{1}
the following estimate holds true

(7.23)

\displaystyle|M^{\delta}_{1}(t,\varphi)|\leqslant C\lambda t^{3}R|\Lambda|\|\varphi\|_{\ell^{1}_{m}}\|\hat{V}\|_{\ell^{1}}\|\hat{V}\|_{\ell^{2}}^{2}\sup_{0\leqslant\tau\leqslant t}\Big[R^{\frac{3}{2}}\nu_{\tau}(\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{S}})^{\frac{1}{2}}+R\nu_{\tau}(\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{S}})+\frac{R}{p_{F}}\nu_{\tau}(\mathcal{N})^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big]\ .

The proof of the above claims are given as follows.

Proof of Claim 1.

Let us fix
k\in\Lambda^{*}
,
t,s\in\mathbb{R}
and
\varphi\in\ell^{1}
, which we assume is real-valued in the reminder of the proof.
In order to prove our claim, we write

\displaystyle D_{k}^{*}(t,\varphi)

\displaystyle=\int_{\Lambda^{*}}\chi^{\perp}(p_{1},p_{1}-k)[\varphi(p_{1})-\varphi(p_{1}-k)]a^{*}_{p_{1}}(t)a_{p_{1}-k}(t)\mathrm{d}p_{1}

(7.24)

\displaystyle-\int_{\Lambda^{*}}\chi(h_{1},h_{1}+k)[\varphi(h_{1})-\varphi(h_{1}+k)]a^{*}_{h_{1}}(t)a_{h_{1}+k}(t)\mathrm{d}h_{1}\ ,

\displaystyle D_{k}(s)

\displaystyle=\int_{\Lambda^{*}}\chi^{\perp}(p_{2},p_{2}+k)a^{*}_{p_{2}}(s)a_{p_{2}+k}(s)\mathrm{d}p_{2}

(7.25)

\displaystyle-\int_{\Lambda^{*}}\chi(h_{2},h_{2}-k)a^{*}_{h_{2}}(s)a_{h_{2}-k}(s)\mathrm{d}h_{2}\ .

Thus we are able to calculate that the following four terms arise

\displaystyle\nu\big(D_{k}^{*}(t,\varphi)D_{k}(s)\big)

\displaystyle=\int_{\Lambda^{*2}}\chi^{\perp}(p_{1},p_{2},p_{1}-k,p_{2}+k)[\varphi(p_{1})-\varphi(p_{1}-k)]

\displaystyle\qquad\times\ \nu\Big(a_{p_{1}}^{*}(t)a_{p_{1}-k}(t)a_{p_{2}}^{*}(s)a_{p_{2}+k}(s)\Big)\mathrm{d}p_{1}\mathrm{d}p_{2}

\displaystyle+\int_{\Lambda^{*2}}\chi(h_{1},h_{2},h_{1}+k,h_{2}-k)[\varphi(h_{1})-\varphi(h_{1}+k)]

\displaystyle\qquad\times\ \nu\Big(a_{h_{1}}^{*}(t)a_{h_{1}+k}(t)a_{h_{2}}^{*}(s)a_{h_{2}-k}(s)\Big)\mathrm{d}h_{1}\mathrm{d}h_{2}

\displaystyle-\int_{\Lambda^{*2}}\chi^{\perp}(p_{1},p_{1}-k)\chi(h_{2},h_{2}-k)[\varphi(p_{1})-\varphi(p_{1}-k)]

\displaystyle\qquad\times\ \nu\Big(a_{p_{1}}^{*}(t)a_{p_{1}-k}(t)a_{h_{2}}^{*}(s)a_{h_{2}-k}(s)\Big)\mathrm{d}p_{1}\mathrm{d}h_{2}

\displaystyle-\int_{\Lambda^{*2}}\chi(h_{1},h_{1}+k)\chi^{\perp}(p_{2},p_{2}+k)[\varphi(h_{1})-\varphi(h_{1}+k)]

(7.26)

\displaystyle\qquad\times\ \nu\Big(a_{h_{1}}^{*}(t)a_{h_{1}+k}(t)a_{p_{2}}^{*}(s)a_{p_{2}+k}(s)\Big)\mathrm{d}h_{1}\mathrm{d}p_{2}\ .

In order to calculate the four terms displayed in the right hand side of (
7.26
) we use the fact
that
\nu
is translation invariant and quasi-free.
In particular,
it is possible to calculate that
for any

p_{1},p_{2},q_{1},q_{2}\in\Lambda^{*}

the following relation hold true

\displaystyle\nu\Big(a^{*}_{p_{1}}(t)a_{q_{1}}(t)a^{*}_{p_{2}}(s)a_{q_{2}}(s)\Big)

\displaystyle=\delta(q_{1}-p_{1})\delta(q_{2}-p_{2})f_{0}(p_{1})f_{0}(p_{2})

(7.27)

\displaystyle+\delta(q_{1}-p_{2})\delta(q_{2}-p_{1})e^{i(t-s)(E_{p_{1}}-E_{p_{2}})}f_{0}(p_{1})\widetilde{f}_{0}(p_{2})\ .

We note that this implies that the third and fourth term in (
7.26
) are zero.
Indeed, for the third term we choose in (
7.27
)

p_{1}=p_{1}
,
q_{1}=p_{1}-k
,
p_{2}=h_{2}
and
q_{2}=h_{2}-k
to find that

\displaystyle\nu\Big(a^{*}_{p_{1}}(t)a_{p_{1}-k}(t)a^{*}_{h_{2}}(s)a_{h_{2}-k}(s)\Big)

\displaystyle=|\Lambda|\delta(k)f_{0}(p_{1})f_{0}(h_{2})

(7.28)

\displaystyle+|\Lambda|\delta(k)\delta(p_{1}-h_{2})e^{i(t-s)(E_{p_{1}}-E_{h_{2}})}f_{0}(p_{1})\widetilde{f}_{0}(h_{2}).

It suffices to note that the right hand side is proportional to
\delta(k)
, and that
[\varphi(p_{1})-\varphi(p_{1}-k)]\delta(k)=0
.
This shows that the third term has a null contribution –the same analysis holds for the fourth term in (
7.26
).

In a similar fashion, the first and second term in (
7.26
) can be collected and re-written thanks to (
7.27
) to find that

(7.29)

\displaystyle\nu\big(D_{k}^{*}

\displaystyle(t,\varphi)D_{k}(s)\big)

\displaystyle=|\Lambda|\int_{\Lambda^{*}}\chi^{\perp}(p,p-k)[\varphi(p)-\varphi(p-k)]e^{i(t-s)(E_{p}-E_{p-k})}\ f_{0}(p)\widetilde{f_{0}}(p-k)\mathrm{d}p

\displaystyle+|\Lambda|\int_{\Lambda^{*}}\chi(h,h+k)[\varphi(h)-\varphi(h+k)]e^{i(t-s)(E_{h}-E_{h+k})}\ f_{0}(h)\widetilde{f_{0}}(h+k)\mathrm{d}h

where we have dropped all terms in (
7.27
) containing
\delta(k)
.
Now, we integrate in time the above equation to find that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{t_{1}}\nu_{t_{2}}\Big(G_{k}(t_{1}-t_{2})

\displaystyle D_{k}^{*}(t_{1},\varphi)D_{k}(t_{2})\Big)\mathrm{d}t_{2}\mathrm{d}t_{1}

\displaystyle=|\Lambda|\int_{\Lambda^{*}}\chi^{\perp}(p,p-k)\Bigg(\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{t_{1}}G_{k}(t_{2})e^{it_{2}(E_{p}-E_{p-k})}\mathrm{d}t_{2}\mathrm{d}t_{1}\Bigg)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\times[\varphi(p)-\varphi(p-k)]f_{0}(p)\widetilde{f}_{0}(p-k)\mathrm{d}p

\displaystyle+|\Lambda|\int_{\Lambda^{*}}\chi(h,h+k)\Bigg(\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{t_{1}}G_{k}(t_{2})e^{it_{2}(E_{h}-E_{h+k})}\mathrm{d}t_{2}\mathrm{d}t_{1}\Bigg)

(7.30)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\times[\varphi(h)-\varphi(h+k)]f_{0}(h)\widetilde{f}_{0}(h+k)\mathrm{d}h

To finalize the proof, let us identify the right hand side of the last displayed equation, with the operator
B_{t}
as given by Def.
2
. Indeed, consider the second term of Eq. (
7.30
).
We may calculate explictly the integrals with respect to time as follows.
First, we rewrite
G_{k}(t)
in terms of the variables
r=p-k

(7.31)

G_{k}(t-s)=\int_{\Lambda^{*}}\chi(r)\chi^{\perp}(r+k)e^{-i(t-s)(E_{r}+E_{r+k})}\mathrm{d}r\ .

Let
h\in\mathcal{B}\cap\mathcal{B}-k
. After integration in time and taking the real part
we find

\displaystyle 2\mathrm{Re}\,\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{t_{1}}G_{k}(t_{2})

\displaystyle e^{it_{2}(E_{h}-E_{h+k})}\mathrm{d}t_{2}\mathrm{d}t_{1}

\displaystyle=\int_{\Lambda^{*}}\chi(r)\chi^{\perp}(r+k)\ 2\mathrm{Re}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{t_{1}}e^{it_{2}(E_{h}-E_{h+k}-E_{r}-E_{r+k})}\mathrm{d}t_{2}\mathrm{d}t_{1}\mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle=\int_{\Lambda^{*}}\chi(r)\chi^{\perp}(r+k)\,2\pi t\delta_{t}\,\big(E_{h}-E_{h+k}-E_{r}-E_{r+k}\big)\mathrm{d}r

(7.32)

\displaystyle=2\pi t\,\alpha^{H}_{t}(h,k)\ .

Here,
\delta_{t}(x)

corresponds to the mollified Delta function defined as

\delta_{t}(x)=t\delta_{1}(tx)

where

\delta_{1}(x)=\frac{2}{\pi}\sin^{2}(x/2)/x^{2}.

On the other hand,
\alpha^{H}_{t}

corresonds to the object defining
B_{t}
, see (
2.24
) in Def.
2
.
A similar calculation shows that the first term of the right hand side of Eq. (
7.30
)
can be put in the following form

\displaystyle\chi^{\perp}(p,p-k)2\mathrm{Re}\,\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{t_{1}}G_{k}(t_{2})e^{it_{2}(E_{p}-E_{p-k})}\mathrm{d}t_{2}\mathrm{d}t_{1}=2\pi t\,\alpha^{P}_{t}(p,k)

where
\alpha_{P}
is the quantity given in (
2.25
), see Def.
2
.
We integrate against
|\hat{V}(k)|^{2}
and change variables
h\mapsto h-k,\ p\mapsto p+k

in the “gain term”
of (
7.30
)
to find that

\displaystyle 2\mathrm{Re}\,\int_{\Lambda^{*2}}|\hat{V}(k)|^{2}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{t_{1}}\nu\Big(G_{k}(t_{1}-t_{2})D_{k}^{*}(t_{1},\varphi)D_{k}(t_{2})\Big)\,\mathrm{d}t_{1}\mathrm{d}t_{2}\mathrm{d}k=-t\left\langle\varphi,B_{t}[f_{0}]\right\rangle

where
B_{t}
is the operator given in Eq. (
2.23
). This finishes the proof.
∎

Proof of Claim 2.

Let us fix throughout the proof the time label
t\in\mathbb{R}
, the parameter
m>0

and the test function
\varphi\in\ell^{1}
.
Based on the fact that
\|G_{k}(\tau)\|_{B(\mathscr{F})}\lesssim R
for all
k\in\Lambda^{*}
and
\tau\in\mathbb{R}
,
our starting point is the following elementary inequality

(7.33)

\displaystyle|M^{\delta}_{1}(t,\varphi)|\,\lesssim\,R\,\|\hat{V}\|_{\ell_{2}}^{2}\,t^{3}\sup_{k\in\textnormal{supp}\hat{V},t_{i}\in[0,t]}\Big|\nu_{t_{3}}\Big(\Big[\,D_{k}^{*}(t_{1},\varphi)D_{k}(t_{2})\,,\,\mathfrak{h}_{I}(t_{3})\,\Big]\Big)\Big|\ .

Thus, it suffices to estimate the
\sup

quantity in Eq. (
7.33
).
For notational convenience
we do not write explictly the time variables
t_{i}\in[0,t]
for
i=1,2,3
–since our estimates are uniform in these variables, there is no risk in doing so.
In addition, we shall only
give estimates for pure states
\left\langle\Psi,\ \cdot\ \Psi\right\rangle

and then apply Remark
5.1

to conclude estimates for the mixed state
\nu
.
Finally, we shall extensively use the results contained in Section
4
–that is, the estimates of Type-I, Type-II, Type-III and Type-IV,
contained in Lemma
4.5
,
4.6
,
4.7
and
4.8
, respectively,
together with the several commutation relations.

Let us fix
k\in\textnormal{supp}\hat{V}
. We begin by expanding the commutator in (
7.33
) as follows

\displaystyle\nu\big([D_{k}^{*}(\varphi)

\displaystyle D_{k},\mathfrak{h}_{I}]\big)

(7.34)

\displaystyle=\lambda\nu\big([D_{k}^{*}(\varphi)D_{k},V_{F}]\big)+\lambda\nu\big([D_{k}^{*}(\varphi)D_{k},V_{FB}]\big)+\lambda\nu\big([D_{k}^{*}(\varphi)D_{k},V_{B}]\big)

Let us estimate the three terms on the right hand side of Eq. (
7.1.1
), separately.
We do this in the following items (I), (II) and (III).

(I) the F term of (
7.1.1
).

A straightforward expansion of
V_{F}
based on the representation (
5.5
)
yields

\displaystyle[D_{k}^{*}(\varphi)D_{k},V_{F}]

\displaystyle=\int_{\Lambda^{*}}\hat{V}(\ell)\ D_{k}^{*}(\varphi)[D_{k},D^{*}_{\ell}]D_{\ell}\ \mathrm{d}\ell+\int_{\Lambda^{*}}\hat{V}(\ell)\ D_{k}^{*}(\varphi)D_{\ell}^{*}[D_{k},D_{\ell}]\ \mathrm{d}\ell

(7.35)

\displaystyle+\int_{\Lambda^{*}}\hat{V}(\ell)\ D_{\ell}^{*}[D_{k}^{*}(\varphi),D_{\ell}]D_{k}\ \mathrm{d}\ell+\int_{\Lambda^{*}}\hat{V}(\ell)\ [D_{k}^{*}(\varphi),D_{\ell}^{*}]D_{\ell}D_{k}\ \mathrm{d}\ell\ .

Each of the four terms in the right hand side above is estimated
in the same way.
Let us look in detail at the first one.
For

\Psi\in\mathscr{F}
and
\ell\in\Lambda^{*}
,
we find using
the Type-I estimate for
D_{\ell}
and
[D_{\ell},D_{k}]
,
the Type-IV estimate for
D_{k}(\varphi)

and the commutation relation
[\mathcal{N},D(\varphi)]=0

\displaystyle|\left\langle\Psi,D_{k}^{*}(\varphi)[D_{k},D^{*}_{\ell}]D_{\ell}\Psi\right\rangle|

\displaystyle=|\left\langle[D_{\ell},D_{k}]D_{k}(\varphi)\Psi,D_{\ell}\Psi\right\rangle|

\displaystyle\leqslant\|\mathcal{N}D_{k}(\varphi)\Psi\|\,\|\mathcal{N}\Psi\|

\displaystyle=\|D_{k}(\varphi)\mathcal{N}\Psi\|\,\|\mathcal{N}\Psi\|

\displaystyle\leqslant\|D_{k}(\varphi)\|\|\mathcal{N}\Psi\|^{2}

(7.36)

\displaystyle\leqslant|\Lambda|\|\varphi\|_{\ell^{1}}\|\mathcal{N}\Psi\|^{2}\ .

We conclude that there is a constant
C>0
such that

(7.37)

\nu\big([D_{k}^{*}(\varphi)D_{k},V_{F}]\big)\leqslant C|\Lambda|\|\hat{V}\|_{\ell^{1}}\|\varphi\|_{\ell^{1}}\nu(\mathcal{N}^{2})\ .

(II) the FB term of (
7.1.1
).

The relation
\overline{\nu(O)}=\nu(O^{*})

and a straightforward expansion
shows that

(7.38)

\displaystyle\nu\big([D_{k}^{*}(\varphi)D_{k},

\displaystyle V_{FB}]\big)

\displaystyle=\int_{\Lambda^{*}}\hat{V}(\ell)\ \nu\big([D_{k}^{*}(\varphi)D_{k},D^{*}_{\ell}\,b_{\ell}]\big)\ \mathrm{d}\ell-\int_{\Lambda^{*}}\hat{V}(\ell)\ \overline{\nu\big([D_{k}^{*}D_{k}(\varphi),D^{*}_{\ell}\,b_{\ell}]\big)}\ \mathrm{d}\ell.

We only estimate the first term in (
7.38
), since the second one is analogous.
Indeed, we expand the commutator to find that

(7.39)

\displaystyle\nu\big([D_{k}^{*}(\varphi)D_{k},D^{*}_{\ell}\,b_{\ell}]\big)

\displaystyle=\nu\big(D_{k}^{*}(\varphi)D^{*}_{\ell}[D_{k},\,b_{\ell}]\big)+\nu\big(D_{k}^{*}(\varphi)[D_{k},D^{*}_{\ell}]\,b_{\ell}\big)

(7.40)

\displaystyle\quad+\nu\big(D^{*}_{\ell}[D_{k}^{*}(\varphi),\,b_{\ell}]D_{k}\big)+\nu\big([D_{k}^{*}(\varphi),D^{*}_{\ell}]\,b_{\ell}D_{k}\big)\ .

We bound these four terms in the following three items below.

\blacksquare

Since both
[D_{k},b_{\ell}]
and
b_{\ell}
satisfy Type-II estimates,
the two
terms in (
7.39
) are bounded above in the same way.
Let us look at the first on in detail.
Indeed, for
\Psi\in\mathscr{F}
and
\ell\in\textnormal{supp}\hat{V}

we find

\displaystyle|\left\langle\Psi,D_{k}^{*}(\varphi)D^{*}_{\ell}[D_{k},\,b_{\ell}]\Psi\right\rangle|

\displaystyle\ =\ |\left\langle D_{\ell}D_{k}(\varphi)\Psi,[D_{k},\,b_{\ell}]\Psi\right\rangle|

\displaystyle\ \leqslant\ \|D_{k}(\varphi)\|\ \|\mathcal{N}\Psi\|\ \|[D_{k},b_{\ell}]\Psi\|

(7.41)

\displaystyle\ \lesssim\ |\Lambda|\|\varphi\|_{\ell^{1}}\|\mathcal{N}\Psi\|R^{\frac{1}{2}}\ \|\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{S}}^{1/2}\Psi\|

where we have used the Type-I estimate for
D_{\ell}
,
the Type-II estimate for
[D_{k},b_{\ell}]
,
the Type-IV estimate for
D_{k}(\varphi)
,
and
the commutation relation
[\mathcal{N},D_{k}(\varphi)]=0
.

\blacksquare

For the first term in (
7.40
) we consider
\Psi\in\mathscr{F}
and
\ell\in\textnormal{supp}\hat{V}
. We find

\displaystyle|\left\langle\Psi,D^{*}_{\ell}[D_{k}^{*}(\varphi),\,b_{\ell}]D_{k}\Psi\right\rangle|

\displaystyle\leqslant\|[D_{k}^{*}(\varphi),b_{\ell}]\|\,\|\mathcal{N}\Psi\|^{2}

(7.42)

\displaystyle\lesssim|\Lambda|p_{F}^{-m}\|\varphi\|_{\ell_{m}^{1}}\|\mathcal{N}\Psi\|^{2}\ .

where we have used
the Type-I estimate for
D_{k}
and
D_{\ell}
,
and the Type-III estimate
[D_{k}^{*}(\varphi),b_{\ell}].

\blacksquare

For the second term in (
7.40
) we consider
\Psi\in\mathscr{F}
and
\ell\in\textnormal{supp}\hat{V}
. We find

\displaystyle|\langle\Psi,[D_{k}^{*}(\varphi),D^{*}_{\ell}]

\displaystyle\,b_{\ell}D_{k}\Psi\rangle|

\displaystyle\leqslant|\left\langle[D_{\ell},D_{k}(\varphi)]\Psi,[b_{\ell},D_{k}]\Psi\right\rangle|+|\left\langle D_{k}^{*}[D_{\ell},D_{k}(\varphi)]\Psi,b_{\ell}\Psi\right\rangle|

\displaystyle\lesssim\|[D_{\ell},D_{k}(\varphi)]\|\|\Psi\|\|[b_{\ell},D_{k}]\Psi\|+\|[D_{\ell},D_{k}(\varphi)]\|\|\mathcal{N}\Psi\|\|b_{\ell}\Psi\|

(7.43)

\displaystyle\lesssim|\Lambda|\|\varphi\|_{\ell^{1}}R^{\frac{1}{2}}\|(\mathcal{N}+1)\Psi\|\ \|\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{S}}^{1/2}\Psi\|\ ,

where, we have used
the Type-I estimate for
D_{k}^{*}
,
Type-II estimates for
[b_{\ell},D_{k}]
and
b_{\ell}
,
Type-IV estimates for
[D_{\ell},D_{k}(\varphi)]
.

We put back the three estimates found in the three items above
to find that
there exists a constant

C>0
such that

(7.44)

\displaystyle\nu\big([D_{k}^{*}(\varphi)D_{k},V_{FB}]\big)\leqslant C\|\hat{V}\|_{\ell^{1}}\|\varphi\|_{\ell^{1}_{m}}\,|\Lambda|\Big[R^{\frac{1}{2}}\nu(\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{S}})^{\frac{1}{2}}+p_{F}^{-m}\nu(\mathcal{N}^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big]\nu(\mathcal{N}^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}\ \ .

(III) the B term of (
7.1.1
).

Similarly as we dealt with the second term, we expand

(7.45)

\displaystyle\nu\big([D_{k}^{*}(\varphi)D_{k},V_{B}]\big)

\displaystyle=\int_{\Lambda^{*}}\hat{V}(\ell)\nu\big([D_{k}^{*}(\varphi)D_{k},b_{\ell}^{*}b_{\ell}]\mathrm{d}\ell\big)

(7.46)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Lambda^{*}}\hat{V}(\ell)\nu\big([D_{k}^{*}(\varphi)D_{k},b_{-\ell}b_{\ell}]\big)\mathrm{d}\ell

(7.47)

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Lambda^{*}}\hat{V}(\ell)\overline{\nu\big([D_{k}^{*}D_{k}(\varphi),b_{-\ell}b_{\ell}]\big)}\mathrm{d}\ell\ .

We only present a proof of the estimates for
the terms in (
7.45
) and (
7.46
).
We do this in (III.1) and (III.2) below.
Since the third one is analogous to the second one, we omit it.
In order to ease the notation we shall omit the indices
k,\ell\in\textnormal{supp}\hat{V}
.

\blacksquare

Analysis of (
7.45
)
.
We expand the commutator to find that

(7.48)

\displaystyle[D^{*}(\varphi)D,b^{*}b]=D^{*}(\varphi)b^{*}[D,b]+D^{*}(\varphi)[D,b^{*}]b+[D^{*}(\varphi),b^{*}b]D

and estimate each term separately.
Let us fix a
\Psi\in\mathscr{F}
.

\blacklozenge
The first term in (
7.48
) may be estimated as

(7.49)

\displaystyle|\langle\Psi,D^{*}(\varphi)

\displaystyle b^{*}[D,b]\Psi\rangle|

\displaystyle\ \leqslant\ \|[b,D(\varphi)]\|\,\|\Psi\|\,\|[D,b]\Psi\|+\|D(\varphi)\|\,\|b\Psi\|\,\|[D,b]\Psi\|

\displaystyle\ \lesssim\ |\Lambda|p_{F}^{-m}\|\varphi\|_{\ell^{1}_{m}}\|\Psi\|R^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{S}}^{1/2}\Psi\|+|\Lambda|\|\varphi\|_{\ell^{1}}R\|\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{S}}^{1/2}\Psi\|^{2}

\displaystyle\ \leqslant\ \|\varphi\|_{\ell^{1}_{m}}|\Lambda|\Big(p_{F}^{-m}\|\Psi\|+R^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{S}}^{1/2}\Psi\|\Big)R^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{S}}^{1/2}\Psi\|\ .

Here, we have used Type-II estimates for
[D,b]
and
b
,
Type-III estimates for
[b,D(\varphi)]
,
and Type-IV estimates for
D(\varphi)
.

\blacklozenge
The second term in (
7.48
)
may be estimated as

\displaystyle|\langle\Psi,D^{*}(

\displaystyle\varphi)[D,b^{*}]b\Psi\rangle|

(7.50)

\displaystyle\ \leqslant\ \|D(\varphi)\|\,\|[b,D^{*}]\Psi\|\,\|b\Psi\|+\|[[b,D^{*}],D(\varphi)]\|\,\|\Psi\|\,\|b\Psi\|

\displaystyle\ \lesssim\ |\Lambda|\|\varphi\|_{\ell^{1}}R\|\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{S}}\Psi\|^{2}+|\Lambda|p_{F}^{-m}\|\varphi\|_{\ell^{1}_{m}}\|\Psi\|R^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{S}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\Psi\|

\displaystyle\ \lesssim\ \|\varphi\|_{\ell^{1}_{m}}|\Lambda|\Big(p_{F}^{-m}\|\Psi\|+R^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{S}}^{1/2}\Psi\|\Big)R^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{S}}^{1/2}\Psi\|

Here, we have used Type-II estimates for

[b,D^{*}]
and
b
,
the Type-III estimate for

[[b,D^{*}],D(\varphi)]
,
and Type-IV
estimates for

D(\varphi)
.

\blacklozenge
The third term in (
7.48
) may be estimated as

\displaystyle|\left\langle\Psi,[D^{*}(\varphi),b^{*}b]D\Psi\right\rangle|

\displaystyle\ \leqslant\ \|[D^{*}(\varphi),b^{*}b]\|\,\|\Psi\|\,\|D\Psi\|

(7.51)

\displaystyle\ \lesssim\ R{|\Lambda|}p_{F}^{-m}\|\varphi\|_{\ell^{1}_{m}}\,\|\Psi\|\,\|\mathcal{N}\Psi\|

Here, we have used the Type-I
estimate for
D
, and
Type-III estimates
and the operator norm bound for
b
(see (
4.20
))
for

\|[D^{*}(\varphi),b^{*}b]]\|\leqslant\|b^{*}\|\,\|[D^{*}(\varphi),b]\|+\|[D^{*}(\varphi),b^{*}]\|\,\|b\|.

\blacksquare

Analysis of (
7.46
)
.
Similarly as before, we expand the commutator

(7.52)

\displaystyle[D^{*}(\varphi)D,bb]=D^{*}(\varphi)b[D,b]+D^{*}(\varphi)[D,b]b+[D^{*}(\varphi),bb]D\ .

and estimate each term separately.
We let
\Psi\in\mathscr{F}
.

\blacklozenge
The first term in (
7.52
) may be estimated as

\displaystyle|\left\langle\Psi,D^{*}(\varphi)b[D,b]\Psi\right\rangle|

\displaystyle\ \leqslant\ \|D(\varphi)\|\|b\|\|\Psi\|\|[D,b]\Psi\|

(7.53)

\displaystyle\ \lesssim\ |\Lambda|\|\varphi\|_{\ell^{1}}R^{\frac{3}{2}}\|\Psi\|\|\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{S}}^{1/2}\Psi\|\ .

Here, we have used the Type-II estimate for
[D,b]
, the Type-IV
estimate for
D(\varphi)
,
and the operator norm bound
\|b\|\lesssim R
.

\blacklozenge
The second term in
(
7.52
)
may be estimated as
