\displaystyle|\left\langle\Psi,D^{*}(\varphi)[D,b]b\Psi\right\rangle|

\displaystyle\ \leqslant\ \|D(\varphi)\|\|[D,b]\|\|\Psi\|\|b\Psi\|

(7.54)

\displaystyle\ \lesssim\ |\Lambda|\|\varphi\|_{\ell^{1}}R^{\frac{3}{2}}\|\Psi\|\|\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{S}}^{1/2}\Psi\|\ .

Here, we have used the Type-II estimate for
b
, the Type-IV
estimate for
D(\varphi)
,
and the operator norm bound
\|[D,b]\|\lesssim R
.

\blacklozenge
The third term in
(
7.52
)
may be estimated as

\displaystyle|\left\langle\Psi,D[D^{*}(\varphi),bb]\Psi\right\rangle|

\displaystyle\ \leqslant\ \|[D^{*}(\varphi),bb]\|\,\|\Psi\|\,\|D^{*}\Psi\|

(7.55)

\displaystyle\ \lesssim\ R{|\Lambda|}p_{F}^{-m}\|\varphi\|_{\ell^{1}_{m}}\,\|\Psi\|\,\|\mathcal{N}\Psi\|\ .

Here, we have used the Type-I
estimate for
D^{*}
, and
Type-III estimates
and the operator norm bound for
b
(see (
4.20
))
for

\|[D^{*}(\varphi),bb]]\|\leqslant\|b\|\,\|[D^{*}(\varphi),b]\|+\|[D^{*}(\varphi),b]\|\,\|b\|.

Putting together the estimates found in the six points above,
we find that
there exists a constant
C>0

such that
for all
k\in\textnormal{supp}\hat{V}

(7.56)

\nu\big([D_{k}^{*}(\varphi)D_{k},V_{B}]\big)\leqslant C|\Lambda|\|\varphi\|_{\ell^{1}_{m}}\|\hat{V}\|_{\ell^{1}}\Big[R^{\frac{3}{2}}\nu(\mathcal{N}_{S})^{\frac{1}{2}}+R\nu(\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{S}})+\frac{R^{\frac{1}{2}}}{p_{F}^{m}}\nu(\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{S}})^{\frac{1}{2}}+\frac{R}{p_{F}^{m}}\nu(\mathcal{N}^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big]\ .

Finally, we can go back to the original decomposition found in (
7.1.1
), plug it back in the starting point (
7.33
),
and use the estimates found in Eqs. (
7.37
), (
7.44
) and (
7.56
) to find that
there exists a constant
C>0

such that

\displaystyle|M_{1}^{\delta}(t,\varphi)|

\displaystyle\leqslant C|\Lambda|\lambda t^{3}R\|\varphi\|_{\ell^{1}_{m}}\|\hat{V}\|_{\ell^{2}}^{2}\|\|\hat{V}\|_{\ell^{1}}

(7.57)

\displaystyle\quad\times\sup_{0\leqslant\tau\leqslant t}\Big(R^{\frac{3}{2}}\nu_{\tau}(\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{S}})^{\frac{1}{2}}+R\nu_{\tau}(\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{S}})+\frac{R^{\frac{1}{2}}}{p_{F}^{m}}\nu_{\tau}(\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{S}})^{\frac{1}{2}}+\frac{R}{p_{F}^{m}}\nu_{\tau}(\mathcal{N})^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big)\ .

To conclude, we note that
\nu(\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{S}})\leqslant R^{1/2}\nu(\mathcal{N})
so that the third term in the right hand side above can be absorbed into the fourth one.
This finishes the proof.
∎

7.1.2.
Analysis of
M^{\mathcal{R}}

Let us estimate the second term of the right hand side in (
7.17
).

Claim 3
.

For all
m>0
there exists a constant
C>0
such that for all
tgeq0
and
\varphi\in\ell^{1}

the following estimate holds true

(7.58)

\displaystyle|M^{\mathcal{R}}(t,\varphi)|\,\leqslant\,Ct^{2}\|\hat{V}\|_{\ell^{1}}^{2}|\Lambda|\|\varphi\|_{\ell^{1}_{m}}\sup_{\tau\leqslant t}\Big(R^{\frac{1}{2}}\nu_{\tau}(\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{S}})^{\frac{1}{2}}+p_{F}^{-m}\Big)\nu_{\tau}(\mathcal{N}^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}\ .

Proof.

Let us fix
m>0
,
t\geqslant 0
and
\varphi\in\ell^{1}
.
Going back to the definition of the main term in
(
7.6
)
,
we plug the reminder operator

\mathcal{R}_{k,\ell}

defined in (
7.1
),
from which the elementary inequality follows

(7.59)

\displaystyle|M^{\mathcal{R}}(t,\varphi)|

\displaystyle\ \lesssim\ t^{2}\|\hat{V}\|_{\ell^{1}}^{2}\sup_{k,\ell\in\textnormal{supp}\hat{V},t_{i}\in[0,t]}\Big|\nu_{t_{2}}\Big(D_{t_{1}}^{*}(k,\varphi)\mathcal{R}_{k,\ell}(t_{1},t_{2})D_{t_{2}}(\ell)\Big)\Big|\ .

Let us estimate the supremum quantity in the above equation.
Since our estimates are uniform in
t_{1},t_{2}
we shall omit them.
Leting
\Psi\in\mathscr{F}
, we find that

\displaystyle|\left\langle\Psi,D_{k}^{*}(\varphi)\mathcal{R}_{k,\ell}D_{\ell}\Psi\right\rangle|

\displaystyle\ \leqslant\ |\left\langle D_{k}^{*}(\varphi)\mathcal{R}^{*}_{k,\ell}\Psi,D_{\ell}\Psi\right\rangle|+|\left\langle\Psi,[D_{k}^{*}(\varphi),\mathcal{R}_{k,\ell}]D_{\ell}\Psi\right\rangle|

(7.60)

\displaystyle\ \leqslant\ \|D_{k}^{*}(\varphi)\|\|\mathcal{R}_{k,\ell}\Psi\|\|D_{\ell}\Psi\|+\|\Psi\|\|[D_{k}^{*}(\varphi),\mathcal{R}_{k,\ell}]\|\|D_{\ell}\Psi\|\ .

Letting
k,\ell\in\textnormal{supp}\hat{V},
we find the following estimates for the quantities containing
\mathcal{R}_{k,\ell}

(7.61)

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{R}_{k,\ell}\Psi\|\lesssim R^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{S}}^{1/2}\Psi\|\quad\textnormal{and}\quad\|[D_{k}^{*}(\varphi),\mathcal{R}_{k,\ell}]\|\lesssim|\Lambda|p_{F}^{-m}\|\varphi\|_{\ell^{1}_{m}}\ .

The proof of these estimates follows the same lines of the proof of Lemma
4.6

and
4.7
, so we shall omit it.
We combine the last three displayed equations together with Remark
5.1
to conclude
the proof of the estimate contained in Eq. (
7.58
)
∎

7.1.3.
Proof of Lemma
7.1

Proof of Lemma
7.1
.

The triangle intequality and the decomposition
M=M^{\delta}_{0}+M^{\delta}_{1}+M^{\mathcal{R}}

gives
|M-M_{0}^{\delta}|\leqslant|M^{\delta}_{1}|+|M^{\mathcal{R}}|
.
It suffices then to use the results contained in Claims
1
,
2
and
3
.
∎

7.2.
Analysis of the remainder terms

In this subsection, we estimate the remainder terms
R^{(i)}

(see (
7.7
))
and give a proof of Lemma
7.2
.

Proof of Lemma
7.2
.

Throughout the proof, we fix
m>0
,
t\geqslant 0
and
\varphi\in\ell_{m}^{1}
.
We make extensive use of the Type-I, Type-II, Type-III and Type-IV estimates contained in
Lemmas
4.5
,

4.6
,

4.7
,
and

4.8
, respectively,
together with the operator bound
\|b\|\leqslant R
–see (
4.20
).
Due to the similarities, we only show all the details for the proof of (1), and
only give the key estimates for the proofs of (2), (3), and (4).

Proof of (1)

Our starting point is the elementary estimate

(7.62)

\displaystyle|R^{(1)}(t,\varphi)|

\displaystyle\,\lesssim\,t^{2}\|\hat{V}\|_{\ell^{1}}^{2}\sup_{k,\ell\in\textnormal{supp}\hat{V},t_{i}\in[0,t]}\Big|\nu_{t_{2}}\Big(D_{k}^{*}(t_{1},\varphi)B^{*}_{\ell}(t_{2})[b_{k}(t_{1}),D_{\ell}(t_{2})]\Big)\Big|\ .

In view of Remark
5.1
,
it is sufficient to give estimates for pure states
\Psi\in\mathscr{F}
.
In order to ease the notation, we shall drop the time variables
t_{1},t_{2}\in[0,t]
,
together with the momentum labels
k,\ell\in\textnormal{supp}\hat{V}
.
Letting
\Psi\in\mathscr{F}
, we find that

\displaystyle|\left\langle\Psi,D^{*}(\varphi)B^{*}[b,D]\Psi\right\rangle|

\displaystyle\ \leqslant\ \|D(\varphi)\|\|\Psi\|\|B^{*}\|\|[b,D]\Psi\|

(7.63)

\displaystyle\ \lesssim\ |\Lambda|\|\varphi\|_{\ell^{1}}\|\Psi\|R^{\frac{3}{2}}\|\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{S}}^{1/2}\Psi\|,

where we used the
Type-II estimate for
[b,D]
,
the Type-IV estimate for
D^{*}(\varphi)
,
and the norm bound
\|B\|\leqslant 2\|b\|\lesssim R
.
The estimate in Eq. (
7.10
) now follows from the last two displayed equations, and
\nu(\mathds{1})=1
.

Proof of (2)

Letting
\Psi\in\mathscr{F}
, we find that

\displaystyle|\left\langle\Psi,[D^{*}(\varphi),B^{*}]Db\Psi\right\rangle|

\displaystyle\ \leqslant\ \|[D^{*}(\varphi),b]\|\|\Psi\|\|Db\Psi\|

\displaystyle\ \lesssim\ \|[D^{*}(\varphi),b]\|\|\Psi\|\|\mathcal{N}b\Psi\|

(7.64)

\displaystyle\ \lesssim\ |\Lambda|p_{F}^{-m}\|\varphi\|_{\ell^{1}_{m}}R\|\Psi\|\|\mathcal{N}\Psi\|\ ,

where we have used the
Type-II estimate, commutation relations and the norm bound for
b

to obtain

\|Db\Psi\|\leqslant\|\mathcal{N}b\Psi\|\leqslant\|b\mathcal{N}\Psi\|\lesssim R\|\mathcal{N}\Psi\|
;
and the Type-III estimate for
[D^{*}(\varphi),b]
.
The proof is finished after one follows the same argument we used for (1).

Proof of (3)

Letting
\Psi\in\mathscr{F}
, we find that

(7.65)

\displaystyle|\left\langle\Psi,B^{*}[D(\varphi),D]b\Psi\right\rangle|

\displaystyle\leqslant\|B^{*}\|\|\Psi\|\|[D(\varphi),D]\|\|b\Psi\|

(7.66)

\displaystyle\lesssim R^{\frac{3}{2}}\|\Psi\||\Lambda|\|\varphi\|_{\ell^{1}}\|\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{S}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\Psi\|\ ,

where we used the Type-II estimate for
b
,
the Type-IV estimate for
[D(\varphi),D]
,
and the norm bound
\|B\|\lesssim R
.
The proof is finished after one follows the same argument we used for (1).

Proof of (4)
Letting
\Psi\in\mathscr{F}
, we find that

\displaystyle|\left\langle\Psi,[Db(\varphi),B^{*}D]\Psi\right\rangle|

\displaystyle\leqslant 2|\left\langle\Psi,Db(\varphi)B^{*}D\Psi\right\rangle|

\displaystyle\lesssim\|D^{*}\Psi\|\|b(\varphi)\|\|B^{*}\|\|D\Psi\|

(7.67)

\displaystyle\lesssim p_{F}^{-m}|\Lambda|\|\varphi\|_{\ell^{1}_{m}}R\|\mathcal{N}\Psi\|^{2}\ .

where we used the Type-I estimate for
D
and
D^{*}
,
the Type-III estimate for
b(\varphi)
,
and the norm bound
\|B\|\lesssim R
.
The proof is finished after one follows the same argument we used for (1).
∎

8.
Subleading Order Terms

In this section we analyze the
T_{\alpha,\beta}(t,p)

terms of the double commutator expansion (
3.20
)
that we regard as subleading order terms.
So far, out of the nine terms
we have analyzed two leading order terms:

T_{F,F}
in Section
6

and
T_{FB,FB}
in Section
7
.
Thus, we shall analyze the reminding seven.
We do this in the following five subsections.

8.1.
Analysis of
T_{F,FB}

The main result of this subsection is the following proposition,
that gives an estimate on the size of
T_{F,FB}
.

Proposition 8.1

(Analysis of
T_{F,FB}
)
.

Let
T_{F,FB}(t,p)

be the quantity defined in (
3.21
)
with
\alpha=F
and
\beta=FB
, and
let
m>0
.
Then, there exists a constant
C>0

such that for all
\varphi\in\ell_{m}^{1}
and
t\geqslant 0

the following estimate holds true

(8.1)

|T_{F,FB}(t,\varphi)|\ \leqslant\ Ct^{2}\|\hat{V}\|_{\ell^{1}}^{2}|\Lambda|\|\varphi\|_{\ell^{1}_{m}}\sup_{0\leqslant\tau\leqslant t}\Big(R^{\frac{1}{2}}\,\nu_{\tau}(\mathcal{N}^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}\nu_{\tau}(\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{S}})^{\frac{1}{2}}+p_{F}^{-m}\nu_{\tau}(\mathcal{N}^{2})\Big)

where we recall

T_{F,FB}(t,\varphi)=\left\langle\varphi,T_{F,FB}(t)\right\rangle

and

R=|\Lambda|p_{F}^{d-1}
.

Proof.

For simplicity, we assume
\varphi
is real-valued–in the general case,
one may expand into real and imaginary parts and use linearity of the commutators.
Starting from (
3.23
) we use the self-adjointness of
V_{F}(t)
and
N(\varphi)=\int_{\Lambda^{*}}\varphi(p)a_{p}^{*}a_{p}\mathrm{d}p
to get
the elementary inequality

\displaystyle|

\displaystyle T_{F,FB}(t,\varphi)|

\displaystyle\quad=\Big|\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{t_{1}}\int_{\Lambda^{*2}}\hat{V}(k)\hat{V}(\ell)2\mathrm{Re}\nu_{t_{2}}\Big([[N(\varphi),D_{k}^{*}(t_{1})D_{k}(t_{1})],D_{\ell}^{*}(t_{2})b_{\ell}(t_{2})]\Big)\mathrm{d}t_{1}\mathrm{d}t_{2}\mathrm{d}k\mathrm{d}\ell\Big|

(8.2)

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim t^{2}\|\hat{V}\|_{\ell_{1}}^{2}\sup_{k,\ell\in\textnormal{supp}\hat{V},t_{i}\in[0,t]}\Big|\nu_{t_{2}}\Big([[N(\varphi),D_{k}^{*}(t_{1})D_{k}(t_{1})],D_{\ell}^{*}(t_{2})b_{\ell}(t_{2})]\Big)\Big|\ .

It suffices now to estimate the supremum quantity in the above equation.
In order to ease the notation, we shall drop the time labels
t_{1},t_{2}\in[0,t]
,
together with the momentum variables
k,\ell\in\textnormal{supp}\hat{V}
.
Using the notation
D^{*}(\varphi)\equiv[N(\varphi),D^{*}]
we compute the commutator

(8.3)

[N(\varphi),D^{*}D]=D^{*}(\varphi)D+D^{*}D(\varphi)\ .

We shall only show how to estimate the contribution that arises from
the first term on the right hand side of (
8.3
); the second one is analogous.
To this end, we expand

(8.4)

\displaystyle[D^{*}(\varphi)

\displaystyle D,D^{*}b]

\displaystyle=D^{*}(\varphi)[D,D^{*}]b+D^{*}(\varphi)D^{*}[D,b]+[D^{*}(\varphi),D^{*}]bD+D^{*}[D^{*}(\varphi),b]D\ .

Next, we estimate the expectation of each term in (
8.4
) separately.
In view of Remark
5.1
, it suffices to provide estimates for pure states
\Psi\in\mathscr{F}
.
We shall make extensive use of Type-I to Type-IV estimates contained in Lemma
4.5
–
4.8
,
the commutation relations from Lemmas
4.3
and
4.4
,
and operator bounds of the form
\|b\|_{B(\mathscr{F})}\lesssim R
.

\blacksquare
The first term in (
8.4
).

Using the Type-I estimate for
[D^{*},D]
,
the Type-II estimate for
b
,
the Type-IV estimate for
D(\varphi)

and the commutation relation

[\mathcal{N},D(\varphi)]=0

we find

\displaystyle|\left\langle\Psi,D^{*}(\varphi)[D,D^{*}]b\Psi\right\rangle|

\displaystyle\ \leqslant\ \|[D^{*},D]D(\varphi)\Psi\|\,\|b\Psi\|

\displaystyle\ \lesssim\ \|\mathcal{N}D(\varphi)\Psi\|R^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{S}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\Psi\|

(8.5)

\displaystyle\ \lesssim\ |\Lambda|\|\varphi\|_{\ell^{1}}\|\mathcal{N}\Psi\|R^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{S}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\Psi\|\ .

\blacksquare
The second term in (
8.4
).

Using the Type-I estimate for
D
,
the Type-II estimate for
[D,b]
,
the Type-IV estimate for
D(\varphi)

and the commutation relation

[\mathcal{N},D(\varphi)]=0

we find

\displaystyle|\left\langle\Psi,D^{*}(\varphi)D^{*}[D,b]\Psi\right\rangle|

\displaystyle\ \leqslant\ \|DD(\varphi)\Psi\|\,\|[D,b]\Psi\|

\displaystyle\ \lesssim\ \|\mathcal{N}D(\varphi)\Psi\|R^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{S}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\Psi\|

(8.6)

\displaystyle\ \lesssim\ |\Lambda|\|\varphi\|_{\ell^{1}}\|\mathcal{N}\Psi\|R^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{S}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\Psi\|\ .

\blacksquare
The third term in (
8.4
).
Using the Type-I estimate for
D^{*}
,
the Type-II estimate for both
b
and
[D,b]
,
the Type-IV estimate for
[D,D(\varphi)]

and the commutation relation

[\mathcal{N},[D,D(\varphi)]]=0

we find

\displaystyle|\left\langle\Psi,[D^{*}(\varphi),D^{*}]bD\Psi\right\rangle|

\displaystyle\ \leqslant\ |\left\langle[D,D(\varphi)]\Psi,[b,D]\Psi\right\rangle|+|\left\langle[D,D(\varphi)]\Psi,Db\Psi\right\rangle|

\displaystyle\ \leqslant\ \|[D,D(\varphi)]\Psi\|\,\|[b,D]\Psi\|+\|D^{*}[D,D(\varphi)]\Psi\|\,\|b\Psi\|

(8.7)

\displaystyle\ \lesssim\ |\Lambda|\|\varphi\|_{\ell^{1}}\|(\mathcal{N}+1)\Psi\|R^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{S}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\Psi\|\ .

\blacksquare
The fourth term in (
8.4
).

Using the Type-I estimate for
D
and
the Type-III estimate for
[D^{*}(\varphi),b]

we find

(8.8)

\displaystyle|\left\langle\Psi,D^{*}[D^{*}(\varphi),b]D\Psi\right\rangle|\,\leqslant\,\|[D^{*}(\varphi),b]\|\|D\Psi\|^{2}\,\lesssim\,|\Lambda|p_{F}^{-m}\|\varphi\|_{\ell^{1}_{m}}\|\mathcal{N}\Psi\|^{2}\ .

The proof now follows by collecting the previous four estimates in the expansion (
8.4
),
and plugging them back in (
8.2
).
∎

8.2.
Analysis of
T_{F,B}

The main result of this subsection is the following proposition,
that gives an estimate on the size of
T_{F,B}
.

Proposition 8.2

(Analysis of
T_{F,B}
)
.

Let
T_{F,B}(t,p)

be the quantity defined in (
3.21
)
with
\alpha=F
and
\beta=B
, and
let
m>0
.
Then, there exists a constant
C>0

such that for all
\varphi\in\ell_{m}^{1}
and
t\geqslant 0

the following estimate holds true

(8.9)

|T_{F,B}(t,\varphi)|\ \leqslant\ Ct^{2}\|\hat{V}\|_{\ell^{1}}^{2}|\Lambda|\|\varphi\|_{\ell^{1}_{m}}\sup_{0\leqslant\tau\leqslant t}\Big(R^{\frac{3}{2}}\nu_{\tau}(\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{S}})^{\frac{1}{2}}+Rp_{F}^{-m}\nu(\mathcal{N}^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}+R\nu_{\tau}(\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{S}})\Big)

where we recall

T_{F,B}(t,\varphi)=\left\langle\varphi,T_{F,B}(t)\right\rangle

and

R=|\Lambda|p_{F}^{d-1}
.

Proof.

For simplicity, we assume
\varphi
is real-valued–in the general case,
one may expand into real and imaginary parts and use linearity of the commutators.
Starting from (
3.23
) we use the self-adjointness of
V_{F}(t)
,
V_{B}(t)
and
N(\varphi)=\int_{\Lambda^{*}}\varphi(p)a_{p}^{*}a_{p}\mathrm{d}p
to get
the elementary inequality

(8.10)

\displaystyle|T_{F,B}(t,\varphi)|

\displaystyle=\Big|\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{t_{1}}\mathrm{Re}\,\nu_{t_{2}}\Big([[N(\varphi),V_{F}(t_{1})],V_{B}(t_{2})]\Big)\mathrm{d}t_{1}\mathrm{d}t_{2}\Big|

\displaystyle\lesssim t^{2}\|\hat{V}\|_{\ell_{1}}^{2}\sup_{k,\ell\in\textnormal{supp}\hat{V},t_{i}\in[0,t]}\Big|\nu_{t_{2}}\Big([[N(\varphi),D_{k}^{*}(t_{1})D_{k}(t_{1})],b_{\ell}^{*}(t_{2})b_{\ell}(t_{2})]\Big)\Big|

\displaystyle\,+t^{2}\|\hat{V}\|_{\ell_{1}}^{2}\sup_{k,\ell\in\textnormal{supp}\hat{V},t_{i}\in[0,t]}\Big|\nu_{t_{2}}\Big([[N(\varphi),D_{k}^{*}(t_{1})D_{k}(t_{1})],b_{\ell}(t_{2})b_{-\ell}(t_{2})]\Big)\Big|\ ,

where in the last line we used the representation
of
V_{F}(t)
and
V_{B}(t)

in terms of
b
- and
D
-operators found in Eqs. (
5.5
) and (
5.7
) –the
b^{*}b^{*}
term
is re-written in terms of
bb
upon taking the real part of
\nu
.

We now estimate the two supremum quantities in (
8.10
), which we shall refer to
as an
off-diagonal contribution
,
and a
diagonal contribution
,
with respect to the operators
b
and
b^{*}
.
In view of Remark
5.1
, it suffices to provide estimates for pure states
\Psi\in\mathscr{F}
.
Further, in order to ease the notation, we omit the time labels
t_{1},t_{2}\in[0,t]

and the momentum variables
k,\ell\in\textnormal{supp}\hat{V}
.
We make extensive use of Type-I to Type-IV estimates contained in Lemma
4.5
–
4.8
,
the commutation relations from Lemmas
4.3
and
4.4
,
and operator bounds of the form
\|b\|_{B(\mathscr{F})}\lesssim R
.

\blacksquare
The off-diagonal contribution of (
8.10
).

We expand the first commutator as follows

(8.11)

\displaystyle[[N(\varphi),D^{*}D],bb]=[D^{*}(\varphi)D,bb]+[D^{*}D(\varphi),bb]\ ,

where we recall we use the notation
D^{*}(\varphi)=[N(\varphi),D]
.
We shall only show in detail how to estimate the first term in (
8.11
)–the second term
can be estimated in the same spirit.
We expand the second commutator as follows

(8.12)

\displaystyle[D^{*}(\varphi)D,bb]=D^{*}(\varphi)b[D,b]+D^{*}(\varphi)[D,b]b+[D^{*}(\varphi),bb]D\ .

We now estimate the three terms in the right hand side of (
8.12
).

\blacklozenge

The first term of (
8.12
).

Letting
\Psi\in\mathscr{F}
, we find that

\displaystyle|\left\langle\Psi,D^{*}(\varphi)b[D,b]\Psi\right\rangle

\displaystyle\ \leqslant\ \|D(\varphi)\|\|\Psi\|\|b\|\|[D,b]\Psi\|

(8.13)

\displaystyle\ \lesssim\ |\Lambda|\|\varphi\|_{\ell^{1}}\|\Psi\|R^{\frac{3}{2}}\|\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{S}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\Psi\|\ ,

where we used
the Type-II estimate for
[D,b]
,
the Type-IV estimate for
D(\varphi)
,
and the norm bound
\|b\|\lesssim R
.

\blacklozenge

The second term of (
8.12
).

Letting
\Psi\in\mathscr{F}
, we find that

\displaystyle|\left\langle\Psi,D^{*}(\varphi)[D,b]b\Psi\right\rangle|

\displaystyle\ \leqslant\ \|D(\varphi)\|\|\Psi\|\|[D,b]\|\|b\Psi\|

(8.14)

\displaystyle\ \lesssim\ |\Lambda|\|\varphi\|_{\ell^{1}}\|\Psi\|R^{\frac{3}{2}}\|\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{S}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\Psi\|\ ,

where we used
the Type-II estimate for
b
,
the Type-IV estimate for
D^{*}(\varphi)
,
and the norm bound
\|[D,b]\|\lesssim R
,

\blacklozenge

The third term of (
8.12
).

Letting
\Psi\in\mathscr{F}
, we find that

\displaystyle|\left\langle\Psi,[D^{*}(\varphi),bb]D\Psi\right\rangle|

\displaystyle\ \leqslant\ \|[D^{*}(\varphi),bb]\|\|\Psi\|\|\mathcal{N}\Psi\|

(8.15)

\displaystyle\ \lesssim\ |\Lambda|\|\varphi\|_{\ell^{1}_{m}}p_{F}^{-m}R\|\Psi\|\|\mathcal{N}\Psi\|\ ,

where we used
the Type-I estimate for
D
,
the Type-III estimate for
[D^{*}(\varphi),b]

and the norm bound
\|b\|\lesssim R
.

We collect the four estimates found above and put them back in (
8.11
)
to find that the off-diagonal contribution satisfies the following upper bound

(8.16)

\displaystyle\Big|\nu\Big([[N(\varphi),D^{*}D],bb]\Big)\Big|\ \lesssim\ |\Lambda|\|\varphi\|_{\ell_{m}^{1}}\Big(R^{\frac{3}{2}}\nu(\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{S}})^{\frac{1}{2}}+\frac{R}{p_{F}^{m}}\nu(\mathcal{N}^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big)\ .

\blacksquare

The diagonal contribution of (
8.10
).

Similarly as before, we shall expand the commutator as follows.

(8.17)

\displaystyle[D^{*}(\varphi)D,b^{*}b]=D^{*}(\varphi)b^{*}[D,b]+D^{*}(\varphi)[D,b^{*}]b+[D^{*}(\varphi),b^{*}b]D\ .

These three terms are estimates as follows.

\blacklozenge

The first term of (
8.17
)
.
Letting
\Psi\in\mathscr{F}
, we find that

\displaystyle|\langle\Psi,D^{*}(\varphi)b^{*}

\displaystyle[D,b]\Psi\rangle|

\displaystyle\leqslant|\left\langle D(\varphi)b\Psi,[D,b]\Psi\right\rangle|+|\left\langle[D(\varphi),b]\Psi,[D,b]\Psi\right\rangle|

\displaystyle\lesssim\|D(\varphi)\|\|b\Psi\|\|[D,b]\Psi\|+\|[D(\varphi),b]\|\|\Psi\|\|[D,b]\|\|\Psi\|

(8.18)

\displaystyle\lesssim|\Lambda|\|\varphi\|_{\ell_{m}^{1}}\Big(R\|\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{S}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\Psi\|^{2}+p_{F}^{-m}R\|\Psi\|^{2}\Big)\ .

where we used
the Type-II estimate for
b
and
[D,b]
,
the Type-III estimate for
[D(\varphi),b]
,
the Type-IV estimate for
D(\varphi)
.

\blacklozenge

The second term of (
8.17
)
.
Letting
\Psi\in\mathscr{F}
, we find that

\displaystyle|\langle\Psi,D^{*}(\varphi)[D,

\displaystyle b^{*}]b\Psi\rangle|

\displaystyle\leqslant|\left\langle D(\varphi)[D^{*},b]\Psi,b\Psi\right\rangle|+|\left\langle[D(\varphi),[D^{*},b]]\Psi,b\Psi\right\rangle|

\displaystyle\lesssim\|D(\varphi)\|\|[D^{*},b]\Psi\|\|b\Psi\|+\|[D(\varphi),[D^{*},b]]\|\|\Psi\|\|b\Psi\|

(8.19)

\displaystyle\lesssim|\Lambda|\|\varphi\|_{\ell_{m}^{1}}\Big(R\|\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{S}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\Psi\|^{2}+p_{F}^{-m}R\|\Psi\|^{2}\Big)\ ,

where we used
the Type-II estimate for
b
and
[D^{*},b]
,
the Type-III estimate for
[D(\varphi),[D^{*},b]]
,
and the Type-IV estimate for
D(\varphi)
.

\blacklozenge

The third term of (
8.17
)
.
Letting
\Psi\in\mathscr{F}
, we find that

\displaystyle|\left\langle\Psi,[D^{*}(\varphi),b^{*}b]D\Psi\right\rangle|

\displaystyle\leqslant\|\Psi\|\|[D^{*}(\varphi),b^{*}b]\|\|D\Psi\|

\displaystyle\leqslant\Big(\|b^{*}\|\|[D^{*}(\varphi),b]\|+\|[D^{*}(\varphi),b^{*}]\|\|b\|\Big)\|\Psi\|\|\mathcal{N}\Psi\|

(8.20)

\displaystyle\lesssim|\Lambda|\|\varphi\|_{\ell^{1}_{m}}p_{F}^{-m}R\|\Psi\|\|\mathcal{N}\Psi\|\ ,

where we used
the Type-I estimate
D
,
the Type-III estimate for
[D^{*}(\varphi),b]
and
[D^{*}[\varphi],b^{*}]
,
and the norm bound
\|b\|\lesssim R
.

We gather the three above estimates to find that
the diagonal contribution is satisfies the following upper bound

(8.21)

\displaystyle\Big|\nu\Big([[N(\varphi),D^{*}D],b^{*}b]\Big)\Big|\ \lesssim\ |\Lambda|\|\varphi\|_{\ell_{m}^{1}}\Big(R\nu(\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{S}})+\frac{R}{p_{F}^{m}}\nu(\mathcal{N}^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big)\ .

The proof of the proposition is finished once we gather the diagonal and off-diagonal contributions
and plug them back in (
8.10
).
∎

8.3.
Analysis of
T_{FB,F}

In this subsection, we analyze the term
T_{FB,F}
.
Our main result is the estimate contained in the next proposition.

Proposition 8.3

(Analysis of
T_{FB,F}
)
.

Let
T_{FB,F}(t,p)

be the quantity defined in (
3.21
)
with
\alpha=FB
and
\beta=F
,
and let
m>0
.
Then, there exists a constant
C>0

such that for all
\varphi\in\ell_{m}^{1}
and
t\geqslant 0

the following estimate holds true

(8.22)

\displaystyle|T_{FB,F}(t,\varphi)|

\displaystyle\,\leqslant C\,t^{2}\|\hat{V}\|_{\ell^{1}}^{2}|\Lambda|\|\varphi\|_{\ell^{1}_{m}}\sup_{0\leqslant\tau\leqslant t}\Big(R^{\frac{1}{2}}\nu_{\tau}(\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{S}})^{\frac{1}{2}}+p_{F}^{-m}\nu_{\tau}(\mathcal{N}^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big)\nu_{\tau}(\mathcal{N}^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}

where we recall

T_{FB,F}(t,\varphi)=\left\langle\varphi,T_{FB,F}(t)\right\rangle

and

R=|\Lambda|p_{F}^{d-1}
.

Proof.

For simplicity, we assume
\varphi
is real-valued–in the general case,
one may expand into real and imaginary parts and use linearity of the commutators.
Starting from (
3.23
) we use the self-adjointness of
V_{FB}(t)
,
V_{F}(t)
and
N(\varphi)=\int_{\Lambda^{*}}\varphi(p)a_{p}^{*}a_{p}\mathrm{d}p
to get
the elementary inequality

(8.23)

\displaystyle|T_{FB,F}(t,\varphi)|

\displaystyle=\Big|\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{t_{1}}\mathrm{Re}\,\nu_{t_{2}}\Big([[N(\varphi),V_{FB}(t_{1})],V_{F}(t_{2})]\Big)\mathrm{d}t_{1}\mathrm{d}t_{2}\Big|

\displaystyle\lesssim t^{2}\|\hat{V}\|_{\ell_{1}}^{2}\sup_{k,\ell\in\textnormal{supp}\hat{V},t_{i}\in[0,t]}\Big|\nu_{t_{2}}\Big([[N(\varphi),D_{k}^{*}(t_{1})b_{k}(t_{1})],D_{\ell}^{*}(t_{2})D_{\ell}(t_{2})]\Big)\Big|

where in the last line we used the representation
of
V_{F}(t)
and
V_{B}(t)

in terms of
b
- and
D
-operators found in Eqs. (
5.5
) and (
5.7
) –the
D_{k}^{*}b^{*}_{-k}
term
is re-written in terms of
D^{*}_{k}b_{k}
upon taking the real part of
\nu
.
Next, we estimate the two supremum quantity in (
8.23
).
In view of Remark
5.1
, it suffices to provide estimates for pure states
\Psi\in\mathscr{F}
.
In order to ease the notation, we omit the variables
t_{1},t_{2}\in[0,t]
and
k,\ell\in\textnormal{supp}\hat{V}
.
We shall make extensive use of Type-I to Type-IV estimates contained in Lemma
4.5
–
4.8
,
and the commutation relations from Lemmas
4.3
and
4.4
.

We expand the first commutator in terms of
D^{*}(\varphi)=[N(\varphi),D]
and
b(\varphi)=[N(\varphi),b]

as follows

(8.24)

\displaystyle[[N(\varphi),D^{*}b],D^{*}D]=[D^{*}(\varphi)b,D^{*}D]+[D^{*}b(\varphi),D^{*}D]\ .

We dedicate the rest of the proof to estimate the expectation of the
two terms in the right hand side of (
8.24
).

\blacksquare

The first term of (
8.24
)

We break up the second commutator into three pieces

(8.25)

\displaystyle[D^{*}(\varphi)b,D^{*}D]=D^{*}(\varphi)D^{*}[b,D]+D^{*}(\varphi)[b,D^{*}]D+[D^{*}(\varphi),D^{*}D]b

which we now estimate separately.

\blacklozenge

The first term of (
8.25
).

Letting
\Psi\in\mathscr{F}
, we find that

\displaystyle|\left\langle\Psi,D^{*}(\varphi)D^{*}[b,D]\Psi\right\rangle|

\displaystyle\ \leqslant\ \|DD(\varphi)\Psi\|\|[b,D]\Psi\|

(8.26)

\displaystyle\ \lesssim\ |\Lambda|\|\varphi\|_{\ell^{1}}R^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\mathcal{N}\Psi\|\|\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{S}}^{1/2}\Psi\|\ ,

where we used
the Type-I estimate for
D
,
the Type-II estimate for
[D,b]
,
the Type-IV estimate for
D(\varphi)
,
and the commutation relation
[\mathcal{N},D(\varphi)]=0
.

\blacklozenge

The second term of (
8.25
)
.
Letting
\Psi\in\mathscr{F}
, we find that

\displaystyle|\langle\Psi,D^{*}(\varphi)

\displaystyle[b,D^{*}]D\Psi\rangle|

\displaystyle\ \leqslant\ |\left\langle\Psi,D^{*}(\varphi)D[b,D^{*}]\Psi\right\rangle|+|\left\langle\Psi,D^{*}(\varphi)[[b,D^{*}],D]\Psi\right\rangle|

\displaystyle\ \lesssim\ \|D^{*}D(\varphi)\Psi\|\|[b,D^{*}]\Psi\|+\|D(\varphi)\|\|\Psi\|\|[[b,D^{*}],D]\Psi\|

(8.27)

\displaystyle\ \lesssim\ |\Lambda|\|\varphi\|_{\ell^{1}}R^{\frac{1}{2}}\|(\mathcal{N}+\mathds{1})\Psi\|\|\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{S}}^{1/2}\Psi\|\ ,

where we used
the Type-I estimate for
D^{*}
,
the Type-II estimate for
[b,D^{*}]
and
[[b,D^{*}],D]
,
the Type-IV estimate for
D(\varphi)
,
and the commutation relation
[\mathcal{N},D(\varphi)]=0.

\blacklozenge

The third term of (
8.25
)
.
Letting
\Psi\in\mathscr{F}
, we find that

\displaystyle|\left\langle\Psi,[D^{*}(\varphi),D^{*}D]b\Psi\right\rangle|

\displaystyle\ \leqslant\ \|[D(\varphi),D^{*}D]\Psi\|\|b\Psi\|

(8.28)

\displaystyle\ \lesssim\ |\Lambda|\|\varphi\|_{\ell^{1}}R^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\mathcal{N}\Psi\|\|\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{S}}^{1/2}\Psi\|\ ,

where we used
the Type-I estimates for
D
and
D^{*}
,
the Type-II estimate for
b
,
the Type-IV estimate for
[D(\varphi),D]
and
[D(\varphi),D^{*}]
,
and the commutation relation
[\mathcal{N},[D(\varphi),D]]=0.

Upon gathering the last three estimates, we find that the first term of (
8.24
)
satisfies the following upper bound

(8.29)

\displaystyle|\nu\big([D^{*}(\varphi)b,D^{*}D]\big)|\lesssim|\Lambda|\|\varphi\|_{\ell_{m}^{1}}R^{\frac{1}{2}}\nu(\mathcal{N}^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}\nu(\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{S}})^{\frac{1}{2}}\ .

\blacksquare

The second term of (
8.24
).

Similarly as before, we break up the double second into three pieces

(8.30)

\displaystyle[D^{*}b(\varphi),D^{*}D]=D^{*}D^{*}[b(\varphi),D]+D^{*}[b(\varphi),D^{*}]D+b(\varphi)[D^{*},D^{*}D]

which can be estimated as follows.

\blacklozenge

The first term in (
8.30
)
.
Letting
\Psi\in\mathscr{F}
, we find that

\displaystyle|\left\langle\Psi,D^{*}D^{*}[b(\varphi),D]\Psi\right\rangle|

\displaystyle\leqslant\|DD(\mathcal{N}+2)^{-1}\Psi\|\|(\mathcal{N}+2)[b(\varphi),D]\Psi\|

(8.31)

\displaystyle\lesssim|\Lambda|\|\varphi\|_{\ell^{1}_{m}}p_{F}^{-m}\|\mathcal{N}\Psi\|^{2}\ ,

where we used
the Type-I estimate for
D
,
the Type-III estimate for
[b(\varphi),D]

and the pull-through formula
(\mathcal{N}+2)[b(\varphi),D]=[b(\varphi),D]\mathcal{N}
.

\blacklozenge

Letting
\Psi\in\mathscr{F}
, we find that

\displaystyle|\left\langle\Psi,D^{*}[b(\varphi),D^{*}]D\Psi\right\rangle|

\displaystyle\ \leqslant\ \|D\Psi\|\|[b(\varphi),D^{*}]\|\|D\Psi\|

(8.32)

\displaystyle\ \lesssim\ |\Lambda|\|\varphi\|_{\ell^{1}_{m}}p_{F}^{-m}\|\mathcal{N}\Psi\|^{2}\ ,

where we used
the Type-I estimate for
D
,
and the Type-III estimate for
[b(\varphi),D^{*}].

\blacklozenge

Letting
\Psi\in\mathscr{F}
, we find that

\displaystyle|\left\langle\Psi,b(\varphi)[D^{*},D^{*}D]\Psi\right\rangle|

\displaystyle\ \leqslant\ \|b^{*}(\varphi)\mathcal{N}\Psi\|\|[D^{*},D^{*}D](\mathcal{N}+2)^{-1}\Psi\|

(8.33)

\displaystyle\ \lesssim\ |\Lambda|\|\varphi\|_{\ell^{1}_{m}}p_{F}^{-m}\|\mathcal{N}\Psi\|^{2}\ ,

where we used
the Type-I estimate for
[D^{*},D^{*}D]
,
the Type-III estimate for
b^{*}(\varphi)
, the pull-through formula
(\mathcal{N}+2)b(\varphi)=b(\varphi)\mathcal{N}

and the commutation relation
[D^{*},\mathcal{N}]=0
.

Upon gathering the last three estimates, we find that the second term of (
8.4
)
satisfies the following upper bound

(8.34)

\displaystyle\Big|\nu\Big([D^{*}b(\varphi),D^{*}D]\Big)\Big|\lesssim|\Lambda|\|\varphi\|_{\ell_{m}^{1}}p_{F}^{-m}\nu(\mathcal{N}^{2}).

\blacksquare

Conclusion
. The proof of the proposition is finished once we put together the estimates found in Eqs.
(
8.29
) and (
8.34
) back in
(
8.24
) .
∎

8.4.
Analysis of
T_{FB,B}

The main result of this subsection is the following proposition.
It contains an estimate on the size of
T_{FB,B}
.

Proposition 8.4

(Analysis of
T_{FB,B}
)
.

Let
T_{FB,B}(t,p)
be the quantity defined in (
3.21
)
with
\alpha=FB
, and
\beta=B
.
Further, let
m>0
.
Then, there exists a constant
C>0

such that for all
\varphi\in\ell_{m}^{1}

and

t\geqslant 0

such that

(8.35)

\displaystyle|T_{FB,B}(t,\varphi)|

\displaystyle\,\leqslant\,Ct^{2}\|\hat{V}\|_{\ell^{1}}^{2}|\Lambda|\|\varphi\|_{\ell^{1}_{m}}\sup_{0\leqslant\tau\leqslant t}\Big(R^{\frac{3}{2}}\,\nu_{\tau}(\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{S}})^{\frac{1}{2}}+R^{2}p_{F}^{-m}\nu_{\tau}(\mathcal{N}_{1}^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big)

where we recall
T_{FB,B}(t,\varphi)=\left\langle\varphi,T_{FB,B}(t)\right\rangle
and
R=|\Lambda|p_{F}^{d-1}
.

Proof.

For simplicity, we assume
\varphi
is real-valued–in the general case,
one may expand into real and imaginary parts and use linearity of the commutators.
Starting from (
3.23
) we use the self-adjointness of
V_{FB}(t)
,
V_{B}(t)
and
N(\varphi)=\int_{\Lambda^{*}}\varphi(p)a_{p}^{*}a_{p}\mathrm{d}p
to get
the elementary inequality

\displaystyle|T_{FB,B}(t,\varphi)|

\displaystyle=\Big|\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{t_{1}}\mathrm{Re}\,\nu_{t_{2}}\Big([[N(\varphi),V_{FB}(t_{1})],V_{B}(t_{2})]\Big)\mathrm{d}t_{1}\mathrm{d}t_{2}\Big|

(8.36)

\displaystyle\lesssim t^{2}\|\hat{V}\|_{\ell_{1}}\sup_{k\in\textnormal{supp}\hat{V},t_{i}\in[0,t]}\Big|\nu_{t_{2}}\Big([[N(\varphi),D_{k}^{*}(t_{1})b_{k}(t_{1})],V_{B}(t_{2})]\Big)\Big|

where in the last line we used the representation
of
V_{FB}(t)
in terms of
b
- and
D
-operators found in (
5.6
)–the
D_{k}^{*}b^{*}_{-k}
term
is re-written in terms of
D^{*}_{k}b_{k}
upon taking the real part of
\nu
.
Next, we estimate the two supremum quantity in (
8.36
).
In view of Remark
5.1
, it suffices to provide estimates for pure states
\Psi\in\mathscr{F}
.
In order to ease the notation, we omit the variables
t_{1},t_{2}\in[0,t]
.
We shall make extensive use of Type-I to Type-IV estimates contained in Lemma
4.5
–
4.8
,
and the commutation relations from Lemmas
4.3
and
4.4
.

In terms of
D_{k}^{*}(\varphi)=[N(\varphi),D_{k}]
and
b_{k}(\varphi)=[N(\varphi),b_{k}]

we calculate the first commutator to be

(8.37)

[[N(\varphi),D^{*}_{k}b_{k}],V_{B}]=[D^{*}_{k}(\varphi)b_{k},V_{B}]+[D^{*}_{k}b_{k}(\varphi),V_{B}],\qquad\forall k\in\textnormal{supp}\hat{V}\ .

We shall estimate the expectation of the two terms in (
8.37
) separately.

\blacksquare

The first term of (
8.37
).

We expand
V_{B}
into three additional terms. Namely

\displaystyle[D_{k}^{*}(\varphi)b_{k},V_{B}]

\displaystyle=\int_{\Lambda^{*}}\hat{V}(\ell)\Big([D_{k}^{*}(\varphi)b_{k},b_{\ell}^{*}b_{\ell}]+\frac{1}{2}[D_{k}^{*}(\varphi)b_{k},b_{\ell}b_{-\ell}]+\frac{1}{2}[D_{k}^{*}(\varphi)b_{k},b_{-\ell}^{*}b_{\ell}^{*}]\Big)\mathrm{d}\ell

(8.38)

\displaystyle\equiv\int_{\Lambda^{*}}\hat{V}(\ell)\Big(C_{1}(k,\ell)+C_{2}(k,\ell)+C_{3}(k,\ell)\Big)\mathrm{d}\ell\ .

Next, we proceed to analyze the commutators
C_{j}
for
j=1,2,3
separately.

\blacklozenge

Analysis of
C_{1}
. We expand the commutator

(8.39)

C_{1}(k,\ell)=D_{k}^{*}(\varphi)[b_{k},b_{\ell}^{*}]b_{\ell}+[D_{k}^{*}(\varphi),b_{\ell}^{*}b_{\ell}]b_{k}\ .

Let us recall that the
[b_{k}(t),b_{\ell}^{*}(s)]

can be calculated explicitly – see (
4.1
).
In particular, it can be easily verified that for
k,\ell\in\textnormal{supp}\hat{V}
it satisfies the estimate

(8.40)

\|[b_{k}(t),b_{\ell}^{*}(s)]\|_{B(\mathscr{F})}\lesssim R

Consequently,
C_{1}

can be estimated as follows.
Omitting momentarily the variables
k,\ell\in\textnormal{supp}\hat{V}
we find

\displaystyle\big|\left\langle\Psi,C_{1}\Psi\right\rangle\big|

\displaystyle\leqslant\big|\left\langle[b,b^{*}]D(\varphi)\Psi,b\Psi\right\rangle\big|+\big|\left\langle\Psi,[D^{*}(\varphi),b^{*}b]b\Psi\right\rangle\big|

\displaystyle\leqslant\|[b,b^{*}]\|\,\|D^{*}(\varphi)\Psi\|\,\|b\Psi\|+\|[D^{*}(\varphi),b^{*}b]\|\|\Psi\|\|b\Psi\|

(8.41)

\displaystyle\lesssim R|\Lambda|\|\varphi\|_{\ell^{1}}\|\Psi\|R^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{S}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\Psi\|+|\Lambda|p_{F}^{-m}\|\varphi\|_{\ell^{1}_{m}}R^{2}\|\Psi\|^{2}\ ,

where we used the
Type-II estimate for
b
,
the Type-III estimate for
[D^{*}(\varphi),b]
and
[D^{*}(\varphi),b^{*}]
,
the Type-IV estimate for
D^{*}(\varphi)
,
the norm bound
\|b\|\lesssim R

and the commutator bound (
8.40
).

\blacklozenge

Analysis of
C_{2}
.
This one is easier to estimate, because we do not pick a non-zero commutator between the
b
operators. Namely,
there holds
C_{2}(k,\ell)=[D_{k}^{*}(\varphi),b_{\ell}b_{-\ell}]b_{k}.

Thus, we find (ommitting the
k,\ell\in\textnormal{supp}\hat{V}
variables)

(8.42)

\displaystyle|\left\langle\Psi,C_{2}\Psi\right\rangle|\lesssim|\Lambda|\|\varphi\|_{\ell^{1}}\|\hat{V}\|_{\ell^{1}}^{2}R^{2}p_{F}^{-m}\|\Psi\|^{2}\ .

\blacklozenge

Analysis of
C_{3}
.
This is the most intricate term among the three terms we analyze, because it involves higher-order commutators.
First we decompose

\displaystyle C_{3}(k,\ell)

\displaystyle=D_{k}^{*}(\varphi)b_{-\ell}^{*}[b_{k},b_{\ell}^{*}]+D_{k}^{*}(\varphi)[b_{k},b_{-\ell}^{*}]b_{\ell}^{*}+[D_{k}^{*}(\varphi),b_{-\ell}^{*}b_{\ell}^{*}]b_{k}

(8.43)

\displaystyle\equiv C_{3,1}(k,\ell)+C_{3,2}(k,\ell)+C_{3,3}(k,\ell)\

and analyze each term separately.

Let us look at the first one. Omitting the
k,\ell\in\textnormal{supp}\hat{V}
variables we find

\displaystyle\big|\left\langle\Psi,C_{3,1}\Psi\right\rangle\big|

\displaystyle=\big|\left\langle bD(\varphi)\Psi,[b,b^{*}]\Psi\right\rangle\big|

\displaystyle\leqslant\|bD(\varphi)\Psi\|\|[b,b^{*}]\Psi\|

\displaystyle\leqslant\|[b,D(\varphi)]\|\|\Psi\|\|[b,b^{*}]\Psi\|+\|D(\varphi)\|\|b\Psi\|\|[b,b^{*}]\Psi\|

\displaystyle\lesssim(|\Lambda|p_{F}^{-m}\|\varphi\|_{\ell_{m}^{1}})R\|\Psi\|^{2}+|\Lambda|\|\varphi\|_{\ell^{1}}R^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{S}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\Psi\|R\|\Psi\|

(8.44)

\displaystyle\leqslant|\Lambda|\|\varphi\|_{\ell^{1}_{m}}\Big(R^{\frac{3}{2}}\|\Psi\|\|\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{S}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\Psi\|+Rp_{F}^{-m}\|\Psi\|^{2}\Big)

where we used
the Type-II estimates for
b
,
the Type-III estimate for
[b,D(\varphi)]
,
and the commutator bound
\|[b,b^{*}]\|\leqslant R
, see Eq. (
8.40
).

Let us now look at the second one.
Let us recall that the boson commutator can be written
as
[b_{k},b_{\ell}^{*}]=\delta(k-\ell)G_{k}\mathds{1}+\mathcal{R}_{k,\ell}

where
G_{k}
is a scalar, and
\mathcal{R}_{k,\ell}
is a reminder operator (see (
7.14
) for details).
Thus, we find

\displaystyle\big|\langle\Psi,C_{3,2}(k,\ell)\Psi\rangle\big|

\displaystyle\leqslant\big|\left\langle\Psi,C_{3,1}(k,-\ell)\Psi\right\rangle\big|+\big|\left\langle\Psi,D_{k}^{*}(\varphi)[\mathcal{R}_{k,-\ell},b_{\ell}]\Psi\right\rangle\big|

\displaystyle\leqslant|\Lambda|\|\varphi\|_{\ell^{1}_{m}}\Big(R^{\frac{3}{2}}\|\Psi\|\|\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{S}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\Psi\|+Rp_{F}^{-m}\|\Psi\|^{2}\Big)

(8.45)

\displaystyle\quad+|\Lambda|\|\varphi\|_{\ell^{1}}R^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\Psi\|\|\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{S}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\Psi\|\ .

where in the last line we used the upper bound for
C_{3,1}(k,\ell)
,
the Type-IV estimate for
D_{k}^{*}(\varphi)
,
and the following commutator estimate

(8.46)

\|[\mathcal{R}_{k,\ell},b_{-\ell}]\Psi\|\lesssim R^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{S}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\Psi\|

valid for
k,\ell\in\textnormal{supp}\hat{V}
.

Let us now look at the third one. Omitting the
k,\ell\in\textnormal{supp}\hat{V}
variables we find

(8.47)

\displaystyle\big|\left\langle\Psi,C_{3,3}\Psi\right\rangle\big|\leqslant 2\|b^{*}\|\|[D^{*}(\varphi),b^{*}]\|\|\Psi\|\|b\Psi\|\lesssim|\Lambda|\|\varphi\|_{\ell^{1}_{m}}R^{2}p_{F}^{-m}\|\Psi\|^{2}\ .

where we used the Type-III estimate for
[D^{*}(\varphi),b^{*}]
,
and the norm bounds
\|b\|,\|b^{*}\|\lesssim R.

Putting the estimates for
C_{3,1}
,
C_{3,2}
and
C_{3,3}
we finally find that for all

k,\ell\in\textnormal{supp}\hat{V}
there holds

(8.48)

\displaystyle\big|\left\langle\Psi,C_{3}(k,\ell)\Psi\right\rangle\big|\lesssim|\Lambda|\|\varphi\|_{\ell^{1}}\Big(R^{\frac{3}{2}}\|\Psi\|\|\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{S}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\Psi\|+R^{2}p_{F}^{-m}\|\Psi\|^{2}\Big)\ .

Finally, we put the estimates (
8.41
), (
8.42
) and (
8.48
) for
C_{1}
,
C_{2}
and
C_{3}
, respectively,
to find that
the expectation of the first term in (
8.37
)
is bounded above by

(8.49)

\displaystyle\Big|\nu\big([D_{k}^{*}(\varphi)b_{k},V_{B}]\big)\Big|\leqslant|\Lambda|\|\varphi\|_{\ell^{1}_{m}}\|\hat{V}\|_{\ell^{1}}\Big(R^{\frac{3}{2}}\nu(\mathds{1})^{\frac{1}{2}}\nu(\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{S}})^{\frac{1}{2}}+R^{2}p_{F}^{-m}\nu(\mathds{1})\Big)\ .

\blacksquare

The second term of (
8.37
).

This one is easy, we use the brutal estimate

(8.50)

\displaystyle|\nu\Big([D_{k}^{*}b_{k}(\varphi),V_{B}]\Big)|\leqslant|\nu\big(D_{k}^{*}b_{k}(\varphi)V_{B}\big)|+|\nu\big(V_{B}D_{k}^{*}b_{k}(\varphi)\big)|

We estimate these two terms as follows.

\blacklozenge

In view of

\|V_{B}\|_{B(\mathscr{F})}\lesssim\|\hat{V}\|_{\ell^{1}}R^{2}

we find
for the first term in (
8.50
) that

\displaystyle\big|\left\langle\Psi,D_{k}^{*}b_{k}(\varphi)V_{B}\Psi\right\rangle\big|

\displaystyle\leqslant\|b_{k}^{*}(\varphi)D_{k}\Psi\|\|V_{B}\Psi\|

\displaystyle\leqslant\|\hat{V}\|_{\ell^{1}}\|b^{*}_{k}(\varphi)\|\|\mathcal{N}\Psi\|R^{2}\|\Psi\|

(8.51)

\displaystyle\leqslant|\Lambda|\|\hat{V}\|_{\ell^{1}}p_{F}^{-m}\|\varphi\|_{\ell^{1}_{m}}R^{2}\|\mathcal{N}\Psi\|\|\Psi\|\ ,

where we used the Type-I estimate for
D_{k}
,
and the Type-III estimate for
b_{k}^{*}(\varphi)
.

\blacklozenge

For the second term in (
8.50
),
we use the same bound for
V_{B}
,
together with the
pull through formula
\mathcal{N}b(\varphi)=b(\varphi)(\mathcal{N}-2)

to find that

\displaystyle\big|\left\langle\Psi,V_{B}D_{k}^{*}b_{k}(\varphi)\Psi\right\rangle\big|

\displaystyle\leqslant\|V_{B}\Psi\|\|D_{k}^{*}(\mathcal{N}+2)^{-1}\|\|(\mathcal{N}+2)b_{k}(\varphi)\Psi\|

\displaystyle\leqslant\|\hat{V}\|_{\ell^{1}}R^{2}\|\Psi\|\|b_{k}(\varphi)\mathcal{N}\Psi\|

(8.52)

\displaystyle\leqslant\|\hat{V}\|_{\ell^{1}}R^{2}|\Lambda|\|\varphi\|_{\ell_{m}^{1}}p_{F}^{-m}\|\Psi\|\|\mathcal{N}\Psi\|\ .

Here, we used the Type-I estimate for
D_{k}^{*}
,
and the Type-III estimate for

b_{k}(\varphi)
.

These last two estimates combined together then imply that

(8.53)

\displaystyle|\nu\big([D_{k}^{*}b_{k}(\varphi),V_{B}]\big)|\leqslant\|\hat{V}\|_{\ell^{1}}R^{2}|\Lambda|\|\varphi\|_{\ell_{m}^{1}}p_{F}^{-m}\nu(\mathds{1})^{\frac{1}{2}}\nu(\mathcal{N}^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}\ .

\blacksquare

Conclusion.

The proof of the proposition is finished once we gather the estimates contained in (
8.49
) and (
8.53
),
and plug them back in (
8.36
).
∎

8.5.
Analysis of
T_{B,\alpha}

Out of the nine terms
T_{\alpha,\beta}(t,\varphi)
,
those with
\alpha=B
are the easiest ones to deal with.
The main result of this subsection is contained in the following proposition.
It contains an estimate for the three terms
T_{B,F}
,
T_{B,FB}
and
T_{B,B}
.

Proposition 8.5

(Analysis of
T_{B,F}
,
T_{B,FB}
and
T_{B,B}
)
.

Let
T_{B,F}(t,p)
,

T_{B,FB}(t,p)

and

T_{B,FB}(t,p)

be the quantities defined in (
3.21
),
for
\alpha=B

and
\beta=F
,

\beta=FB

and

\beta=B
, respectively.
Further, let
m>0
.
Then, there exists a constant
C>0

such that for all
\varphi\in\ell_{m}^{1}

and
t\geqslant 0

there holds

\displaystyle|T_{B,F}(t,\varphi)|+|T_{B,FB}

\displaystyle(t,\varphi)|+|T_{B,B}(t,\varphi)|

(8.54)

\displaystyle\leqslant Ct^{2}\|\hat{V}\|_{\ell^{1}}^{2}|\Lambda|\|\varphi\|_{\ell^{1}_{m}}R^{3}p_{F}^{-m}\sup_{0\leqslant\tau\leqslant t}\Big(1+R^{-2}\nu_{\tau}(\mathcal{N}^{4})^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big)\ ,

where we recall

T_{\alpha,\beta}(t,\varphi)=\left\langle\varphi,T_{\alpha,\beta}(t)\right\rangle

and

R=|\Lambda|p_{F}^{d-1}
.

Proof.

In what follows, we let
\alpha
be either
F
,
FB
or
B
,
and we fix
m>0
,
t\geqslant 0
and
\varphi\in\ell_{m}^{1}
.
Starting from (
3.21
) one finds the following elementary bound

(8.55)

\displaystyle|T_{B,\alpha}(t,\varphi)|\lesssim t^{2}\sup_{t_{i}\in[0,t]}\big|\nu_{t_{2}}\big([[N(\varphi),V_{B}(t_{1})],V_{\alpha}(t_{2})]\big)\big|

and so it suffices to estimate the supremum quantity in the above inequality.
In view of Remark
5.1
,
it suffices to consider estimates on pure states
\Psi\in\mathscr{F}
.
In order to ease the notation, we drop the time variables
t_{1},t_{2}\in[0,t]
.
Thus, we find that

(8.56)

\displaystyle|\left\langle\Psi,[[N(\varphi),V_{B}],V_{\alpha}]\Psi\right\rangle|\leqslant 2|\left\langle\Psi,[[N(\varphi),V_{B}]V_{\alpha}\Psi\right\rangle|\leqslant 2\|[N(\varphi),V_{B}]\|\|\Psi\|\|V_{\alpha}\Psi\|

Using the expansion of
V_{B}
in terms of
b
-operators (see (
5.7
)),
it is straightforward to find that, in terms of
b_{k}(\varphi)=[N(\varphi),b_{k}]
,

(8.57)

\|[N(\varphi),V_{B}]\|\leqslant 2\|\hat{V}\|_{\ell^{1}}\|b\|\|b_{k}(\varphi)\|\lesssim\|\hat{V}\|_{\ell^{1}}R|\Lambda|p_{F}^{-m}\|\varphi\|_{\ell^{1}_{m}}

where we used the Type-III estimate on
b_{k}(\varphi)
(see Lemma
4.7
),
together with the norm bound
\|b_{k}\|\lesssim R
.
On the other hand, we have previously established the estimate

(8.58)

\|V_{\alpha}\Psi\|\lesssim\|\hat{V}\|_{\ell^{1}}\Big(\|\mathcal{N}^{2}\Psi\|+R^{2}\|\Psi\|\Big)\ .

The proof is finished once we gather the last four estimates.
∎

9.
Proof of Theorem
1

We are now ready to give a proof of our main result, Theorem
1
.
We shall make extensive use of the excitation estimates
established in Section
5
.
Namely, letting
(\nu_{t})_{t\in\mathbb{R}}

be the interaction dynamics (
3.14
) with initial data satisfying Condition
2
,
we know that for all
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
exists a constant
C>0
such that
for all
t\geqslant 0
there holds

(9.1)

\displaystyle\nu_{t}(\mathcal{N}^{\ell})

\displaystyle\leqslant Cn^{\ell}\exp(C\lambda Rt)\ ,

(9.2)

\displaystyle\nu_{t}(\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{S}})

\displaystyle\leqslant(\lambda R\left\langle t\right\rangle)^{2}\exp(C\lambda Rt)\ .

Here,
n=\nu_{0}(\mathcal{N})\lesssim R^{1/2}
is the initial number of particles/holes in the system, and

R=|\Lambda|p_{F}^{d-1}
is our recurrent parameter.

Proof.

Throughout the proof, we shall fix the parameter
m>0
.
Let
f_{t}(p)
be the momentum distribution of the system, as defined in Def.
1
.
In Section
3
,
we performed a double commutator expansion of
f_{t}(p)
, given in (
3.20
),
in terms of the quantities
T_{\alpha,\beta}(t,p)
, defined in Eq. (
3.21
).
It then follows by the triangle inequality that
for all
t\geqslant 0

\displaystyle\big\|f_{t}-f_{0}-\lambda^{2}t\,\big(Q_{t}[f_{0}]

\displaystyle+B_{t}[f_{0}]\big)\big\|_{\ell_{m}^{1*}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{\lambda^{2}}{|\Lambda|}\Big(\big\|T_{F,F}(t)+t|\Lambda|Q_{t}[f_{0}]\big\|_{\ell_{m}^{1*}}+\big\|T_{FB,FB}(t)+t|\Lambda|B_{t}[f_{0}]\big\|_{\ell_{m}^{1*}}\Big)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{\lambda^{2}}{|\Lambda|}\Big(\|T_{F,FB}(t)\|_{\ell_{m}^{1*}}+\|T_{F,B}(t)\|_{\ell_{m}^{1*}}\Big)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{\lambda^{2}}{|\Lambda|}\Big(\|T_{FB,F}(t)\|_{\ell_{m}^{1*}}+\|T_{FB,B}(t)\|_{\ell_{m}^{1*}}\Big)

(9.3)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{\lambda^{2}}{|\Lambda|}\Big(\|T_{B,F}(t)\|_{\ell_{m}^{1*}}+\|T_{B,FB}(t)\|_{\ell_{m}^{1*}}+\|T_{B,B}(t)\|_{\ell_{m}^{1*}}\Big)\

where
Q_{t}
and
B_{t}

are the operators defined in Def.
3
and
2
, respectively.
We shall now estimate the right hand side of (
9.3
).
First, we estimate the leading order terms, previously analyzed in Section
6
and
7
.
Secondly, we describe the subleading order terms, previously analyzed in Section
8
.

Leading order terms.

First, we collect the Boltzmann-like dynamics.
This term emerges from
T_{F,F}
.
Indeed, it follows from Proposition
6.1
and
Eq. (
9.1
)
that
there exists a constant
C>0

such that for all
t\geqslant 0

\displaystyle\|T_{F,F}(t)+t|\Lambda|Q_{t}[f_{0}]\|_{\ell_{m}^{1*}}

\displaystyle\leqslant C|\Lambda|t^{3}\lambda\sup_{\tau\leqslant t}\Big(R^{2}\nu_{\tau}(\mathcal{N}^{4})^{\frac{1}{2}}+\nu_{\tau}(\mathcal{N}^{4})\Big)

\displaystyle\leqslant C|\Lambda|t^{3}\lambda(R^{2}+n^{2})n^{2}\exp(C\lambda Rt)

(9.4)

\displaystyle\leqslant C|\Lambda|t^{3}\lambda R^{2}n^{2}\exp(C\lambda Rt)\ ,

where we have used the assumption
n\lesssim R
.

Now, we collect the interactions between holes/particles and bosonized particle-hole pairs around the Fermi surface.
In view of Proposition
7.1
and
Eqs. (
9.1
) and (
9.2
)
we find that there exists a constant
C>0

such that for all
t\geqslant 0
there holds

\displaystyle\|T_{FB,FB}(t)+t|\Lambda|

\displaystyle B_{t}[f_{0}]\|_{\ell_{m}^{1*}}

\displaystyle\leqslant C|\Lambda|t^{2}\sup_{\tau\leqslant t}\Big[R^{\frac{1}{2}}\nu_{\tau}(\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{S}})^{\frac{1}{2}}\nu_{\tau}(\mathcal{N})^{\frac{1}{2}}+R^{\frac{3}{2}}\nu_{\tau}(\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{S}})^{\frac{1}{2}}+\frac{R}{p_{F}^{m}}\nu_{\tau}(\mathcal{N}^{2})\Big]

\displaystyle\qquad+C|\Lambda|t^{3}\lambda R\sup_{\tau\leqslant t}\Big[R^{\frac{3}{2}}\nu_{\tau}(\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{S}})^{\frac{1}{2}}+R\nu_{\tau}(\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{S}})+\frac{R}{p_{F}^{m}}\nu_{\tau}(\mathcal{N})^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big]\ ,

\displaystyle\leqslant C|\Lambda|t^{2}\Big[R^{\frac{1}{2}}\lambda R\left\langle t\right\rangle n^{\frac{1}{2}}+R^{\frac{3}{2}}\lambda R\left\langle t\right\rangle+\frac{Rn^{2}}{p_{F}^{m}}\Big]e^{C\lambda Rt}

\displaystyle\qquad+C|\Lambda|t^{3}\lambda R\Big[R^{\frac{3}{2}}\lambda R\left\langle t\right\rangle+R(\lambda R\left\langle t\right\rangle)^{2}+\frac{Rn^{\frac{1}{2}}}{p_{F}^{m}}\Big]e^{C\lambda Rt}\ ,

\displaystyle\leqslant C|\Lambda|\Big[t^{2}\left\langle t\right\rangle\lambda R^{\frac{3}{2}}n^{\frac{1}{2}}+t^{2}\left\langle t\right\rangle\lambda R^{\frac{5}{2}}+t^{2}\frac{Rn^{2}}{p_{F}^{m}}\Big]e^{C\lambda Rt}

(9.5)

\displaystyle\qquad+C|\Lambda|\Big[t^{3}\left\langle t\right\rangle\lambda^{2}R^{\frac{7}{2}}+t^{3}\left\langle t\right\rangle^{2}\lambda^{3}R^{4}+t^{3}\frac{\lambda R^{2}n^{\frac{1}{2}}}{p_{F}^{m}}\Big]e^{C\lambda Rt}\ .

Under the assumptions
1\lesssim n\lesssim R
we find the following upper bound,
for some constant
C>0
.
Note that we absorb polynomias on the variable
\lambda R\left\langle t\right\rangle
into the exponential factor
\exp(C\lambda R\left\langle t\right\rangle)
, after updating the constant
C
.

\displaystyle\|T_{FB,FB}(t)

\displaystyle+t|\Lambda|B_{t}[f_{0}]\|_{\ell_{m}^{1*}}

\displaystyle\leqslant C|\Lambda|\Big[\lambda t^{2}\left\langle t\right\rangle R^{\frac{5}{2}}\Big(1+\lambda R\left\langle t\right\rangle+R^{-\frac{1}{2}}(\lambda R\left\langle t\right\rangle)^{2}\Big)+\frac{t^{2}Rn^{2}}{p_{F}^{m}}\big(1+\lambda Rt\big)\Big]e^{C\lambda R\left\langle t\right\rangle}

(9.6)

\displaystyle\leqslant C|\Lambda|\Big(\lambda t^{2}\left\langle t\right\rangle R^{\frac{5}{2}}+\frac{t^{2}Rn^{2}}{p_{F}^{m}}\Big)e^{C\lambda R\left\langle t\right\rangle}\ .

Subleading order terms.

In the expansion given by (
3.20
) we have already analyzed the leading order terms given by
T_{F,F}(t)
and
T_{FB,FB}(t)
.
The remainding seven terms are regarded as subleading order terms.
These can be estimated as follows.

Using Proposition
8.1

and Eqs. (
9.1
) and (
9.2
),
we find
that there is a constant
C>0
such that

\displaystyle\|T_{F,FB}(t)\|_{\ell_{m}^{1*}}

\displaystyle\leqslant Ct^{2}|\Lambda|\sup_{0\leqslant\tau\leqslant t}\Big(R^{\frac{1}{2}}\,\nu_{\tau}(\mathcal{N}^{2})^{1/2}\nu_{\tau}(\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{S}})^{1/2}+p_{F}^{-m}\nu_{\tau}(\mathcal{N}^{2})\Big)

\displaystyle\leqslant Ct^{2}|\Lambda|\Big(R^{\frac{1}{2}}\,n^{2}(\lambda R\left\langle t\right\rangle)+p_{F}^{-m}n^{2}\Big)e^{C\lambda Rt}

(9.7)

\displaystyle\leqslant C|\Lambda|\Big(\lambda t^{2}\left\langle t\right\rangle R^{\frac{3}{2}}n^{2}+\frac{n^{2}t^{2}}{p_{F}^{m}}\Big)e^{C\lambda Rt}\ .

Using Proposition
8.2

and Eqs. (
9.1
) and (
9.2
),
we find
that there is a constant
C>0
such that

\displaystyle\|T_{F,B}(t)\|_{\ell_{m}^{1*}}

\displaystyle\leqslant Ct^{2}|\Lambda|\sup_{0\leqslant\tau\leqslant t}\Big(R^{\frac{3}{2}}\nu_{\tau}(\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{S}})^{\frac{1}{2}}+R\nu_{\tau}(\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{S}})+Rp_{F}^{-m}\nu(\mathcal{N}^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big)\ .

\displaystyle\leqslant Ct^{2}|\Lambda|\Big(R^{\frac{3}{2}}\lambda R\left\langle t\right\rangle+R(\lambda R\left\langle t\right\rangle)^{2}+\frac{Rn}{p_{F}^{m}}\Big)e^{C\lambda Rt}

\displaystyle\leqslant C|\Lambda|\Big(\lambda t^{2}\left\langle t\right\rangle R^{\frac{5}{2}}\big(1+\lambda R^{\frac{1}{2}}\left\langle t\right\rangle\big)+\frac{Rnt^{2}}{p_{F}^{m}}\Big)e^{C\lambda Rt}

(9.8)

\displaystyle\leqslant C|\Lambda|\Big(\lambda t^{2}\left\langle t\right\rangle R^{\frac{5}{2}}+\frac{Rnt^{2}}{p_{F}^{m}}\Big)e^{C\lambda R\left\langle t\right\rangle}\ .

Using Proposition
8.3

and Eqs. (
9.1
) and (
9.2
),
we find
that there is a constant
C>0
such that

\displaystyle\|T_{FB,F}(t)\|_{\ell_{m}^{1*}}

\displaystyle\leqslant Ct^{2}|\Lambda|\sup_{0\leqslant\tau\leqslant t}\Big(R^{\frac{1}{2}}\nu_{\tau}(\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{S}})^{\frac{1}{2}}+p_{F}^{-m}\nu_{\tau}(\mathcal{N}^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big)\nu_{\tau}(\mathcal{N}^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leqslant Ct^{2}|\Lambda|\Big(R^{1/2}(\lambda R\left\langle t\right\rangle)n+\frac{n^{2}}{p_{F}^{m}}\Big)e^{C\lambda Rt}

(9.9)

\displaystyle\leqslant C|\Lambda|\Big(\lambda t^{2}\left\langle t\right\rangle R^{\frac{3}{2}}n+\frac{n^{2}t^{2}}{p_{F}^{m}}\Big)e^{C\lambda Rt}\ .

Using Proposition
8.4

and Eqs. (
9.1
) and (
9.2
),
we find
that there is a constant
C>0
such that

\displaystyle\|T_{FB,B}(t)\|_{\ell_{m}^{1*}}

\displaystyle\leqslant Ct^{2}|\Lambda|\sup_{0\leqslant\tau\leqslant t}\Big(R^{\frac{3}{2}}\,\nu_{\tau}(\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{S}})^{\frac{1}{2}}+R^{2}p_{F}^{-m}\nu_{\tau}(\mathcal{N}^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big)

\displaystyle\leqslant Ct^{2}|\Lambda|\Big(R^{\frac{3}{2}}(\lambda R\left\langle t\right\rangle)+\frac{R^{2}n}{p_{F}^{m}}\Big)e^{C\lambda Rt}

(9.10)

\displaystyle\leqslant C|\Lambda|\Big(\lambda t^{2}\left\langle t\right\rangle R^{\frac{5}{2}}+\frac{R^{2}nt^{2}}{p_{F}^{m}}\Big)e^{C\lambda Rt}\ .

Using Proposition
and Eqs. (
9.1
) and (
9.2
),
we find
that there is a constant
C>0
such that

(9.11)

\displaystyle\|T_{B}(t)\|_{\ell_{m}^{1*}}

\displaystyle\leqslant C|\Lambda|t^{2}R^{3}p_{F}^{-m}\sup_{0\leqslant\tau\leqslant t}\Big(1+R^{-2}\nu_{\tau}(\mathcal{N}^{4})^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big)\leqslant C|\Lambda|t^{2}\frac{R^{3}}{p_{F}^{m}}e^{C\lambda Rt}\ ,

where we have additionally used the fact that
1\lesssim n\lesssim R.

Conclusion.

It suffices now to gather all the estimates
for the leading and subleading order terms, and plug them back in the expansion given in Eq. (
9.3
)
for the momentum distribution of the system.
This finishes the proof of our main theorem.
∎

10.
Collision operator estimates

In this section, we prove the inequalities that were stated in Section
2

concerning the
three-dimensional torus
\Lambda
of fixed length
L>0
.
We establish three lemmas in arbitrary dimension
d\geqslant 1
,
and specialize to
d=3
when constructing the initial data.

10.1.
The delta function

First, we recall that
\delta_{t}(x)

is the mollified Delta function, defined in (
2.21
).
Here, we prove the following approximation lemma.

Lemma 10.1
.

There is
C>0
such that for all
|x|\geqslant(\frac{2\pi}{L})^{2}
,

|y|\leqslant\frac{|x|}{2\lambda}

and
t>0

(10.1)

|\delta_{t}(x+\lambda y)-(2/\pi)t\delta_{x,0}|\leqslant C\,\frac{(1-\delta_{x,0})}{x^{2}}\frac{1}{t}+C\delta_{x,0}\lambda^{2}t^{3}|y|^{2}\ .

Proof.

We consider the decomposition

(10.2)

\delta_{t}(x+\lambda y)=\delta_{x,0}\delta_{t}(\lambda y)+(1-\delta_{x,0})\delta_{t}(x+\lambda y)\ .

The first term in (
10.2
)
is estimated as follows.
Using
\delta_{t}(0)=2t/\pi
, we find that

(10.3)

\displaystyle|\delta_{t}(\lambda y)-2t/\pi|=t|\delta_{1}(t\lambda y)-\delta_{1}(0)|\leqslant Ct(t\lambda|y|)^{2}\ .

In the last line,
C>0
is a constant that verifies
|\delta_{1}(z)-\delta_{1}(0)|\leqslant C|z|^{2}

for all
z\in\mathbb{R}
–the constant exists because
\delta_{1}^{\prime}(0)=0
, and
\delta_{1}(z)
is globally bounded.
The second term in (
10.2
) is estimated as follows.
For
|x|\geqslant 1
and
\lambda|y|\leqslant 1/2
we have

(10.4)

\displaystyle\delta_{t}(x+\lambda y)\leqslant\frac{2/\pi}{t(x+\lambda y)^{2}}\leqslant\frac{2/\pi}{tx^{2}(1-|x|^{-1}\lambda|y|)^{2}}\leqslant\frac{C}{tx^{2}}\ .

The proof is finished once we put all the inequalities together.
∎

10.2.
Operator estimates

Let us now analyze the time dependence of the operators
Q_{t}
and
B_{t}
.

Let us recall that
Q_{t}
was defined in Def.
3
, and the time independent operator

\mathscr{Q}
is defined in the same way,
but with the discrete Delta function

(2/\pi)\delta_{\mathbb{Z}}(\Delta e)

replacing the energy mollifier
\delta_{t}(\Delta E)
.
Here,
\Delta E
corresponds to the dispersion relation (
2.20
),
whereas
\Delta e
corresponds to (signed) free dispersion

e(p)=(\chi^{\perp}(p)-\chi(p))\,p^{2}/2.

We shall prove that, under our assumptions for
\hat{V}
, the following result is true.

Lemma 10.2

(Analysis of
Q_{t}
)
.

Assuming that
0<\lambda\|\hat{V}\|_{\ell_{1}}\leqslant\frac{1}{2}(\frac{2\pi}{L})^{2}
, there is
C=C(V)>0
such that for all
f\in\ell^{1}(\Lambda^{*})
and
t>0
there holds

(10.5)

\displaystyle\|Q_{t}[f]-t\mathscr{Q}[f]\|_{\ell^{\infty}}\leqslant

\displaystyle Ct\big(1/t^{2}+(\lambda t)^{2}\big)\|\widetilde{f}\|_{\ell^{\infty}}^{2}\|f\|_{\ell^{1}}\|f\|_{\ell^{\infty}}\ .

Proof.

Starting from the definition of
Q_{t}[f]
, one finds
after evaluating the delta functions
\delta(p-p_{1})+\delta(p-p_{2})-\delta(p-p_{3})-\delta(p-p_{4})

that

(10.6)

\displaystyle Q_{t}[f]-t\mathscr{Q}[f]=R_{t}^{+}[f]-R_{t}^{-}[f]

where on the right hand side we have two remainder terms, corresponding to a gain, and a loss term.
Namely, for
p\in\Lambda^{*}
we have

(10.7)

\displaystyle R_{t}^{+}[f](p)

\displaystyle=\pi\int\sigma(\vec{p})\Big(\delta_{t}(\Delta E)-2t/\pi\delta_{\Delta e,0}\Big)f(p_{3})f(p_{4})\widetilde{f}(p_{2})\widetilde{f}(p)\,\mathrm{d}p_{2}\mathrm{d}p_{3}\mathrm{d}p_{4}\ ,

(10.8)

\displaystyle R_{t}^{-}[f](p)

\displaystyle=\pi\int\sigma(\vec{p})\Big(\delta_{t}(\Delta E)-2t/\pi\delta_{\Delta e,0}\Big)f(p)f(p_{2})\widetilde{f}(p_{3})\widetilde{f}(p_{4})\,\mathrm{d}p_{2}\mathrm{d}p_{3}\mathrm{d}p_{4}\ .

Here, we have denoted
\vec{p}=(p,p_{2},p_{3},p_{4})
,

\Delta E=E(p)+E(p_{2})-E(p_{3})-E(p_{4})

and

\Delta e\equiv\frac{1}{2}(p^{2}+p_{2}^{2}-p_{3}^{2}-p_{4}^{2})
.
Lemma
10.1
with
x=\Delta e

and
y=\mathcal{O}(\|\hat{V}\|_{\ell^{1}})
now implies that there is
C>0
such that

(10.9)

\displaystyle|R_{t}^{+}[f](p)|

\displaystyle\leqslant C(1/t+\lambda^{2}t^{3}\|\hat{V}\|_{\ell^{1}}^{2})\|\widetilde{f}\|_{\ell^{\infty}}^{2}\int\sigma(\vec{p})\,|f(p_{3})|\,|f(p_{4})|\ \mathrm{d}p_{2}\mathrm{d}p_{3}\mathrm{d}p_{4}\ ,

(10.10)

\displaystyle|R_{t}^{-}[f](p)|

\displaystyle\leqslant C(1/t+\lambda^{2}t^{3}\|\hat{V}\|_{\ell^{1}}^{2})\|\widetilde{f}\|_{\ell^{\infty}}^{2}\int\sigma(\vec{p})\,|f(p)|\,|f(p_{2})|\ \mathrm{d}p_{2}\mathrm{d}p_{3}\mathrm{d}p_{4}\ .

Next, we consider the following upper bound
for the coefficients

\displaystyle\sigma(\vec{p})

\displaystyle\leqslant\delta(p+p_{2}-p_{3}-p_{4})|\hat{V}(p-p_{3})-\hat{V}(p-p_{4})|^{2}+2\delta(p-p_{2}-p_{3}+p_{4})|\hat{V}(p-p_{3})|^{2}

\displaystyle=\delta(p+p_{2}-p_{3}-p_{4})\Big(\hat{V}(p-p_{3})^{2}+\hat{V}(p-p_{4})^{2}-2\hat{V}(p-p_{3})\hat{V}(p-p_{4})\Big)

(10.11)

\displaystyle\quad+2\delta(p-p_{2}-p_{3}+p_{4})|\hat{V}(p-p_{3})|^{2}\ .

We insert the above inequality in the right hand side of
(
10.9
),
and use some elementary manipulations
to obtain the crude upper bound

(10.12)

\int\sigma(\vec{p})|f(p_{3})|\,|f(p_{4})|\,\mathrm{d}p_{2}\mathrm{d}p_{3}\mathrm{d}p_{4}\leqslant C\|\hat{V}\|_{\ell^{1}}\|\hat{V}\|_{\ell^{\infty}}\|f\|_{\ell^{\infty}}\|f\|_{\ell^{1}},

and the same bound holds for the right hand side of Eq. (
10.10
).
This finishes the proof after we collect all the estimates,
and collect the
\hat{V}
-dependent factors into a constant
C>0.

∎

Next, we analyze the operator
B_{t}
, defined in Def.
2
,
and its relation
to the time independent operator
\mathscr{B}
, defined in the same way but
with
\delta_{t}(E_{1}-E_{2}-E_{3}-E_{4})

being replaced by

(2/\pi)\delta_{\mathbb{Z}}(e_{1}-e_{2}-e_{3}-e_{4}).

While for the operator
Q_{t}
an upper bound can be given in terms of
the number of holes

n=|\Lambda|\int f(p)\mathrm{d}p
,
the operator
B_{t}
depends on the total number of fermions
N
.
Physically,
this is due to the fact that a hole can interact with any of the
N^{(d-1)/d}
virtual particle-hole pairs around the Fermi surface.

Lemma 10.3

(Analysis of
B_{t}
)
.

Assuming that
0<\lambda\|\hat{V}\|_{\ell_{1}}\leqslant\frac{1}{2}(\frac{2\pi}{L})^{2}
, there is
C=C(V)>0
such that for all

f\in\ell^{1}(\Lambda^{*})

and
t>0
there holds

(10.13)

\displaystyle\|B_{t}[f]-t\mathscr{B}[f]\|_{\ell^{\infty}}\ \leqslant\,C\,t\,\big(1/t^{2}+(\lambda t)^{2}\big)\bigg(\frac{N}{|\Lambda|}\bigg)^{\frac{d-1}{d}}\|\widetilde{f}\|_{\ell^{\infty}}\|f\|_{\ell^{\infty}}

Proof.

Recall that
B=B^{(H)}+B^{(P)}
is defined in Def.
2

in terms of the respective hole and particle interaction terms.
Let us look only at the
B^{(H)}
term, the second one being analogous.
We find in terms of
\mathscr{B}=\mathscr{B}^{(H)}+\mathscr{B}^{(P)}

that for

f\in\ell^{1}(\Lambda^{*})

(10.14)

\displaystyle B_{t}^{(H)}[f]-t\mathscr{B}^{(H)}[f]=L_{t}[f]

where we define the following reminder term

\displaystyle L_{t}[f](h)

\displaystyle=2\pi\int|\hat{V}(k)|^{2}\Big(\rho^{H}_{t}(h-k,k)f(h-k)\widetilde{f}(h)-\rho^{H}_{t}(h,k)f(h)\widetilde{f}(h+k)\Big)\mathrm{d}k.

Here, the new remainder coefficient
\rho_{t}(h,k)
are given by

(10.15)

\displaystyle\rho_{t}(h,k)

\displaystyle\equiv\chi(h)\chi(h+k)\int\chi(r)\chi^{\perp}(r+k)\Big(\delta_{t}(\widetilde{\Delta E})-\frac{2t}{\pi}\delta_{\mathbb{Z}}(\widetilde{\Delta e})\Big)\mathrm{d}r

where we denote
\widetilde{\Delta E}=E_{h}-E_{h+k}-E_{r}-E_{r+k}

and

\widetilde{\Delta e}=e_{h}-e_{h+k}-e_{r}-e_{r+k}.

Thus, it follows from Lemma
10.1

with
x=\widetilde{\Delta e}

and
|y|\leqslant\|\hat{V}\|_{\ell^{1}}

that there is
C>0
such that

\displaystyle\|B_{t}[f]-t\mathscr{B}[f]\|_{\ell^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leqslant C(1/t+t^{3}\lambda^{2}\|\hat{V}\|_{\ell^{1}}^{2})\|\widetilde{f}\|_{\ell^{\infty}}\|f\|_{\ell^{\infty}}\int|\hat{V}(k)|^{2}\chi(r)\chi^{\perp}(r+k)\mathrm{d}r\mathrm{d}k\

(10.16)

\displaystyle\leqslant C(1/t+t^{3}\lambda^{2}\|\hat{V}\|_{\ell^{1}}^{2})\|\widetilde{f}\|_{\ell^{\infty}}\|f\|_{\ell^{\infty}}\|\hat{V}\|_{\ell^{1}}^{2}N^{\frac{d-1}{d}}\ .

In the last line, we have used the geometric estimate

\int\chi(r)\chi^{\perp}(r+k)\mathrm{d}r\lesssim(N/|\Lambda|)^{\frac{d-1}{d}}
,
valid for
k\in\textnormal{supp}\hat{V}
.
This finishes the proof after we absorb
\hat{V}
into the constant
C>0.

∎

10.3.
Example of Initial Data

In the reminder of this section, we work in three spatial dimensions
d=3
.
The inequality contained in Theorem
2

becomes a meaningful approximation for
f_{t}

provided
f_{0}
is such that

(10.17)

\|\mathscr{Q}[f_{0}]\|_{\ell_{m}^{1*}}+\|\mathscr{B}[f_{0}]\|_{\ell_{m}^{1*}}\gg\|\textnormal{Rem}_{1}(T)\|_{\ell_{m}^{1*}}\ .

Clearly, we need a lower bound on
\hat{V}
.
For simplicity, we assume the following.
Recall
r>0
from Condition
4
.

Condition 3
.

\hat{V}(k)
is rotationally symmetric and
\hat{V}(0,0,|k|)>0

for all
|k|\leqslant r
.

In the rest of this section, we construct examples of initial data
f

for which the lower bound (
10.17
) holds true.
We recall here that we denote by
\mathcal{S}
the Fermi surface defined in (
2.16
),
in terms of the parameter
r>0
.

We consider initial data with delta support in the union of the sets,
with properties that we describe in Condition
4
below.

Definition 9
.

Let
n\geqslant 1
, and consider
sets
P=\{p_{k}\}_{k=1}^{n}\subset\mathcal{B}^{c}/\mathcal{S}
,
and
H=\{h_{\ell}\}_{\ell=1}^{n}\subset\mathcal{B}/\mathcal{S}
.
We define

(10.18)

f_{H,P}(p):=\sum_{q\in H\cup P}\delta(p-q)\ .

For simplicity, we shall simply write
f\equiv f_{H,P}
.
Note that one may easily construct an initial state
\nu:B(\mathscr{F})\rightarrow\mathbb{C}

with momentum distribution

f
by considering the pure state associated to the Slater determinant

(10.19)

\nu(\mathcal{O})\equiv\left\langle\Psi,\mathcal{O}\Psi\right\rangle_{\mathscr{F}}\quad\textnormal{with}\quad\Psi\equiv\frac{1}{|\Lambda|}\prod_{p\in H\cup P}a^{*}_{p}\ \Omega\ .

As we have already argued in Section
2
,
the state
\nu
verifies Condition
2
.
We will additionally assume the following support conditions

Condition 4
.

We assume that the sets
H
and
P
satisfy the following two conditions.

(1)

|x-y|>r
for all pairwise different
x,y\in H\cup P

(2)

There exists a constant
\varepsilon>0
such that the following holds:
for all
q\in H\cup P
there exists
i\in\{1,2,3\}

such that

(10.20)

\varepsilon\ p_{F}^{2}\ \leqslant\ |q_{i}|^{2}\ \leqslant\ (1-\varepsilon)\ p_{F}^{2}\ .

Estimates for
\mathscr{Q}[f]
.
The upper bound

\|\mathscr{Q}[f]\|_{\ell^{\infty}}\leqslant C\|\widetilde{f}\|_{\ell^{\infty}}^{2}\|f\|_{\ell^{1}}\|f\|_{\ell^{\infty}}

can be established in an analogous way as we did for Lemma
10.2
.
Consequently, one easily finds that for all
f
as in Definition
9
there holds

(10.21)

\|\mathscr{Q}[f]\|_{\ell^{\infty}}\leqslant Cn

for a constant
C>0
, independent of
n
.

Estimates for
\mathscr{B}[f]
.
Let us recall that in the present case, the
operator
\mathscr{B}[f]
has the following
decomposition into holes and particles

(10.22)

\displaystyle\mathscr{B}

\displaystyle=\mathscr{B}^{(H)}+\mathscr{B}^{(P)}

where
each of one of
the operators acts on
\ell^{1}(\Lambda^{*})
as follows

\displaystyle\mathscr{B}^{(H)}[f](h)

\displaystyle=\frac{2\pi}{\pi/2}\int|\hat{V}(k)|^{2}\Big(\alpha^{H}_{t}(h-k,k)f(h-k)\widetilde{f}(h)-\alpha^{H}_{t}(h,k)f(h)\widetilde{f}(h+k)\Big)\mathrm{d}k

\displaystyle\mathscr{B}^{(P)}[f](p)

\displaystyle=\frac{2\pi}{\pi/2}\int|\hat{V}(k)|^{2}\Big(\alpha^{P}_{t}(p+k,k)f(p+k)\widetilde{f}(p)-\alpha^{P}_{t}(p,k)f(p)\widetilde{f}(p-k)\Big)\mathrm{d}k

for
f\in\ell^{1}
and
p,h\in\Lambda^{*}
.
Here, the coefficients
\alpha^{H}
and
\alpha^{P}
are given as follows

(10.23)

\displaystyle\alpha^{H}(h,k)

\displaystyle\equiv\chi(h)\chi(h+k)\int\chi(r)\chi^{\perp}(r+k)\delta_{\mathbb{Z}}\big(r\cdot k-h\cdot k\big)\mathrm{d}r

(10.24)

\displaystyle\alpha^{P}(p,k)

\displaystyle\equiv\chi^{\perp}(p)\chi^{\perp}(p-k)\int\chi(r)\chi^{\perp}(r+k)\delta_{\mathbb{Z}}\big(r\cdot k-(p-k)\cdot k\big)\mathrm{d}r

for all
p,h,k\in\Lambda^{*}.

In particular, we have evaluated the free dispersion relation
\Delta e
in terms
of
p
,
h
and
k
.

Certainly, it is sufficient to analyze the counting function defined as

\displaystyle N(q,k)

\displaystyle\equiv\int\chi(r)\chi^{\perp}(r+k)\delta_{\mathbb{Z}}\big(r\cdot k-q\cdot k\big)\mathrm{d}r

(10.25)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{|\Lambda|}\Big|\Big\{r\in\big(\frac{2\pi}{L}\mathbb{Z}\big)^{3}:|r|\leqslant p_{F},\ |r+k|>p_{F},\ r\cdot k=q\cdot k\Big\}\Big|

for
q\in\big(\frac{2\pi}{L}\mathbb{Z}\big)^{3}
and
1\leqslant|k|\leqslant r
– where
r\sim|\textnormal{supp}\hat{V}|
.

Remark 10.1
.

Geometrically,
N(q,k)
counts the number of lattice points that lie in the intersection of
the
lune set

L(k)=\{r\in\big(\frac{2\pi}{L}\mathbb{Z}\big)^{3}:|r|\leqslant p_{F},\ |r+k|>p_{F}\}

and the plane
H(q,k):=\{r\in\big(\frac{2\pi}{L}\mathbb{Z}\big)^{3}:r\cdot k=q\cdot k\}
.
Notice that
L(k)\cap H(q,k)

is nonempty only if

|q\cdot k|\leqslant p_{F}|k|
.

Lemma 10.4

(Upper bound for
\mathscr{B}
)
.

There is a constant
C=C(\hat{V})
such that for all

f\in\ell^{\infty}(\Lambda^{*})

the following bound holds true

(10.26)

\|\mathscr{B}[f]\|_{\ell^{\infty}}\leqslant C(N/|\Lambda|)^{1/3}\|f\|_{\ell^{\infty}}\|1-f\|_{\ell^{\infty}}\ .

Proof.

Let us first given an upper bound for the counting function
N(q,k)
,
for
(q,k)
with
q\in\big(\frac{2\pi}{L}\mathbb{Z}\big)^{3}
and
1\leqslant|k|\leqslant r
.
Indeed, let us assume that
|q\cdot k|\leqslant p_{F}|k|
for otherwise
N(q,k)=0
.
Then, a standard integral estimate shows that for a constant
C>0

(10.27)

N(q,k)\leqslant C\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathds{1}(|x|\leqslant p_{F}+1)\mathds{1}(|x+k|\geqslant p_{F}-1)\mathds{1}(|x\cdot k-q\cdot k|\leqslant 1)\mathrm{d}x\ .

We now evaluate the last integral by changing variables so that
x\cdot k=x_{3}|k|
.
Indeed, denoting
\hat{k}=k/|k|
we find using cylindrical coordinates

\displaystyle N(q,k)

\displaystyle\leqslant C\int_{q\cdot\hat{k}-1/|k|}^{q\cdot\hat{k}+1/|k|}dx_{3}\int_{0}^{\infty}rdr\mathds{1}\Big((p_{F}-1)^{2}-(x_{3}+|k|)^{2}\leqslant r^{2}\leqslant(p_{F}+1)^{2}-x_{3}^{2}\Big)

(10.28)

\displaystyle\leqslant C(k)p_{F}\ ,

where we have evaluated the last integral and used the upper bound
|q\cdot k|\leqslant p_{F}|k|
.

Going back to the operator
\mathscr{B}[f]
, one may readily find that
for a constant
C>0
there holds

(10.29)

\|\mathscr{B}[f]\|_{\ell^{\infty}}\leqslant C\|f\|_{\ell^{\infty}}\|1-f\|_{\ell^{\infty}}\int|\hat{V}(k)|^{2}\sup_{q\in\Lambda^{*}}N(q,k)\mathrm{d}k\leqslant C\|\hat{V}\|_{\ell^{2}}^{2}\|f\|_{\ell^{\infty}}\|1-f\|_{\ell^{\infty}}\ p_{F}

where we have used the bound (
10.28
) for the counting function
in terms of
p_{F}
.
This finishes the proof after we use
p_{F}\sim(N/|\Lambda|)^{1/3}

∎

In order to give a lower bound for
\mathscr{B}[f]
,
we take
f
as in Definition
9

satisfying Condition
4
.
It turns out that one can easily calculate the leading order term of the asymptotics of
N(q,k)

provided
k
is chosen parallel to one of the
basis vectors, and
q
is large enough in this direction.
We do this in the following lemma.

Lemma 10.5

(Counting function)
.

Let
k=(0,0,\pm|k|)\in\textnormal{supp}\hat{V}
and let
q\in\Lambda^{*}

satisfy the lower bound
\pm q_{3}\geqslant Cp_{F}^{2/3}
.
Then, the following asymptotics holds true

(10.30)

N(q,k)=\frac{q\cdot k}{2\pi L}\Big(1+\mathcal{O}(p_{F}^{-1/3})\Big)\ ,\qquad p_{F}\rightarrow\infty\ .

The same result holds for
k=(\pm 1,0,0)|k|
and
k=(0,\pm 1,0)|k|

provided
\pm q_{1}\geqslant Cp_{F}^{2/3}
and
\pm q_{2}\geqslant Cp_{F}^{2/3}
.

Remark 10.2
.

Before we turn to the proof, let us note that for
k=(0,0,|k|)

one can explicitly calculate that

(10.31)

N(q,k)=|\Lambda|^{-1}\big|\big\{x\in(\frac{2\pi}{L}\mathbb{Z})^{2}:{p_{F}^{2}-(q_{3}+|k|)^{2}}<|x|^{2}\leqslant{p_{F}^{2}-q_{3}^{2}}\big\}\big|\ .

Note the area of the above annulus is
\pi(2q_{3}|k|+|k|^{2})>0.

Determining the leading order term
of the asymptotics of the counting function (
10.31
) with
L=2\pi

is a problem that has received attention in other fields;
see for instance
[
14
,
15
,
28
,
36
,
39
]
and the references therein.
The problem is more challenging than the usual Gauss circle problem because if
q_{3}
is small
(i.e. in the thin annulus situation),
the remainder term can overcome
the size of the area–this would be the case for holes
h\in H
of really small norm.
In our case, the lower bound
q_{3}\geqslant C\,p_{F}^{2/3}

(introduced in Condition
4
)
is sufficiently large and the problem is avoided.

Remark 10.3
.

In the proof of Lemma
10.5

we compute the asymptotics of
N(q,k)
as
p_{F}\rightarrow\infty

for particular values of
k\in(\frac{2\pi}{L}\mathbb{Z})^{3}
,
with remainder
o(p_{F}^{2/3})
.
These particular values are enough to establish the desired lower bounds on

\mathscr{B}[f]
,
for
f
verifying Condition
4
.
The asymptotics for arbitrary values of

k\in(\frac{2\pi}{L}\mathbb{Z})^{3}

has also been computed in the literature for
L=2\pi

with remainder
O\big(\log(p_{F})^{2/3}p_{F}^{2/3}\big)
.
See e.g.
[
27
, Eq. (B.85)]
.

Proof of Lemma
10.5
.

First, we recall some estimates from the Gauss circle problem.
Namely, let us denote by

n(r)\equiv|\{x\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}:|x|^{2}\leqslant r^{2}\}|

the area of the circle
\pi r^{2}
.
It is known that
the remainder
E(r)\equiv n(r)-\pi r^{2}

satisfies the following bound:
for all
\varepsilon>0
there exists
C_{\varepsilon}
and
r_{\varepsilon}>0
such that

(10.32)

|E(r)|\leqslant C_{\varepsilon}r^{\theta+\varepsilon}\ ,\qquad\forall r\geqslant r_{\varepsilon}.

Here,
\theta=262/416<2/3

is (to the authors best knowledge)
the current best power for the bound (
10.32
), and is due to Huxley

[
16
]
.

We now assume
k=(0,0,|k|)
.
Let us now use (
10.32
)
with
\theta+\varepsilon<2/3
.
Indeed, as
p_{F}\rightarrow\infty
the area of the annulus is the difference between the area of two concentric circles and one finds

\displaystyle N(q,k)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{L^{3}}\Big(n\Big(\big(\frac{L}{2\pi}\big)\sqrt{p_{F}^{2}-q_{3}^{2}}\Big)-n\Big(\big(\frac{L}{2\pi}\big)\sqrt{p_{F}^{2}-(q_{3}+|k|)^{2}}\Big)\Big)\ ,

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{L^{3}}\Big(\pi\big(\frac{L}{2\pi}\big)^{2}\,(2q_{3}|k|+|k|^{2})+E\Big(\big(\frac{L}{2\pi}\big)\sqrt{p_{F}^{2}-q_{3}^{2}}\Big)-E\Big(\big(\frac{L}{2\pi}\big)\sqrt{p_{F}^{2}-(q_{3}+|k|)^{2}}\Big)\Big)\ ,

(10.33)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2\pi L}q_{3}|k|\Big(1+o\bigg(\frac{1+|k|}{p_{F}^{1/3}}\bigg)\Big)\ .

Here, we have used the lower bound

\varepsilon p_{F}^{2}\leqslant p_{F}^{2}-q_{3}^{2}\leqslant p_{F}^{2}

and similarly for
p_{F}^{2}+(q_{3}+|k|)^{2}
;
see Condition
4
.
This finishes the proof in view of

q\cdot k=q_{3}|k|
.
∎

We are now ready to give a lower bound for the
\mathscr{B}
operator.
We do this by evaluating the function
\mathscr{B}[f]
over the points
q\in(\frac{2\pi}{L}\mathbb{Z})^{3}
where
f
has
non-trivial support.

Lemma 10.6

(Lower bound for
\mathscr{B}
)
.

Let
f
be as in Definition
9

satisfying Condition
4
.
Then, there exsists
C_{\Lambda}>0
such that for all
q\in H\cup P

there holds

(10.34)

|\mathscr{B}[f](q)|\geqslant C_{\Lambda}N^{1/3}

Remark 10.4
.

Since
H\cup P
does not intersect the Fermi surface, the above lemma combined with the previous
upper bound implies that

(10.35)

c_{\Lambda}N^{1/3}\leqslant\|\mathscr{B}[f]\|_{\ell^{\infty}(\Lambda^{*}/\mathcal{S})}\leqslant C_{\Lambda}N^{1/3}

for constants
c_{\Lambda},C_{\Lambda}
depending on the volume
|\Lambda|=L^{d}
, and
N\geqslant 1
large enough.

Proof.

We prove the lemma only for
q=p\in P
since the proof for
q=h\in H
is analogous.
To this end, we notice that thanks to Condition
4
,
it holds that
f(p)=1
and
f(p+k)=0
for all
k\in\textnormal{supp}\hat{V}
.
Consequently, the “gain term” vanishes and one is left with
a simplified loss term. Namely, there holds

(10.36)

\mathscr{B}^{(P)}[f](p)=-4\int|\hat{V}(k)|^{2}N(p-k,k)\mathrm{d}k

Let
i\in\{1,2,3\}
be the index for which the lower bound holds true

|p_{i}|\geqslant\varepsilon p_{F}
.
Assume without loss of generality that
i=3
.
Thanks to our assumption on
\hat{V}
given by Condition
3
,
there exists
k_{*}=(0,0,1)|k|
with
\hat{V}(k_{*})>0
.
Then,
|p_{3}-|k||\geqslant Cp_{F}

and we may use Lemma
10.5

with
q=p-k_{*}
.
Hence, we find that for some
c_{\Lambda}>0

(10.37)

|\mathscr{B}^{(P)}[f](p)|\ \geqslant\ C|\hat{V}(k_{*})|^{2}N(p-k_{*})\ \geqslant\ C|\hat{V}(k_{*})|^{2}\big(p_{3}-|k|\big)\ \geqslant\ c_{\Lambda}N^{1/3}

This finishes the proof.
∎

11.
Proof of Theorem
3

The main purpose of this section
is the comparison of the operators
\mathscr{C}

and
\mathscr{B}
, acting on
\ell^{1}(\Lambda^{*})
.
In what follows, in order to shorten some lengthy expressions, we will use the following notation
for the three-fold lattice sum

(11.1)

\int\equiv\int\mathrm{d}p^{\prime}\mathrm{d}p_{*}\mathrm{d}p_{*}^{\prime}

which are summing over the pre-collisional variable
p_{*}\in\Lambda^{*}
,
and the post-collisional variables
p^{\prime},p^{\prime}_{*}\in\Lambda^{*}
.
We also introduce the measure corresponding to the kernel

(11.2)

\mathrm{d}\sigma\equiv b(pp_{*}p^{\prime}p^{\prime}_{*})\,\mathrm{d}p_{*}\mathrm{d}p^{\prime}\mathrm{d}p_{*}^{\prime}

where the function
b
was defined in (
1.12
).
We also recall the following notations

(11.3)

F=F(p)\qquad F_{*}=F(p_{*})\qquad F^{\prime}=F(p^{\prime})\qquad F^{\prime}_{*}=F(p^{\prime}_{*})

which will be extensively used throughout this section.

Let us recall that, with these notations, the operator
\mathscr{C}
acts on
\ell^{1}(\Lambda^{*})
as follows

(11.4)

\displaystyle\mathscr{C}[F]\equiv\int\Big(F^{\prime}F^{\prime}_{*}(1-F)(1-F_{*})-FF_{*}(1-F^{\prime})(1-F_{*}^{\prime})\Big)\mathrm{d}\sigma\ .

Similarly, the operator
\mathscr{B}=\mathscr{B}^{(P)}+\mathscr{B}^{(H)}

acts on
\ell^{1}(\Lambda^{*})

and was defined as the limits of the operator
\frac{1}{t}B_{t}
given in Definition
2
.
The effect of taking the limit is that we replace the approximate delta function
\delta_{t}(\Delta E)
with
\frac{1}{\pi/2}\delta_{\mathbb{Z}}(\Delta E)
.
Here, we write them as
after a change of variables:

\displaystyle\mathscr{B}^{(P)}[f]

\displaystyle=(1-\chi)\int(1-\chi^{\prime})\Big(\chi_{*}(1-\chi_{*}^{\prime})f^{\prime}(1-f)-\chi_{*}^{\prime}(1-\chi_{*})f(1-f^{\prime})\Big)\mathrm{d}\sigma_{P}

(11.5)

\displaystyle\mathscr{B}^{(H)}[f]

\displaystyle=\chi\int\chi^{\prime}\Big(\chi_{*}(1-\chi_{*}^{\prime})f^{\prime}(1-f)-\chi_{*}^{\prime}(1-\chi_{*})f(1-f^{\prime})\Big)\mathrm{d}\sigma_{H}\ .

The measures
are written as

(11.6)

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}\sigma_{P}

\displaystyle=\frac{2\pi}{(\pi/2)}\,\delta(p+p_{*}-p^{\prime}-p^{\prime}_{*})\delta_{\mathbb{Z}}(p^{2}+p_{*}^{2}-(p^{\prime})^{2}-(p_{*})^{\prime}|^{2})|\widehat{V}(p-p^{\prime})|^{2}

(11.7)

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}\sigma_{H}

\displaystyle=\frac{2\pi}{(\pi/2)}\,\delta(p+p_{*}-p^{\prime}-p^{\prime}_{*})\delta_{\mathbb{Z}}(p^{2}+p_{*}^{2}-(p^{\prime})^{2}-(p_{*})^{\prime}|^{2})|\widehat{V}(p-p^{\prime})|^{2}

11.1.
Statements

The main result of this section is the comparison between the operators
\mathscr{C}[F]

and
\mathscr{B}[f]

after one changes variables from the original picture,
to the particle-hole picture.
We assume that the perturbation
f
does not have support around the surface
\mathcal{S}
, in the sense of Condition
2
.
More precisely.

Proposition 11.1
.

Let
\mathscr{C}
and
\mathscr{B}
be the operators described by (
11.4
) and (
11.5
).
Let
F,f\in\ell^{1}(\Lambda^{*})
satisfy
0\leqslant F,f\leqslant 1
and assume that they are related through

(11.8)

F=(1-\chi)f+\chi(1-f)\ .

Furthermore, let
\hat{V}
verify Condition
1

and assume that
f|_{\mathcal{S}}\equiv 0
, where
\mathcal{S}
is the Fermi surface given by (
2.16
).
Then, it holds that for all
p\in\mathcal{S}

(11.9)

\mathscr{C}[F]=(1-\chi)\mathscr{B}[f]-\chi\mathscr{B}[f]+R[f]

where
R[f]
is a remainder that verifies the estimate

(11.10)

\|R[f]\|_{\ell^{\infty}(\Lambda^{*}\backslash\mathcal{S})}\leqslant C\|\widehat{V}\|_{\ell^{2}}^{2}\|f\|_{\ell^{1}(\Lambda^{*})}\ .

Proof.

We decompose the operator
\mathscr{C}
into its particle, and hole contributions.
We then decompose it further
into the gain and collision terms. More precisely, we write

(11.11)

\mathscr{C}[F]=\chi\mathscr{C}^{+}[F]-\chi\mathscr{C}^{-}[F]+(1-\chi)\mathscr{C}^{+}[F]-(1-\chi)\mathscr{C}^{-}[F]

and analyze each term separately.

Observation 1
.
Let us recall that the measure
\mathrm{d}\sigma=b(pp_{*}p^{\prime}p_{*}^{\prime})\mathrm{d}p_{*}\mathrm{d}p^{\prime}\mathrm{d}p_{*}^{\prime}

is defined in (
1.12
) in terms of the symmetrized matrix elements

(11.12)

|\widehat{V}(p-p^{\prime})-\widehat{V}(p-p_{*}^{\prime})|^{2}=|\widehat{V}(p-p^{\prime})|^{2}+|\widehat{V}(p-p_{*}^{\prime})|^{2}-2\widehat{V}(p-p^{\prime})\widehat{V}(p-p_{*}^{\prime})\ .

Observe that inside the integral we can change variables and obtain

|\widehat{V}(p-p_{*}^{\prime})|^{2}\mapsto|\widehat{V}(p-p^{\prime})|^{2}
for the second term.
On the other hand, observe that for fixed
p\in\mathcal{S}
the product

\widehat{V}(p-p^{\prime})\widehat{V}(p-p_{*}^{\prime})
forces
all
momenta to lie close to each other, within a radius
O(1)

within the Fermi surface. Since
f{|_{\mathcal{S}}}=0
such term drops out.
Thus, without loss of generality we assume throughout the proof
that the measure is determined by
2|\hat{V}(p-p^{\prime})|^{2}

rather than (
11.12
).
This factor
2
enters the
\mathscr{B}
operators in the kernels (
11.6
).

Observation 2
. Let us make an observations that will facilitate the analysis of each term.
Recall that we regard
p
and
p^{\prime}
as pre and post-collisional momenta (resp.
p_{*}
and
p_{*}^{\prime}
)
and that the measure
\mathrm{d}\sigma
is proportional to
|\widehat{V}(p-p^{\prime})|^{2}

(resp.
|\widehat{V}(p_{*}-p^{\prime}_{*})|^{2}
thanks to momentum conservation).
Recall also that the map
k\mapsto\hat{V}(k)
is supported in a ball of radius
r>0
,
and the Fermi surface
\mathcal{S}
is defined as a neighborhood of the Fermi surface of order
3r
.
Therefore, under the integral sign
\int\mathrm{d}\sigma
we have that
the product of
\chi
functions induce the following restrictions.

(11.13)

\displaystyle\chi(1-\chi^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\chi(1-\chi^{\prime})(\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{S}}\times\mathds{1}_{\mathcal{S}})(p,p^{\prime})

and similarly for
\chi_{*}(1-\chi^{\prime}_{*})
.
Since
f{|_{\mathcal{S}}}=0
we then find that several combinations simplify. For instance:

(11.14)

\chi(1-\chi^{\prime})f=0\qquad\textnormal{and}\qquad\chi(1-\chi^{\prime})(1-f)=\chi(1-\chi^{\prime})\ .

The first term of (
11.11
)
.
Thanks to (
11.8
) we have
\chi(1-F)=\chi f
. Thus,

(11.15)

\chi\mathscr{C}^{+}[F]=\int\chi fF^{\prime}\,F^{\prime}_{*}(1-F_{*})\mathrm{d}\sigma

Next,
we can compute
thanks to (
11.8
) for
F^{\prime}
and the observation (
11.13
)

(11.16)

\chi fF^{\prime}\ =\ \chi^{\prime}\chi f(1-f^{\prime})\ +\ (1-\chi^{\prime})\chi ff^{\prime}\ =\ \chi^{\prime}\chi f(1-f^{\prime})

Thereby

(11.17)

\chi\mathscr{C}^{+}[F]=\int\chi\chi^{\prime}f(1-f^{\prime})F^{\prime}_{*}(1-F_{*})\mathrm{d}\sigma\ .

Next, we compute using (
11.8
) the four contributions
of
F_{*}(1-F_{*})

which then subsequently simplify thanks to a variation of (
11.14
):

(11.18)

\displaystyle F_{*}^{\prime}(1-F_{*})=\chi_{*}^{\prime}\chi_{*}(1-f_{*}^{\prime})f_{*}+\chi_{*}^{\prime}(1-\chi_{*})+(1-\chi_{*}^{\prime})(1-\chi_{*})f_{*}^{\prime}(1-f_{*})

We conclude that by putting (
11.17
) and (
11.18
) together that

\displaystyle\chi\mathscr{C}^{+}[F]

\displaystyle=\int\chi\chi^{\prime}\chi_{*}^{\prime}(1-\chi_{*})f(1-f^{\prime})\mathrm{d}\sigma

\displaystyle+\int\chi\chi^{\prime}\chi_{*}^{\prime}\chi_{*}f(1-f^{\prime})(1-f_{*}^{\prime})f_{*}\mathrm{d}\sigma

(11.19)

\displaystyle+\int\chi\chi^{\prime}(1-\chi_{*}^{\prime})(1-\chi_{*})f(1-f^{\prime})f_{*}^{\prime}(1-f_{*})\mathrm{d}\sigma\ .

Let us now show that the second and third terms can be bounded in the
\ell^{\infty}
norm by
\|f\|_{\ell^{1}}
.
To see this,
we write
\mathrm{d}\sigma\leqslant C|V(p^{\prime}-p)|^{2}\delta(p+p_{*}-p^{\prime}-p^{\prime}_{*})\mathrm{d}p_{*}\mathrm{d}p^{\prime}\mathrm{d}p_{*}^{\prime}

and
use the uniform bounds
