0\leqslant\chi_{*}^{\prime},\chi_{*},f^{\prime}\leqslant 1
to find

\displaystyle\Big|\int\chi\chi^{\prime}f(1-f^{\prime})\chi_{*}^{\prime}\chi_{*}(1-f_{*}^{\prime})f_{*}\mathrm{d}\sigma\Big|

\displaystyle\ \leqslant\ C\int f_{*}|V(p^{\prime}-p)|^{2}\delta(p^{\prime}_{*}+p^{\prime}-p-p_{*})dp_{*}dp^{\prime}dp_{*}^{\prime}

(11.20)

\displaystyle\ \leqslant\ C\|f\|_{\ell^{1}}\|\widehat{V}\|_{\ell^{2}}^{2}\ .

For the third term, the analogous bound yields

\displaystyle\Big|\int\chi\chi^{\prime}f(1-f^{\prime})(1-\chi_{*}^{\prime})(1-\chi_{*})f_{*}^{\prime}(1-f_{*})\mathrm{d}\sigma\Big|

\displaystyle\leqslant C\|f\|_{\ell^{1}}\|\widehat{V}\|_{\ell^{2}}^{2}\ .

We conclude that

(11.21)

\chi\mathscr{C}^{+}[F]=\int\chi\chi^{\prime}\chi_{*}^{\prime}(1-\chi_{*})f(1-f^{\prime})\mathrm{d}\sigma+R_{1}[f]

where
R_{1}[f]
satisfies the claimed
\ell^{1}
estimate.

The second term of (
11.11
)
.
We use (
11.8
) for
1-F^{\prime}
and then
a variation of (
11.14
) to find

(11.22)

\displaystyle\chi\mathscr{C}^{-}[F]

\displaystyle=\int\chi\chi^{\prime}(1-f)f^{\prime}F_{*}(1-F_{*}^{\prime})\mathrm{d}\sigma+\int\chi(1-\chi^{\prime})F_{*}(1-F_{*}^{\prime})\mathrm{d}\sigma

Next, we expand
F_{*}(1-F_{*}^{\prime})
with (
11.8
) and then use a variation of (
11.14
) to find

(11.23)

\displaystyle F_{*}(1-F_{*}^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\chi_{*}\chi_{*}^{\prime}(1-f_{*})f_{*}^{\prime}+\chi_{*}(1-\chi_{*}^{\prime})+(1-\chi_{*})(1-\chi_{*}^{\prime})f_{*}(1-f_{*}^{\prime})\ .

The combination of the last two identities
gives a total combination of six terms.
Three of them contain one (and only one)
of the combinations

\chi(1-\chi^{\prime})
,

\chi^{\prime}(1-\chi)
,

\chi_{*}(1-\chi_{*}^{\prime})

and

\chi_{*}^{\prime}(1-\chi_{*})
.
These will be the leading terms. We thus expand the operator and write

\displaystyle\chi\mathscr{C}^{-}[F]

\displaystyle=\int\chi\chi^{\prime}\chi_{*}(1-\chi_{*}^{\prime})(1-f)f^{\prime}+\chi(1-\chi^{\prime})\bigg[\chi_{*}\chi_{*}^{\prime}(1-f_{*})f_{*}^{\prime}+(1-\chi_{*})(1-\chi_{*}^{\prime})f_{*}(1-f_{*}^{\prime})\bigg]

\displaystyle+\int\chi\chi^{\prime}\chi_{*}\chi_{*}^{\prime}(1-f)f^{\prime}(1-f_{*})f_{*}^{\prime}+\int\chi\chi^{\prime}(1-\chi_{*})(1-\chi_{*}^{\prime})(1-f)f^{\prime}f_{*}(1-f_{*}^{\prime})

(11.24)

\displaystyle+\int\chi(1-\chi^{\prime})\chi_{*}(1-\chi_{*}^{\prime})

The two terms in the second line can be combined into a single operator
R_{2}[f]
, which is easily estimated in
\ell^{\infty}
norm
as in
(
11.20
).

The third term of (
11.11
)
.
This term can be computed analogously as we did with the
\chi\mathscr{C}^{-}[F]
term.
Namely, we use the relations (
11.8
)
to expand various
F
factors and then use the observation (
11.14
)
to simplify them.
A straightforward although long computation shows

\displaystyle(1-\chi)\mathscr{C}^{+}[F]

\displaystyle=\int(1-\chi)(1-\chi^{\prime})\chi_{*}^{\prime}(1-\chi_{*})f^{\prime}(1-f)

\displaystyle\quad+\int\chi^{\prime}(1-\chi)\chi_{*}^{\prime}\chi_{*}f_{*}(1-f_{*}^{\prime})

\displaystyle\quad+\int\chi^{\prime}(1-\chi)(1-\chi_{*}^{\prime})(1-\chi_{*})f_{*}^{\prime}(1-f_{*})

\displaystyle\quad+\int(1-\chi)(1-\chi^{\prime})\chi_{*}^{\prime}\chi_{*}f^{\prime}(1-f)f_{*}(1-f_{*}^{\prime})

\displaystyle\quad+\int(1-\chi)(1-\chi^{\prime})(1-\chi_{*}^{\prime})(1-\chi_{*})f^{\prime}(1-f)f_{*}^{\prime}(1-f_{*})

(11.25)

\displaystyle\quad+\int\chi^{\prime}(1-\chi)\chi_{*}^{\prime}(1-\chi_{*})\ .

The first three terms are the corresponding leading order terms, as they contain
one (and only one)
combination of

\chi(1-\chi^{\prime})
,

\chi^{\prime}(1-\chi)
,

\chi_{*}(1-\chi_{*}^{\prime})

and

\chi_{*}^{\prime}(1-\chi_{*})
.
The fourth and fifth term
can be estimated similarly as in (
11.20
)
and will be denoted by
R_{3}[f]
.

The fourth term of (
11.11
)
. We proceed analogously as
we did for the term
\chi\mathscr{C}^{+}[F]
.
We use (
11.8
) for
1-F^{\prime}
and then
a variation of (
11.14
) to find

\displaystyle(1-\chi)\mathscr{C}^{-}[F]

\displaystyle=\int(1-\chi)(1-\chi^{\prime})\chi_{*}(1-\chi_{*}^{\prime})f(1-f^{\prime})

\displaystyle\qquad+\int(1-\chi)(1-\chi^{\prime})(1-\chi_{*})(1-\chi_{*}^{\prime})f(1-f^{\prime})f_{*}(1-f_{*}^{\prime})

(11.26)

\displaystyle\qquad+\int(1-\chi)(1-\chi^{\prime})\chi_{*}\chi_{*}^{\prime}f(1-f^{\prime})f_{*}^{\prime}(1-f_{*})

The first term is leading, and the second and third terms are remainder terms, denoted by
R_{4}[f]
,
which satisfy the estimate (
11.20
).

Conclusion
.
Next, we put (
11.11
), (
11.1
), (
11.24
), (
11.1
) and (
11.1
) together.
To this end, we denote
R[f]\equiv\sum_{i}R_{i}[f]
the sum
of the remainder terms that satisfy (
11.20
). Further, we use the fact
that the Fermi ball is a stationary solution of the quantum Boltzmann equation, i.e.

(11.27)

\mathscr{C}[\chi]=0\ .

We thus find that for all
p\in\mathcal{S}

\displaystyle\mathscr{C}[F]=

\displaystyle\int\chi\chi^{\prime}\chi_{*}^{\prime}(1-\chi_{*})f(1-f^{\prime})\mathrm{d}\sigma

\displaystyle-\int\chi\chi^{\prime}\chi_{*}(1-\chi_{*}^{\prime})(1-f)f^{\prime}

\displaystyle-\int\chi(1-\chi^{\prime})\chi_{*}\chi_{*}^{\prime}(1-f_{*})f^{\prime}_{*}

\displaystyle-\int\chi(1-\chi^{\prime})(1-\chi_{*})(1-\chi_{*}^{\prime})f_{*}(1-f^{\prime}_{*})

\displaystyle+\int(1-\chi)(1-\chi^{\prime})\chi_{*}^{\prime}(1-\chi_{*})f^{\prime}(1-f)

\displaystyle+\int\chi^{\prime}(1-\chi)\chi_{*}^{\prime}\chi_{*}f_{*}(1-f_{*}^{\prime})

\displaystyle+\int\chi^{\prime}(1-\chi)(1-\chi_{*}^{\prime})(1-\chi_{*})f_{*}^{\prime}(1-f_{*})

(11.28)

\displaystyle-\int(1-\chi)(1-\chi^{\prime})\chi_{*}(1-\chi_{*}^{\prime})f(1-f^{\prime})+R[f]\ .

Finally, we find that when restricting to
p\in\mathcal{S}
the factor
\chi(1-\chi^{\prime})
vanishes, thanks to the measure
\mathrm{d}\sigma
and (
11.14
).
Thus, for all such momenta,
one finds after re-arranging terms

\displaystyle\mathscr{C}[F]

\displaystyle=(1-\chi)\int(1-\chi^{\prime})\big[\chi_{*}^{\prime}(1-\chi_{*})f^{\prime}(1-f)-\chi_{*}(1-\chi_{*}^{\prime})f(1-f^{\prime})\big]\mathrm{d}\sigma

(11.29)

\displaystyle\qquad\ \ -\chi\int\chi^{\prime}\big[\chi_{*}(1-\chi_{*}^{\prime})f^{\prime}(1-f)-\chi_{*}^{\prime}(1-\chi_{*})f(1-f^{\prime})\big]\mathrm{d}\sigma+R[f]\ .

The right hand side can be identified with the
\mathscr{B}
operators given in (
11.5
).
This finishes the proof.
∎
